# Automatic FIR hyperparameter optimization and qualification

## Problem
Discussion began with qualification-result presentation and then phase-aware evaluation. The latest user correction removes phase Loss and focuses on automatic Ridge alpha, History and Robust K optimization; prior phase decisions are retained below as superseded history. A table-only change is not selected.
Evidence from the current screenshot and code: independent header and row layouts misalign columns; Coverage Gap does not expose missing required activity evidence; Put false zero may mean absent Put evidence; worst latency includes only detected collisions; valid cycle count measures supplied evidence rather than passing cycles. Visible rows also have false triggers and missed collisions. Presentation changes alone cannot improve detector accuracy.

## Solution

### Confirmed operative contract

This section and its resolution index state the current contract. The numbered Requirements, Decisions and Acceptance Criteria preserve the sequence of accepted refinements for traceability; they are not simultaneous alternatives. Earlier statements that a choice is pending describe its historical state only when a later controlling requirement below resolves it. Superseded requirements do not authorize their former behavior. Historical acceptance cases are checked for replacement consistency, not implemented as obsolete functionality. No user decision remains open; implementation and runtime verification have not occurred for this redesign.

1. **Start Learning settings and admission.** Capture target numeric type, Use Robust refit (default off), Include Training MSE (default on), N (integer 2-10, default 3), convergence tolerance (0.001-100 percent, at most three decimals, default 0.1 percent), and Validation allowance p (0-100 percent, at most three decimals, default 20 percent). Lock the new settings through all run stages, fallback and cleanup. Preflight every active condition before fitting workers: each recording has at least two valid complete Cycles; each active Speed x Offset has at least N in total; actual constrained fold allocation must be feasible. Report all count/allocation shortages and stop Learning without override, omission, lower N or scientific fallback.
2. **Fold plan.** Whole Cycle identity is the assignment unit. For every condition, balance held-out Cycle counts across N folds using floor(n/N) or ceil(n/N), with at least one Validation Cycle per condition/fold and at least one Training Cycle from every recording in every fold. Each Cycle is held out exactly once. Do not impose whole-recording priority or divide Cycles/samples. Each Start Learning captures a fresh automatic seed, each fallback round derives a new reproducible plan, and all candidates within that round share it. Coincident legal draws are allowed. Record actual Cycle-to-fold maps and provenance; no same-round redraw to improve metrics or evidence.
3. **Fitting and evaluation evidence.** Fit on eligible Cycle Normal/Pick/Put rows, excluding Collision/Ignore/outside-Cycle evidence and preserving existing finite, contiguous FIR support. Per fold, build separate fixed Training and Validation evaluation target masks supported by the maximum realizable 128 ms History; every parameter candidate uses those same respective target indices. Candidate fitting uses its own History-eligible rows, not the common evaluation intersection. Robust-rejected otherwise eligible rows still count in evaluation. Empty common targets in any required fold/condition/subset after feasible Cycle allocation skip that round with its reason and invoke existing fallback, without lower History or same-round resampling.
4. **Fitting modes and numeric identity.** Robust off uses one plain Ridge fit with no residual rejection, affected-history rejection, five-percent cap or robust second fit. Robust on preserves the existing initial Ridge, residual rejection/affected-row logic, five-percent cap and one robust refit. The mode is chosen by the user, not searched or changed automatically. Fit in float64, convert final coefficients/biases to the selected target type before evaluation, and use target Replay prediction/subtraction semantics. Convert residuals to float64 before squares/aggregation. Calibrate axis residual centers/STD from final target-model residuals on final fitting rows only (all eligible rows off; retained refit rows on), never Validation or per-recording Replay data. Keep target-representable calibration with the same kernel. Preserve invalid-scale checks; no epsilon repair. Detector normalization is STD and normalized XYZ combination is L2_NORM.
5. **MSE and parameter Loss.** Each per-condition Training/Validation MSE is squared XYZ residual sum divided by the corresponding number of XYZ observations, pooling eligible samples within that fold/condition. Let T_fc and V_fc be that fold kernel's separate raw errors. With Training inclusion on, L_fc=0.5*T_fc+0.5*V_fc; off, L_fc=V_fc. First average L_fc equally across all N folds for each condition, then take the maximum condition mean: J_params=max_c(mean_f(L_fc)). Lower is better. No sample weighting between folds, condition pooling, phase Loss, pooled-MSE secondary elimination or absolute Training-error cutoff. Keep Training, Validation and mixed metrics distinguishable.
6. **Search domain and coarse seeds.** History is 0-128 ms, realized by taps=floor(History_ms*sample_rate_hz/1000)+1; zero means one current-sample tap. Alpha is [1e-6,100]. Enabled K is integer [2,32]. Coarse requested histories are {0,4,8,16,32,64,128} ms and alphas {1e-6,1e-4,1e-2,1,100}; enabled K adds {2,4,8,16,24,32}. This is 35 off-mode pairs or 210 on-mode triples before effective tap-alias deduplication, not a fold-fit count. Retain the raw-Loss best two coarse candidates and all raw ties with second place as independent fine starts.
7. **Fine search and bounds.** Evaluate the joint lower/current/upper neighborhood, excluding the center: up to eight proposals with Robust off, or 26 on. Start History step at max(1,floor(0.032*sample_rate_hz)) taps, alpha ratio 10, enabled K step 4. A strict raw improvement moves the center at unchanged steps. A full neighborhood with no improvement floor-halves tap/K steps to at least one and reduces alpha ratio to max(1.05,sqrt(r)). Before evaluation, project each out-of-bounds coordinate to its nearest allowed endpoint, using realizable integer tap bounds, then deduplicate. Boundary arrival does not itself mean convergence.
8. **Convergence and reuse.** Final steps are one tap, alpha multiply/divide 1.05, and enabled K step one. Only after a complete planned final-step sweep compare relative improvement with the captured tolerance; strictly less is insufficient. Keep every best evaluated improvement even if sub-tolerance. Exact zero uses the normal full-sweep/minimum-step process without division by zero. Every distinct equally best strictly improving continuation remains an independent branch when continuation is required; unchanged-Loss plateaus do not branch. All admitted starts/branches finish before selection. Effective identical candidates in the same complete round/evidence/numeric context share in-flight or completed evaluations; paths retain their own steps/state. No time-limit termination, tie-count cap or global-optimum claim is introduced.
9. **Worker scheduling.** One candidate parameter configuration by one fold is one search job fitting one joint active-condition model. Use one globally shared queue with at most eight concurrent search workers, not eight per fold/start/configuration. Coordinator owns plans, search state and aggregation. All N valid fold results are required before parameter ranking; completion order and partial averages cannot select moves. Qualification uses a separate global maximum of four complete-recording workers, without slicing a recording's stateful replay.
10. **Final parameter selection.** Consider all valid evaluated coarse/fine configurations in the round, including intermediate candidates and non-starting coarse candidates. Round only final J_params to four decimal places using decimal half-up and retain all distinct minimum-rounded configurations. Coarse ranking, search moves/convergence and Validation checks remain raw. Do not merge different parameters merely because their Loss or coefficients match. Do not compare Loss across different fallback scopes/plans.
11. **Validation and final fold kernels.** Each required condition of a fold kernel must satisfy raw V_fc <= T_fc*(1+p/100); equality passes, T=0 allows only V=0, with no epsilon or rounding. Finite gate-failing folds remain in full N-fold parameter Loss, but cannot qualify. After best parameter selection, filter failed fold kernels and, independently per retained configuration, rank passing kernels by their own worst-condition Loss J_kernel(f)=max_c(L_fc). Round this scalar half-up to four decimals, take the lowest two and all ties with second place. If only one passes, use it; if zero, omit that group's kernels. If all retained best groups have zero survivors, mark the round Validation failed and follow existing fallback. No worse-parameter promotion, relaxed allowance, resampling, coefficient averaging or additional full-data final fit. The same original fold kernel/calibration proceeds to Score/Qualification.
12. **Qualification and retention.** Preserve all-supplied condition replay, Score-derived inclusive [N,C] threshold search, 150 ms collision windows minus Ignore without time extension/compression, annotation overlap rejection, startup zero-padding/Score inclusion and repaired-input rules. Here detector bound N is distinct from N-fold count. Threshold N is the minimum of Normal interval maximum scores; C is the minimum of Collision-window maxima. C<N skips that model. Existing positive/unique threshold and confirmation rules remain. Every selected fold kernel must pass its own existing Qualification checks; keep all selected tied kernels that qualify with their actual evidence and operating scope. No automatic export or promotion of diagnostic-only results.
13. **Fallback and failure classes.** Preserve existing nested offset/speed progression: an unsuccessful scope removes the lowest remaining Training speed while more than one remains; success or speed exhaustion then removes the smallest active offset and restores supplied speeds. Each new scope optimizes afresh and draws a new fold plan; Qualification still sees all supplied conditions. Keep prior qualified kernels only under existing provenance/staleness guards. Expected narrowly classified candidate numerical invalidity skips that parameter configuration (one invalid required fold cannot supply full CV Loss); all configurations invalid invokes scientific fallback. Invalid data/program errors, memory shortage, worker startup/unexpected exit and Cycle-count/allocation failures stop the entire run. No worker retries or automatic concurrency/History reduction. Confirmed memory shortage is reported as such; unknown worker failures retain actual errors rather than claiming a proven hardware cause. User cancellation remains distinct. Do not publish partial interrupted work as successful.
14. **Save/Open and provenance.** Persist next-run settings and separate original-result settings/seed/fold maps through explicit Save/Save As. Restore historical assignments for inspection, not reuse on next Start. Setting-only idle edits preserve otherwise-current qualified Replay/Export under original settings; never relabel, revive evidence-stale or failed results, or bypass active-run/new-run replacement guards. In otherwise supported projects, missing/invalid next-run fields restore their approved defaults with notice as specified: Robust off, convergence 0.1 percent, allowance 20 percent, N=3, Training inclusion on. Preserve valid nondefaults. Invalid N resets to 3, not a range endpoint; flags require valid booleans. No automatic disk overwrite or fabricated historical evidence; unsupported schema/unrelated corruption handling stays unchanged.

## User Stories
As a user, I want automatic selection of Ridge alpha, History and Robust K using prediction Loss before detector qualification, with fresh optimization after an existing Speed/Offset fallback; evaluation does not partition Cycles into phases.

## Requirements

Applicability: read these stable decision records through the Current resolution index. Superseded fragments and historically pending questions do not override its confirmed controlling rules.

| ID | Requirement |
| --- | --- |
| REQ-001 | Historical scope clarification, closed by the confirmed Solution. Clarify whether the requested improvement concerns table/status interpretation or actual detection performance before defining changes. |
| REQ-002 | Historical scope clarification, closed by the confirmed Solution. Investigate ways to reduce late-Cycle prediction error without confusing Replay error, held-out training error, or detector Score. Exact method and acceptable tradeoffs are unresolved. |
| REQ-003 | Superseded phase requirement; REQ-023 removes this feature. Evaluate candidates by Cycle phase on held-out evidence and include phase errors in model selection while retaining current FIR fitting and one fixed deployed model. Ranking details remain unresolved. |
| REQ-004 | Superseded phase requirement; REQ-023 removes this feature. Rank candidate models first by the largest phase MSE (smaller wins), then by overall MSE (smaller wins) when the first metric ties. Aggregation across conditions and exact equality handling remain to be settled. |
| REQ-005 | Superseded phase requirement; REQ-023 removes this feature. Expose evaluation segment count in Learning Settings so users choose how many Cycle phases candidate evaluation and ranking use. Default, allowed range and lifecycle semantics remain unconfirmed. |
| REQ-006 | Superseded phase requirement; REQ-023 removes this feature. The user-configurable integer evaluation segment count shall have maximum 10. Minimum and default remain unconfirmed. |
| REQ-007 | Superseded phase requirement; REQ-023 removes this feature. Divide each original Cycle duration into the selected number of equal-duration phases before filtering to existing Training-eligible data. Exclusions must not compress time or shift phase boundaries. Preserve fitting eligibility and continuous FIR-history semantics; phase partition is for evaluation only. |
| REQ-008 | Superseded phase requirement; REQ-023 removes this feature. Learning Settings evaluation segment count shall accept integers 1 through 10 inclusive, defaulting to 3. Count 1 evaluates each whole Cycle as one phase under the same eligibility rules. |
| REQ-009 | Superseded phase requirement; REQ-023 removes this feature. Remove Ignore portions from each Cycle before constructing evaluation segments. Partition the remaining ordered Cycle data, not the original unfiltered duration. Supersede the original-time-first partition in REQ-007. This segmentation must not rewrite original timestamps, splice FIR input history across an Ignore gap, or alter the existing collision window/qualification policy. Whether Collision is also removed before partition remains unresolved. |
| REQ-010 | Superseded phase requirement; REQ-023 removes this feature. Before evaluation segmentation, remove both annotated Ignore and annotated Robot Fork Collision portions from the Cycle. Preserve chronological order and source indices for remaining data. Do not expand this exclusion to the 150-ms detector response window or alter FIR fitting/history and qualification rules. |
| REQ-011 | Superseded phase requirement; REQ-023 removes this feature. Divide the remaining chronological samples into the selected count P using q=floor(L/P) and r=L mod P: first r groups contain q+1 samples, all later groups contain q. Every retained sample appears once and in order; source timestamps and FIR input/history remain unchanged. The exact FIR-support eligibility stage and insufficient L policy remain unresolved. |
| REQ-012 | Superseded phase requirement; REQ-023 removes this feature. For each candidate, take the largest phase MSE within each active Training Speed x Offset condition, then the maximum of those condition values as the primary ranking value. Smaller wins; overall MSE is the next key as already accepted. Only conditions participating in the current Training round contribute, not excluded or qualification-only conditions. Within-condition cross-Cycle pooling remains unresolved. |
| REQ-013 | Superseded phase requirement; REQ-023 removes this feature. Within each active Training Speed x Offset, pool corresponding phases across held-out Cycles using total squared residual over total XYZ observation count. Thus phase MSE=sum over Cycle/sample/axis of error squared divided by 3 times total valid sample count. XYZ are equally weighted; Cycle contributions are sample-count weighted, not an unweighted mean of Cycle MSEs or the worst individual Cycle. |
| REQ-014 | Superseded phase requirement; REQ-023 removes this feature. Persist and restore evaluation segment count with the project. Lock changes during the active Learning lifecycle, including qualification/fallback/cancellation. A post-run value change marks the project dirty and previous results stale; a new Learning run is required before results can be treated/exported as current for the new setting. Do not start retraining automatically. |
| REQ-015 | Superseded phase requirement; REQ-023 removes this feature. Provide user-visible segmentation-feasibility feedback identifying the Recording, Cycle, requested count, relevant FIR history/taps and available eligible sample count when a requested partition cannot be supported. Exact preflight timing and block-versus-warn behavior remain pending. |
| REQ-016 | Superseded phase requirement; REQ-023 removes this feature. On a segment-count change, immediately check and display segmentation feasibility in Learning Settings. Recheck against current validated data, annotations and the captured setting before Start Learning launches expensive work. Previously computed feedback must not be treated as valid after its input data/annotations change. Warning versus blocking enforcement remains pending. |
| REQ-017 | Superseded phase requirement; REQ-023 removes this feature. Supersede immediate-on-edit preflight in REQ-016: perform segmentation counting when Start Learning is clicked, show results in a confirmation prompt, and begin expensive Learning only after explicit confirmation. Count edits alone do not trigger segmentation checking. Cancellation does not launch training. Preserve the accepted persistence/staleness lifecycle. |
| REQ-018 | Superseded phase requirement; REQ-023 removes this feature. Partition feasibility is an evaluation-availability check, not an additional FIR training admission gate. Otherwise training-eligible data must continue to participate even when the requested partition cannot supply a phase Loss. Mark unavailable phase Loss explicitly and exclude only that unavailable phase contribution from phase-loss aggregation; do not manufacture zero error, reject a whole history/model/recording solely for segmentation, silently lower count, or delete data. Existing non-segmentation training validity checks still apply. Exact unavailable unit and all-unavailable ranking behavior remain pending. |
| REQ-019 | Historical optimizer direction, refined by REQ-024 and the Current resolution index. Support automatic additional training when an explicitly defined Loss criterion is not met, while retaining the single fixed deployed FIR and phase-loss selection objective. The criterion, retry parameter family/ranges, search policy, best-model retention, stopping/resource limits and missing-metric behavior require confirmation before implementation. This does not authorize automatic runs on setting edits. |
| REQ-020 | Historical optimizer direction, refined by REQ-024 and the Current resolution index. Automatic additional training shall search Ridge alpha and Robust K jointly, rather than alpha alone. The user asks about gradient-based search; gradient feasibility and the final search algorithm remain unresolved. Preserve the accepted held-out phase-loss ranking and one fixed deployed FIR. Parameter bounds, K domain, trigger and budget require confirmation. |
| REQ-021 | Historical optimizer direction, refined by REQ-024 and the Current resolution index. Extend the requested Loss optimization parameters to Ridge alpha, FIR History and Robust K. For each active Training scope, perform Loss-based parameter/model optimization before detector Score qualification; after an existing Speed/Offset fallback transition, reoptimize using the new scope before qualifying again. Preserve prior phase evaluation and qualification acceptance contracts. The user requests gradient-based optimization; direct-gradient feasibility, algorithm and strictness of that requirement remain unresolved. |
| REQ-022 | Historical optimizer direction, refined by REQ-024 and the Current resolution index. Automatically optimize Ridge alpha, History and Robust K without requiring every parameter to be updated by gradients. Preserve the Loss-before-detector-qualification and renewed-optimization-after-fallback ordering in REQ-021. The specific optimizer remains unselected. |
| REQ-023 | Active removal of segmentation; later CV objective, optional Robust and multi-kernel rules supersede older restatements in this row. Remove Cycle phase segmentation from the Loss objective and proposed UI/checks: no segment-count control, phase partition/preflight, or phase-unavailable metric policy is required by this change set. Pool eligible held-out squared XYZ residuals by active Training Speed x Offset into whole-condition MSE, retain the accepted maximum across conditions and overall pooled-MSE tie-breaker; smaller values are better for both ranking metrics. Preserve Cycle-grouped validation, existing fit eligibility, no history splicing, current robust refit, and a single deployed FIR; do not change qualification or event masking. This supersedes the phase-specific REQ-003 through REQ-018 while restating their applicable non-phase safeguards. |
| REQ-024 | Use bounded coarse-to-fine derivative-free optimization of Ridge alpha, History and Robust K: compare broad initial combinations, then evaluate additional values around better candidates using the accepted whole-condition held-out Loss. Exact domains, coarse seeds, retained neighborhoods, refinement spacing, budget and stopping policy remain to be specified. This does not guarantee a global optimum or permit changing fitting/qualification semantics. |
| REQ-025 | History optimization must be able to explore beyond the existing 4-32 ms envelope rather than treating that proposed envelope as a final restriction. Exact lower/upper limits, outside-domain exploration schedule, resource cap and termination policy require confirmation. Retain discrete sample-rate-realizable taps, held-out Loss comparison and the accepted coarse-to-fine method. |
| REQ-026 | The maximum permitted History in the automatic search shall be 128 ms. Convert admissible histories to integer taps using the sampling rate and do not evaluate effective histories beyond the approved cap. Lower bound, exact coarse/refinement candidates and resource/stop limits remain unconfirmed. |
| REQ-027 | The permitted History interval shall be 0-128 ms inclusive, realized as integer FIR tap counts at the recording sample rate. Zero History means one tap using the current Internal IMU sample, not zero taps or bypassing training. Preserve the 128 ms ceiling, current fitting and qualification rules. Exact coarse/refinement candidates and fair validation support remain to be specified. |
| REQ-028 | The Ridge alpha search domain shall be [1e-6, 100] inclusive, with coarse-to-fine candidate placement in a logarithmic coordinate. Permit positive intermediate alpha values rather than only the original five seeds. Do not search alpha zero or expand beyond these bounds implicitly. Exact seed/refinement schedule remains unconfirmed. |
| REQ-029 | Robust K optimization must be able to evaluate values outside the proposed 4-8 interval. Do not treat the old 4,6,8 seeds as a final search domain. The new lower/upper bounds, integer-versus-fractional representation and coarse/refinement schedule require explicit confirmation. Preserve current hard-rejection/refit semantics unless separately changed. |
| REQ-030 | Use the user-proposed Robust K interval [2,32] as the requested bounded search envelope, including both endpoints. This expands the old [4,8] envelope but is not evidence that it contains the global optimum. Integer-versus-fractional representation and candidate schedule remain unresolved. Existing validators restricted to 4,6,8 require approved changes before this domain is supported. |
| REQ-031 | Robust K shall remain integer-valued with admissible values 2 through 32 inclusive. A finite domain is independent of the candidate-selection algorithm: it does not require exhaustive evaluation of every K or every cross-parameter combination. Preserve the existing integer representation while updating approved validation rules for the wider domain. Exact coarse/refinement and stopping policy remain unresolved. |
| REQ-032 | Treat search as converged only when the agreed finest parameter steps have been reached and a complete planned refinement sweep yields no material improvement in the best held-out Loss. Reaching the finest steps alone, one unchanged candidate, a local zero difference, or a wall-clock duration is not sufficient evidence of convergence. Numerical alpha resolution, Loss tolerance and exact sweep coverage remain unconfirmed. This does not guarantee a global optimum. |
| REQ-033 | The finest regular alpha refinement step shall multiply or divide a candidate alpha by 1.05, equivalent to a symmetric log-space step ln(1.05), within the approved [1e-6,100] domain. Do not substitute the previously proposed 1.1 ratio. Exact boundary handling, deduplication and coarse-to-fine ratio schedule remain to be specified. |
| REQ-034 | The numerical tolerance for material improvement is relative to best held-out Loss: for positive previous best J_old, compute (J_old-J_new)/J_old after the complete planned final-resolution sweep. The initially accepted tolerance is 0.1percent (fraction 0.001); improvement strictly below it is not material. REQ-035 makes this configurable. Keep the best evaluated model even when its improvement is below tolerance. Exact-zero handling and numeric comparison details remain pending. |
| REQ-035 | Expose the relative Loss-improvement convergence tolerance as a user-configurable Learning Settings parameter, displayed in percent, with the previously accepted 0.1percent as its default. It must control the material-improvement check rather than remain a hard-coded constant. Clearly distinguish this from detector Score threshold and alpha multiplicative spacing. Input bounds/precision, persistence, run-time editing/capture and result-provenance behavior remain unresolved. |
| REQ-036 | Within a fixed Training scope and grouped validation plan, compare all History candidates on the same held-out source sample indices supported by the maximum permitted History under existing contiguous/finite FIR eligibility. Fix that evaluation mask before searching rather than changing it as candidates are visited. Each candidate still fits its own otherwise eligible training rows, followed by the existing robust refit; do not crop fitting evidence to the evaluation intersection. Preserve original timestamps, Replay startup zero padding/Score inclusion and Qualification. Insufficient common evidence remains unresolved. |
| REQ-037 | Capture the configured Loss-improvement tolerance at Start Learning and keep it fixed through the entire run, including qualification, fallback and cancellation in progress. Unlock only when work finishes or cancellation completes. Later edits configure the next run. Default remains 0.1 percent; persistence and stale-result behavior remain pending. |
| REQ-038 | Persist the configured relative Loss-improvement tolerance in the project and restore that value when reopening the project, rather than resetting a stored value to the default. Preserve the accepted whole-run capture/locking rule. Missing-field compatibility, invalid stored values, run provenance and stale-result behavior remain separate decisions. |
| REQ-039 | For an otherwise supported project that lacks the new Loss-improvement tolerance field, use the approved 0.1 percent default for its setting without rejecting Open Project solely for that absence. This does not add support for unsupported schema/contracts, silently repair a present invalid value, or claim old results were generated by the new optimizer or with that tolerance. |
| REQ-040 | A tolerance-only edit after completion preserves otherwise-current qualified results and their existing view/export eligibility, labeled with the original captured run tolerance. Apply the new setting only to the next Learning run. Never rewrite provenance, revive results stale for other reasons, or upgrade unqualified diagnostics. Unknown legacy run provenance remains a separate decision. |
| REQ-041 | In each active Training round admit only the model ranked best by the approved held-out Loss objective to detector Score/threshold Qualification. Do not test runner-up Loss models in that scope. If it cannot qualify, apply the existing fallback and optimize again on the resulting scope; preserve previously qualified results under existing rules. |
| REQ-042 | Preserve the approved History ceiling when a required fold/condition has no common held-out validation rows. Report the affected evidence scope and reason, skip optimization and Qualification for that round, and continue existing fallback while retaining earlier qualified results under existing rules. Do not automatically lower History, fabricate zero Loss, or silently omit a required fold/condition. Numerical failures remain distinct from evidence shortages. |
| REQ-043 | Retain the three distinct best-ranked coarse parameter combinations as separate starting points for fine search. Compare the resulting candidates using the accepted Loss objective and admit only one final Loss winner to Qualification. Three starting points do not imply three distinct basins, three deployed models or Qualification of runner-up models. Exact coarse points and fine-search mechanics remain to be specified. |
| REQ-044 | Start coarse search in each round from the Cartesian product of requested History ms {0,4,8,16,32,64,128}, Ridge alpha {1e-6,1e-4,1e-2,1,100}, and integer Robust K {2,4,8,16,24,32}: 210 requested triples before History sample-realization deduplication. This count excludes CV folds and scale/score variants. Subsequent fine search explores intermediate admissible values under the accepted bounds and resolution. |
| REQ-045 | Configure coarse search with a maximum of eight compute workers and fine search with four worker slots. Preserve the accepted three fine-search starting combinations and one final model admitted to Qualification. Worker counts are distinct from candidate/model counts and do not promise all slots remain busy. Qualification worker count, work granularity, resource admission and actual scheduling remain unresolved. |
| REQ-046 | Preserve three independent fine-search starts while dispatching their ready candidate-evaluation jobs through a shared queue to four compute workers. The coordinator owns search state and collects all results required for a refinement update before changing that search direction; completion order must not change the candidate sequence or ranking. Do not add a fourth start or allow independent worker mutations of optimizer state. |
| REQ-047 | Qualification uses at most four compute workers on complete independent Recordings for the single admitted final model and target numeric type. Aggregate active-round score evidence centrally to determine shared N/C and threshold/confirmation candidates before detector evaluation, reuse fixed-model recording scores, and combine recording metrics centrally. Preserve all-supplied-recording coverage and continuous within-recording FIR/detector semantics; no worker-local independent threshold selection or arbitrary time slicing. |
| REQ-048 | On detected insufficient hardware memory, report hardware insufficiency and terminate the entire active Learning run, including queued work and active compute workers. Do not automatically reduce worker concurrency, wait for memory as an adaptive recovery, retry an explicit memory failure, or treat it as scientific evidence shortage. Do not enter Speed/Offset fallback, reduce History bounds, omit candidates or publish a partial result as successful because of a resource failure. Preserve the hardware-failure reason distinctly from user cancellation. |
| REQ-049 | At fixed fine-search steps, each of the three starts evaluates the valid joint neighborhood from lower/current/upper alpha, History taps and integer K. Excluding the already evaluated center gives at most 26 neighbor triples before invalid or duplicate proposals are removed. Use the accepted four-worker queue and required-result barriers; this does not change parameter bounds, final alpha ratio, starting-point count or single-model Qualification. |
| REQ-050 | Initialize each fine-search start with History step max(1, floor(0.032 times sample_rate)) taps, integer K step 4 and alpha ratio 10. Before final resolution, a strictly lower primary Loss moves the center while retaining steps; a completed neighborhood with no lower primary Loss reduces tap and K steps by integer-floor halving with minimum one, and alpha ratio to max(1.05, sqrt(r)). Coordinates already at minimum remain there. Final steps are one tap, one K and alpha ratio 1.05; final convergence retains its separate complete-sweep improvement rule. |
| REQ-051 | Retain the four distinct best-ranked coarse parameter combinations as four fine-search starts. This explicitly replaces the three-start counts and prohibition on adding a fourth start in REQ-043, REQ-045, REQ-046 and REQ-049 while preserving their other safeguards. All four starts share the existing four fine-worker slots and coordinator-owned candidate queue; they are not permanently bound one-to-one to processes. Coarse grid, worker capacities and single-model Qualification are unchanged. |
| REQ-052 | Each of the four fine-search starts independently reaches the minimum steps, completes its required valid neighbor comparisons and applies the configured relative Loss-improvement convergence rule to its own best Loss. Finished starts stop proposing work while unfinished starts continue using shared workers. Final selection waits until all four successfully finish, then chooses the single best observed valid candidate for final refit and Qualification. A global-best plateau alone must not stop another unfinished start; resource failure or cancellation is not successful convergence. |
| REQ-053 | Fix residual scale method to STD and score mode to L2_NORM throughout the new coarse/fine optimizer and final selected model. STD uses the existing mean/standard-deviation residual normalization; L2_NORM uses the Euclidean magnitude of normalized XYZ residuals. Do not search MAD or MAX_ABS variants. Preserve robust K rejection/refit semantics, raw held-out MSE ranking, common validation rows, 210 requested coarse triples and single-model Qualification. Do not rewrite historical model settings. |
| REQ-054 | Retain all distinct kernels tied for the best selection Loss and send every retained tied kernel through the existing Score/bounds/Qualification pipeline instead of eliminating ties by History, alpha or K. This explicitly replaces the single-admitted-model restriction of REQ-041 and its restatements in later requirements; it does not admit strictly worse-Loss runner-ups. Preserve all-four fine-search completion, existing threshold/evidence guards and global worker capacities. Exact tie metric/equality, retained-candidate population and post-Qualification result retention remain to be clarified. |
| REQ-055 | Preserve the previously accepted primary-then-secondary Loss ordering for final admission: first retain kernels with minimum maximum-condition MSE; among those retain kernels with minimum pooled MSE. Retain every kernel tied on both metrics and send each through the existing Score/bounds/Qualification pipeline. Do not use History, alpha, K or worker completion order to eliminate these ties. Pooled MSE is minimized within the primary-best set, not as an independent union of global per-metric minima. Numeric equality policy and candidate-population scope remain separate. |
| REQ-056 | Use minimum maximum-condition MSE alone to identify the best-Loss kernels for retention and Qualification admission. Retain every distinct kernel tied at that minimum irrespective of pooled MSE, History, alpha or K. Pooled MSE may remain diagnostic but must not filter this tied set. This supersedes REQ-055 two-metric admission and overrides the pooled-MSE elimination component of REQ-023 for this selection. Preserve existing per-model Score/bounds/Qualification guards, worker capacities and hardware-failure cancellation. Numerical equality and candidate-population scope remain separate. |
| REQ-057 | For best-Loss tie retention, round each final primary maximum-condition MSE to four digits after the decimal point and retain all kernels in the minimum rounded-Loss group. Preserve the unrounded value for search-improvement and convergence calculations; do not round residuals or individual squared errors. Exact halfway rounding convention remains to be specified. The selected Loss data source is subject to the separate requested Training-Loss redesign. |
| REQ-058 | Redesign candidate selection around post-fit Loss computed on all data designated as Training for the active Speed/Offset scope, with approximately one quarter to one third reserved for assessing overfitting, rather than continuing to assume held-out CV Loss is the selection metric. Preserve primary maximum-condition MSE aggregation unless separately changed. Exact Training/holdout membership, split grouping and ratio, history-support comparison, overfit criterion/action and final-refit policy remain unresolved and must be reconciled before implementation. |
| REQ-059 | Partition eligible active-scope data before fitting into disjoint Training and reserved Validation subsets. Fit each candidate using the Training subset and calculate its post-fit Training Loss on that designated subset; reserved Validation participates in neither fitting nor the Training-Loss selection objective. Preserve maximum-condition MSE, four-decimal tied retention and raw-Loss optimization. Exact grouping, ratio, evaluation support, robust-filtered-row handling, validation admission policy and final refit remain unresolved. |
| REQ-060 | Apply reserved Validation as a mandatory admission gate to the retained best-Training-Loss kernels. A kernel failing that check shall not enter collision Qualification. Passing Validation does not replace existing Score/bounds or Qualification checks. Preserve Training-Loss selection, four-decimal tied retention and worker/resource limits; validation metric, threshold, insufficient-evidence and all-rejected behavior remain unresolved. |
| REQ-061 | Compare Training and reserved Validation MSE within each identical active Training Speed x Offset condition. Every required condition must satisfy the subsequently approved allowed-deterioration criterion before the kernel passes Validation. Do not substitute a comparison of independently aggregated Training and Validation maxima or silently omit a failing condition. This does not change the maximum-condition Training-Loss search objective or all-supplied collision Qualification scope. |
| REQ-062 | Provide a separate Learning Settings parameter p in percent for the permitted Validation MSE increase over the same active condition Training MSE. For positive, non-negligible Training MSE T and reserved Validation MSE V, pass that condition when V <= T*(1+p/100). Every required condition must pass. Keep p independent of optimization convergence tolerance and detector Score thresholds. Default, input range/precision, zero/near-zero handling, numerical comparison, persistence and run capture remain unresolved. |
| REQ-063 | Set the default separate Validation MSE allowed-increase parameter to 20 percent. Preserve its user-configurable Learning Settings role, condition-matched all-conditions admission and separation from optimization convergence tolerance and detector Score thresholds. Default selection does not approve zero handling, input bounds/precision or persistence/lifecycle policy. |
| REQ-064 | Apply the Validation gate to finite nonnegative unrounded Training MSE T and Validation MSE V using V <= T*(1+p/100), including zero and arbitrarily small positive T, without an added absolute allowance or epsilon floor. For T=0 only V=0 passes. Do not round gate inputs to four decimal places or divide by T to decide acceptance. Existing invalid-fit and nonfinite/numerical-failure behavior remains distinct and unchanged. |
| REQ-065 | When every retained minimum-rounded-Training-Loss kernel fails the reserved Validation gate, record the round as Validation failed, run no collision Qualification for those rejected kernels, and advance the existing unsuccessful Speed/Offset fallback schedule. Remove the lowest remaining Training speed while multiple speeds remain; otherwise remove the smallest active offset and restore all supplied speeds, stopping when scopes are exhausted. Reoptimize each new active Training scope and preserve earlier qualified results under existing rules. Do not admit higher-Training-Loss runners-up, relax Validation allowance, or misclassify numerical/resource failures as ordinary Validation rejection. |
| REQ-066 | Always use complete Cycle identity (recording_id, cycle_index) as the indivisible assignment unit for the new Training/reserved-Validation partition, regardless of the number of recordings for the same active Speed x Offset. Do not impose whole-recording priority: distinct Cycles from one recording may belong to different subsets, but all otherwise eligible evidence of one Cycle belongs to only one subset. Preserve Collision/Ignore exclusions, original timestamps and existing FIR continuity safeguards; do not splice separate Cycles or recordings into a synthetic history. Partition counts, selected Cycle identities and lifecycle remain unresolved. |
| REQ-067 | For each active Speed x Offset with at least three valid Cycles, pool the valid Cycle identities across its participating recordings, reserve floor(n/3) complete Cycles for Validation and assign the remainder to Training. Counts are per condition, not separately rounded per recording and not based on sample counts. Accept integer rounding deviations such as n=5 reserving one Cycle (20 percent). Preserve indivisible Cycle assignment and existing evidence eligibility. |
| REQ-068 | Every participating recording must retain at least one valid Cycle assigned to Training. Constrain Validation Cycle selection so it never takes every valid Cycle from any recording. This is not a requirement for each recording to contribute a Validation Cycle and does not authorize reducing an existing minimum input-evidence requirement. Exact count and this constraint must both hold; infeasible partition handling remains unresolved rather than silently reducing the Validation count or omitting a recording. |
| REQ-069 | Select complete Validation Cycle identities through deterministic fixed-seed constrained sampling, preserving floor(n/3) per active condition and at least one valid Training Cycle per participating recording. Use the same partition for all candidate kernels, including coarse and fine searches. The same unchanged data identities, canonical ordering, seed and selector version must reproduce the same assignment. Exact seed and ordering/version provenance are implementation details to specify; fallback-scope assignment lifecycle remains pending. |
| REQ-070 | Resample the complete-Cycle Training/Validation partition at the start of each new fallback round rather than retaining whole-run Cycle roles. Within one round, every coarse/fine candidate shares the same partition. Preserve floor(n/3) per active condition, at least one Training Cycle per participating recording and accepted fixed-seed reproducibility, including deterministic round identity in the sampling inputs. A Cycle may change roles across rounds but never within the same round. Exact repeat-partition policy and selector mechanics remain unresolved. |
| REQ-071 | Permit a constrained resampling result in a new fallback round to coincide with a previously drawn partition. Accept the legal result without retrying solely to force different Cycle identities, maintaining deterministic round-specific sampling, floor(n/3), per-recording Training retention and same-round shared evidence. No cross-round uniqueness requirement or partition-exhaustion mechanism is needed. |
| REQ-072 | After a Training-only fitted kernel passes reserved Validation, use that same fitted kernel for Score generation, threshold/confirmation search and collision Qualification. Preserve its FIR coefficients and fitted residual normalization; do not add reserved Validation evidence to an extra combined-data refit before Qualification. The existing one robust refit inside candidate Training remains. Later fallback may train new kernels on its newly assigned Training subset, without relabeling earlier Validation results as evidence for a different fitted kernel. |
| REQ-073 | Include robust-refit-rejected but otherwise eligible designated Training evaluation rows in post-fit Training Loss. The robust rejection mask must not remove points from this evaluation set. Preserve Collision/Ignore and reserved Validation exclusions, raw XYZ MSE and maximum-condition aggregation. Whether to remove fitting rejection itself is a separate unresolved proposal. |
| REQ-074 | Fit each new candidate with Ridge on all otherwise eligible designated Training fitting rows, without residual-based rejection, its affected-history-row mask, its five-percent rejection cap or a subsequent Robust refit. Optimize only Ridge alpha and History; remove Robust K as an active optimization dimension. Preserve Collision/Ignore and reserved Validation exclusions, existing finite/contiguous FIR eligibility and original timestamps. Keep STD normalization and L2_NORM for frozen-model detector Score, using fitted Training evidence without Validation leakage. Removal does not modify raw waveforms, introduce a smoothing filter or certify that all remaining data are noise-free. |
| REQ-075 | Project the accepted coarse-to-fine search onto alpha and History only. Coarse seeds are History ms {0,4,8,16,32,64,128} crossed with alpha {1e-6,1e-4,1e-2,1,100}, giving 35 requested pairs before tap-alias deduplication. Each of four fine starts evaluates lower/current/upper choices in both coordinates, excluding the center, for at most eight valid distinct neighbors. Initial tap step remains max(1,floor(0.032*sample_rate)) and alpha ratio 10; retain the accepted primary-Loss move and step-reduction rules, tap-floor halving to one, and ratio max(1.05,sqrt(r)). Remove K steps. Preserve bounds, final one-tap/alpha-ratio-1.05 resolution, independent four-start convergence, worker limits, Training-Loss objective, tied retention, Validation and fallback. |
| REQ-076 | Provide a Learning Settings option to use Robust refit, default disabled. Disabled mode follows REQ-074/REQ-075 plain Ridge with alpha/History optimization; enabled mode retains the existing residual-based rejection and one robust refit with alpha/History/K optimization. The user-selected mode is not an additional optimizer-search dimension or an automatic failure fallback. Both modes compute Training Loss on all otherwise eligible Training evaluation rows under REQ-073, keep the reserved Validation separate, and retain existing Validation/Qualification guards. Setting capture/edit/persistence and historical model provenance remain to be specified. |
| REQ-077 | When Robust refit is enabled, restore the previously selected integer K domain [2,32], coarse seeds {2,4,8,16,24,32}, 210 requested alpha/History/K triples before tap alias deduplication, up to 26 joint neighbors, initial K step 4 and floor-halving to minimum 1. Preserve the current rejection/refit mechanism including its five-percent cap. When disabled, K has no active search role and the 35-pair/eight-neighbor rules apply. In both modes retain alpha/History bounds and steps, fixed STD/L2 scoring, four fine starts, coarse maximum eight/fine four workers, raw-Loss convergence and rounded tied retention. |
| REQ-078 | Capture the Use Robust refit option when Start Learning begins and lock edits for the entire active run, including coarse/fine search, Validation, Qualification and all fallback rounds. Every candidate uses the captured mode. Unlock only after completion, failure or cancellation cleanup has finished; later edits affect a future run rather than changing fitted-model provenance or active work. Project persistence and existing-result eligibility remain separate. |
| REQ-079 | Persist the Use Robust refit setting with the project through existing Save/Save As operations and restore its saved value on Open Project. New projects retain default disabled. Saved next-run configuration is distinct from each fitted model recorded mode; restoring the setting must not relabel historical model provenance. Missing/invalid fields in existing projects and result eligibility after edits remain separate. |
| REQ-080 | In each round, build separate fixed common target-index sets for Training-Loss and reserved-Validation evaluation using complete finite contiguous support for the maximum permitted 128 ms History realized at the sample rate. All History candidates use those same target indexes within each subset; Training and Validation remain disjoint. Do not crop candidate fitting evidence to this evaluation intersection: each candidate prepares its own otherwise eligible Training rows and follows the selected plain/robust fitting mode. Robust rejection must not remove common evaluation points. This explicitly limits all-eligible Training-Loss evaluation to the shared History-supported set without changing Replay startup zero padding or Score inclusion. |
| REQ-081 | When only the idle Use Robust refit setting changes after a completed run, preserve otherwise-current qualified kernels and their existing Replay/Export eligibility, retaining the fitted model original training mode. Apply the newly selected mode to a subsequent Learning run only and distinguish the next-run setting from result provenance. Do not revive evidence-stale results, promote diagnostic-only candidates, rewrite model mode, bypass active-run export guards or change new-run replacement behavior. |
| REQ-082 | Capture the Validation MSE allowed-increase percentage when Start Learning begins and lock edits throughout coarse/fine search, Validation, Qualification and every fallback round. Every applicable gate in that run uses the captured value, not a later UI value. Unlock only after completion, failure or cancellation cleanup finishes. Project persistence and eligibility of old results after a later edit remain separate. |
| REQ-083 | Persist the configured Validation MSE allowed-increase percentage through existing project Save/Save As and restore its saved value on Open Project. New projects default to 20 percent. Persisted next-run settings must remain separate from the threshold actually used for existing result evidence; do not relabel earlier Validation results. Missing/invalid fields and eligibility after an idle edit remain separately unresolved. |
| REQ-084 | When only the idle Validation allowance setting changes, preserve otherwise-current already-qualified results and existing Replay/Export eligibility under the original recorded allowance. Apply the edited allowance to the next Learning run. Clearly distinguish original-result threshold from next-run configuration and never claim an old result passed a newly tightened threshold. Do not promote rejected/diagnostic results after loosening, revive evidence-stale results, rerun checks implicitly or alter active-run export/new-run replacement guards. |
| REQ-085 | After all four fine-search starts complete, determine the final minimum rounded Training-Loss group from every valid evaluated coarse and fine candidate in the same round, including intermediate candidates and coarse candidates that did not seed refinement. Retain every distinct candidate tied in that group for reserved Validation, with only passing candidates eligible for existing Score/bounds/Qualification. Do not restrict final admission to four branch endpoints or pool Training Loss across fallback rounds with different scopes/partitions. Preserve separately retained earlier qualified results under existing rules. Parameter/model deduplication remains to be specified. |
| REQ-086 | Do not eliminate tied coarse candidates from fine-search starts by shorter History, smaller alpha, smaller K or completion order. Retain all candidates belonging to the qualifying coarse tie group for refinement, permitting more than four starts when ties expand admission. Keep four shared fine-worker slots, not one new worker per start; all admitted starts independently converge and final selection waits for all to finish. The exact top-four cutoff/tie-group population is pending clarification; do not infer refinement of every coarse candidate regardless of Loss. Preserve hardware-insufficiency cancellation and same-round final all-evaluated candidate retention. |

| REQ-087 | Limit concurrent search workers to eight: coarse remains at most eight and the shared fine-search pool may now use up to eight instead of four. Candidate/start count is independent of worker count; excess work queues, all admitted starts complete before final selection, and hardware-insufficiency still cancels the whole run. This is not authorization to multiply pools per start/kernel or to increase the separately accepted Qualification recording-worker cap of four. |
| REQ-088 | Select the top two coarse candidates as the nominal fine-search starts instead of the former top four. Preserve DEC-082 intent to retain tied candidates without parameter-based elimination; the explicit second-place boundary tie expansion remains pending DISC-082, so do not silently impose an exactly-two truncation or refine all coarse candidates. Preserve REQ-085 final all-evaluated same-round rounded-best retention. |

| REQ-089 | Rank valid coarse candidates by unrounded primary Training Loss and admit the best two plus every additional candidate whose raw Loss equals that of the second-ranked candidate. Do not eliminate boundary ties by History, alpha, K or completion order. All admitted starts independently converge, using at most eight shared fine workers with queued excess work, before final selection. Preserve the separate four-decimal final same-round all-evaluated retention rule. |

| REQ-090 | For final best-Loss tie classification, round the final nonnegative primary maximum-condition Training MSE to four decimal places using decimal half-up: at an exact decimal halfway value, round upward. Do not depend on language-default half-even rounding or introduce an epsilon adjustment. Preserve original unrounded Loss for coarse ranking, moves and convergence and preserve unrounded condition MSEs for Validation. Do not round residuals or intermediate per-condition MSEs before maximum aggregation. Float-to-decimal conversion must be deterministic, documented and covered by boundary tests during implementation. |

| REQ-091 | Within a round, an identical effective parameter candidate under identical data, fitting/evaluation masks, split, numerical type and fitting settings is evaluated once and shared across coarse/fine requesting paths. Effective identity uses realized History tap count and exact alpha, plus K only when Robust refit is enabled. Share in-flight or completed valid evaluations and retain one candidate/model entry with requesting-path provenance. Each fine path keeps its own center, step and convergence state and continues independently. Different parameter identities are not merged merely because their Loss or coefficients match. No reuse across fallback rounds is implied; preserve bounded-resource design and whole-run hardware-insufficiency cancellation. |

| REQ-092 | When an otherwise valid candidate's best unrounded primary Training Loss is exactly zero, retain the usual full-neighborhood step-reduction and minimum-step convergence process. Treat zero-to-zero as no improvement without division by zero, finish the planned minimum-step comparisons, and do not stop other admitted paths. A positive raw Loss displayed as rounded zero is not this case. Preserve finite/nonnegative validity checks, all-admitted-start completion, final rounded ties, Validation and Qualification; zero Training MSE is not automatic validation/qualification success. |

| REQ-093 | Retain every distinct candidate in the round's final minimum rounded Training-Loss group that passes both reserved Validation and existing Qualification. Do not reduce this qualified set to a single representative by parameter order, completion order or a newly invented secondary metric. Preserve each retained kernel's fitted model, detector threshold/configuration, Validation and Qualification evidence, originating run/round and actual verified condition coverage. Failed candidates are not qualified models. Preserve REQ-091 identical-parameter deduplication, existing export compatibility/staleness guards, earlier qualified results, fallback scope and resource-failure policy. This is not automatic export or expansion of a kernel's verified operating range. |

| REQ-094 | After the accepted complete-Cycle split, if any active Speed x Offset condition has zero eligible common 128 ms History-supported evaluation targets in either Training or reserved Validation, report the affected condition/subset and reason, skip optimization and Qualification for that round, and continue the established fallback while preserving earlier qualified kernels. Do not lower History, omit the required condition, fabricate Loss, or resample within that round merely to obtain usable evidence. New fallback rounds still resample under REQ-070. This is an evidence-shortage outcome, distinct from numerical failure and hardware-insufficiency whole-run cancellation. |

| REQ-095 | The per-recording valid-Cycle evidence minimum is one, not three. Do not reject a participating recording solely because it has only one or two valid complete Cycles. Preserve the requirement that each recording retains at least one Training Cycle after condition-level partitioning. This governs Cycle evidence, not an invented requirement that raw imported files already contain annotations. |
| REQ-096 | If Learning detects insufficient valid Cycle counts or cannot satisfy the required complete-Cycle Training/Validation allocation, stop the entire Learning run and report the affected condition/recordings and counts. Do not proceed via scientific fallback, silently omit recordings, reduce the Validation count or promote unevaluated models. For each condition with n valid Cycles distributed as n_r per recording, the accepted constraints require v=floor(n/3) >= 1, every n_r >= 1, and sum(n_r-1) >= v so each recording retains one Training Cycle. Cycle-count/allocation shortage is terminal; this differs from REQ-094 zero common 128 ms evaluation targets after an otherwise feasible split, which remains round-skip/fallback. Preserve earlier qualified results subject to their existing provenance/staleness rules. |

| REQ-097 | On Start Learning, before launching fitting workers, check all active conditions' valid complete-Cycle counts and Training/Validation allocation feasibility under REQ-095/REQ-096. If any condition fails, report all detected count/allocation shortages together and stop the Learning request without launching fitting work or allowing a proceed-anyway override. This preflight occurs on Start Learning, not on every settings edit. It does not replace later common-target, fit/numerical or resource checks; any later detected Cycle-count/allocation failure remains terminal under REQ-096. |

| REQ-098 | The Learning Settings relative Loss-improvement convergence tolerance accepts finite values from 0.001 through 100 percent inclusive with at most three digits after the decimal point; retain the 0.1 percent default. This parameter continues to govern the existing strict relative-improvement comparison only after minimum parameter steps and a complete planned sweep. It does not round raw Loss, alter final four-decimal ties, replace the Validation allowance or enable zero/negative tolerance. Preserve accepted persistence, whole-run locking and original-result provenance. |

| REQ-099 | The separate Validation MSE allowed-increase setting accepts finite percentages from 0 through 100 inclusive with at most three decimal places, retaining default 20 percent. Apply the unchanged unrounded per-condition V <= T*(1+p/100) rule to every active condition; zero percent allows no increase, 100 percent permits at most twice Training MSE, and T=0 still requires V=0. Preserve separation from convergence tolerance and detector thresholds, project persistence, run capture/locking and original-result provenance. |

| REQ-100 | When an otherwise supported project omits a new next-run setting field, initialize only that missing field to its approved default: Use Robust refit off, Validation allowed increase 20 percent, and convergence improvement tolerance 0.1 percent (consistent with REQ-039). Preserve every saved valid value. These defaults do not change historical kernel fitting modes, recorded thresholds/evidence or qualification/export eligibility, expand supported schemas, repair present invalid fields or authorize automatic disk overwrite. Use existing explicit Save/Save As for persistence. |

| REQ-101 | For an otherwise supported project whose present new next-run Learning Settings field is invalid, replace only that invalid setting with its approved default and continue loading: Use Robust refit off, Validation allowed increase 20 percent, convergence improvement tolerance 0.1 percent. Report which setting was corrected and its default so the change is visible. Preserve saved valid fields and historical kernel modes, thresholds, evidence and eligibility guards. This does not repair arbitrary malformed project content or unsupported schemas, relabel old results, or overwrite the source file merely by opening it; persistence uses explicit Save/Save As. |

| REQ-102 | When a fine-search path completes its planned neighborhood and continuation is required under the accepted convergence rules, continue from every distinct neighbor tied for the lowest raw primary Loss if that Loss strictly improves the current center. These centers become independent continuing branches with the applicable inherited steps/state; no History/alpha/K/completion-order tie elimination is allowed. Neighbors only equal to the current Loss do not spawn plateau branches: retain the existing no-improvement step reduction. Preserve the minimum-step/full-sweep improvement stopping rule, including retaining evaluated sub-tolerance improvements without forcing further search. All continuing branches must finish before final selection, share at most eight fine workers and identical-candidate evaluations, and obey whole-run hardware-insufficiency cancellation. |

| REQ-103 | Compute each fitted model's External XYZ residual centers and STD from its final Training-fit rows only: all otherwise eligible fitting rows when Robust refit is off, and the retained final-refit rows when Robust refit is on. Use final-model residuals pooled over that model's active Training data, with one mean and STD per axis. Never include reserved Validation for fitted normalization, or recalibrate per replay recording. Store the statistics in the kernel and reuse them unchanged for its Replay/Qualification. Preserve the separate common-mask raw MSE evaluation rules and existing invalid-scale checks; this does not authorize epsilon repair or accepting zero/nonfinite scales. |

| REQ-104 | Fit Ridge and optional internal Robust selection/refit in float64, then convert each final candidate's FIR coefficients/biases to the user's selected target numeric type before evaluating Training Loss or reserved Validation. Predict and subtract raw residuals with the same arithmetic semantics as target Replay on the fixed evaluation masks; convert residuals to float64 before squaring and aggregating MSE. Calibrate REQ-103 residual mean/STD from final target-model residuals on its final Training-fit rows, then store target-representable calibration values. The resulting model/statistics are the same representation used for Score, Qualification and export, with no post-Validation conversion/refit changing the verified model. Preserve finite/scale checks after conversion, target-type cache identity, startup padding and no-Validation calibration. |

| REQ-105 | Explicitly recognized expected candidate-specific invalidity on otherwise valid input, such as nonfinite final model coefficients/predictions or unusable residual STD, records that candidate's parameters/reason and skips the candidate while other candidates continue. Invalid candidates have no valid ranking Loss and cannot qualify; do not fabricate zero/finite metrics or silently change dtype, History, coefficients or scales. If no valid model remains in a round, report that outcome and continue established scientific fallback, preserving earlier qualified kernels under existing guards. Limit this recovery to narrow explicit candidate-invalidity categories: invalid source data, program errors/unclassified exceptions, memory shortage and insufficient Cycle allocation are not swallowed as candidate skips and retain whole-run stop policies. Worker process failure/retry handling is separate. |

| REQ-106 | Do not retry worker startup failures or unexpected process exits without a terminal result. Stop the entire active Learning run on the first such failure, stop queued dispatch and clean up active workers, preserving the actual failure evidence. Do not restart the failed job, reduce concurrency, resample or continue scientific fallback as infrastructure recovery. Preserve earlier qualified results only under existing provenance/staleness rules; do not publish an interrupted partial run as successful. Keep explicit candidate-invalidity skips under REQ-105 distinct from these worker failures. Exact user-facing classification of unconfirmed hardware causes remains pending DISC-099. |

| REQ-107 | Report confirmed memory shortage distinctly as hardware memory insufficient and Learning stopped. For startup/unexpected-exit worker failures whose cause is not established, report worker execution failure and Learning stopped, advise checking hardware specifications/execution environment, and include available actual exception/exit details without diagnosing unproven hardware insufficiency. Both retain immediate whole-run stop with zero retries. Preserve separate program-error, invalid-data, user-cancellation and candidate-invalidity reporting. |

| REQ-108 | A subsequent Start Learning performs new Training/Validation Cycle sampling for the new run instead of inheriting the prior run's saved assignments. Historical assignments restored on Open are evidence for old results, not the next run's partition. Preserve shared assignments within each round, resampling on new fallback rounds, required Cycle counts and per-recording Training retention. The new-run seed generation/lifecycle and associated persistence are pending DISC-101; do not claim resampling guarantees a different legal partition. |

| REQ-109 | Replace post-fit Training MSE as the coarse/fine optimization and parameter-selection objective with held-out Validation MSE from N-fold cross-validation. Evaluate each fixed alpha/History/(enabled Robust K) combination using separately fitted fold models and their held-out Cycle predictions, without fitting coefficients or normalization on that fold's Validation data. Preserve the maximum-condition MSE intent; exact fold aggregation is pending DISC-103. Training MSE no longer ranks search candidates; its diagnostic/overfit-gate role remains to reconcile. Apply the established raw-Loss ranking/convergence and four-decimal half-up final tie rules to the adopted CV objective once fully specified. Do not assume N=3, restore legacy whole-recording-priority CV, choose the best fold as the final model, or infer a final combined-data refit. Fold count/coverage, singleton-recording constraints, the former fixed holdout/20-percent gate and model-to-Qualification identity require explicit reconciliation. |

| REQ-110 | For each candidate parameter configuration, fit separate kernels across its N folds and use the arithmetic mean of the held-out Validation MSEs for CV comparison. Exclude Training MSE from this mean; do not replace equal fold weights by sample-count pooling without a new decision. The supplied three-column example yields means 0.3, 0.5 and 0.4 and selects the first parameter configuration, not an individual best fold kernel. This settles the illustrated within-condition fold-averaging rule; reconciling the previously selected maximum-condition MSE with multiple Speed x Offset conditions remains explicit pending work. N and the final model used for Qualification remain undecided. |

| REQ-111 | Reconsider the Validation-only selection objective so Training MSE also influences candidate assessment: the user explicitly requests mixing Training MSE and wants poor Training fits to be rejectable. Preserve N-fold rotation and separate identifiable Training and held-out Validation measurements. The exact mixed-loss weighting versus an independent Training-quality rejection gate is pending DISC-106; do not silently select a 50/50 weight, threshold or sample-count weighting. A mixed in-sample/held-out metric must not be labeled pure held-out CV MSE or treated as an independent generalization estimate. |

| REQ-112 | Add a Learning Settings boolean option, Include Training MSE in N-fold selection Loss. Enabled: for each fold f compute L_f=0.5*T_f+0.5*V_f using the same fold-trained kernel on its separate Training and Validation data, then arithmetic mean of L_f across folds. Disabled: use the arithmetic mean of Validation MSE alone. Both modes retain N-fold training/held-out rotation and separately reported Training and Validation errors; enabling is not permission to fit or calibrate on held-out data. Lower selection Loss is better, using established raw search/convergence and rounded-four-decimal final ties. Do not introduce a hard Training MSE cutoff or configurable weights. Default, option lifecycle/persistence and multi-condition placement remain to confirm. |

| REQ-113 | The Learning Settings Include Training MSE in N-fold selection Loss option defaults enabled. This initializes the 50/50 mixed objective for a newly initialized setting while retaining the user-selectable Validation-only mode. Existing project missing/invalid-field behavior and saved-result/run lifecycle are not automatically settled by this initial default. |

| REQ-114 | For each fixed candidate configuration and each active Speed x Offset condition c, compute per-fold L_fc according to REQ-112, then condition Loss L_c=(sum_f L_fc)/N, then final candidate Loss=max_c L_c. Keep equal fold weighting within each condition and no averaging or pooling across conditions. Use this same order in both Training-included and Validation-only modes; do not replace it with the mean of per-fold condition maxima. This scalar feeds raw coarse/fine ranking and convergence plus four-decimal half-up final ties. The same candidate fold kernel evaluates all active conditions for its fold. Fold/condition coverage feasibility remains to resolve; missing or invalid folds must not silently be dropped from the mean. |

| REQ-115 | Expose N-fold count N in Learning Settings as a user-configurable integer from 2 through 10 inclusive. N controls the number of rotating held-out folds, independently of the eight-worker ceiling, the 50/50 Loss weights and any Validation MSE allowance. Nominal Validation share per fold is 1/N and Training share is (N-1)/N; complete-Cycle indivisibility and variable Cycle lengths mean actual Cycle/sample shares can differ. Do not add an independently tunable data percentage that contradicts N. Default, run/project lifecycle and fold feasibility remain unresolved; the permitted setting range alone does not establish sufficient evidence for every value. |

| REQ-116 | Initialize the configurable N-fold count to 3. Preserve integer bounds 2 through 10 under REQ-115 and permit users to select another valid value. This initial default does not authorize overriding an explicit valid selection, automatically reducing N for evidence shortage, or selecting a default for legacy/malformed persisted fields without the pending lifecycle policy. |

| REQ-117 | Each participating recording must have at least two valid complete Cycles, replacing the former minimum of one. Preserve at least one Training Cycle from each recording in every fold: all of a recording's Cycles must not be assigned to the same held-out fold. Recordings with fewer than two valid Cycles fail the Learning evidence readiness/preflight, and an infeasible allocation stops Learning under REQ-096; do not silently omit recordings or lower N. This is a completed-annotation/evidence requirement, not a demand that raw imported data already contain Cycle annotations or an instruction to prohibit transient incomplete annotation edits. Two Cycles per recording is not sufficient by itself for N-fold condition coverage or common 128 ms evaluation support; those checks remain separately specified. |

| REQ-118 | For every active Speed x Offset condition, require at least N valid complete Cycles in total across its participating recordings and allocate at least one of those Cycles to Validation in each of the N folds. Each Cycle is held out exactly once and is Training in the remaining folds. Also enforce REQ-117: at least two valid Cycles per recording and at least one Training Cycle from each recording in every fold. Check counts and the actual constrained assignment before fitting; if either is infeasible stop the entire Learning run and report affected conditions/recordings, selected N and available counts. Do not reduce N, omit conditions/recordings or average only nonempty folds. Count/allocation failure remains distinct from zero common 128 ms evaluation targets after an otherwise feasible split. Balancing/randomization details remain pending. |

| REQ-119 | In both coarse and fine search, make evaluation of one candidate parameter configuration on one fold a worker job. Dispatch ready jobs through one shared queue with at most eight concurrent search workers globally, not eight per candidate, fold or fine-search branch. Each job fits one joint model using that fold's eligible active-condition Training data and computes its Training/Validation metrics. Every candidate uses the centrally fixed same-round fold assignments and common evaluation masks. A candidate is eligible for Loss aggregation/ranking only after all N required fold results are valid; partial averages and completion order must not select optimizer moves. Keep central ownership of search state and aggregate max-condition-of-fold-means under REQ-114, existing same-context deduplication and all-search-path completion barriers. Preserve memory-insufficiency cancellation, zero worker-failure retries, and the distinct Qualification maximum of four whole-recording workers. Ordinary capacity queueing does not authorize waiting/retrying after confirmed insufficient memory. |

| REQ-120 | After the established CV parameter-selection stage, select the nominal two lowest-Loss fold-trained kernels for downstream admission rather than fitting a new kernel on combined Training and Validation data. Preserve those kernels' fitted coefficients, target representation, Training-only residual calibration, fold/evidence provenance and separate Qualification metrics. Do not average coefficients or attach one kernel's Validation/Qualification result to another. This changes the proposed one-kernel fold selection to two and rejects the proposed extra full-data final fit for this workflow. The exact fold-kernel scalar across conditions, whether two applies per retained tied parameter configuration or across their combined pool, cutoff ties, fewer-than-two survivors and ordering with Validation admission remain open; do not silently discard parameter-level ties or weaken existing gates. |

| REQ-121 | After each fold kernel computes raw finite nonnegative Training MSE T_fc and Validation MSE V_fc, evaluate V_fc <= T_fc*(1+p/100) separately for every identical active Speed x Offset condition c. The user-configurable allowance p retains its 20-percent default and established range/precision rules; every required condition must pass, equality passes, and T=0 permits only V=0 without rounding or an epsilon floor. Preserve all N folds and their original numerical errors in parameter-level CV Loss, including valid finite folds that fail this allowance. First select the best parameter configurations using the established full-fold aggregate Loss, then exclude failing fold kernels before selecting the nominal two lowest passing fold-kernel Loss entries. Never admit failed kernels to Qualification, average only passing folds, or silently promote worse parameter configurations/relax p when best configurations fail. Pool scope, fold-kernel comparator, cutoff ties and fewer-than-two survivors remain pending. |

| REQ-122 | Apply nominal-two passing fold-kernel selection independently within each retained best parameter configuration, including every configuration tied at the lowest four-decimal parameter-level CV Loss. Do not select only two from a combined cross-configuration pool or allow one configuration to consume another's slots. First exclude that configuration's Validation-failing fold kernels under REQ-121, then select its two lowest fold-kernel Loss entries subject to the pending scalar/cutoff-tie/survivor rules. With two tied best configurations each having sufficient passing kernels and no cutoff ties, select two from each, four total. Preserve exact fold model/calibration provenance, no additional final refit and existing global worker limits; additional selected kernels do not authorize additional concurrent workers. |

| REQ-123 | Within each retained best parameter configuration, rank Validation-passing fold kernels by their own worst-condition Loss J_kernel(f)=max_c L_fc over the required active Speed x Offset conditions. L_fc uses that exact fold kernel's existing Training/Validation measurements: 0.5*T_fc+0.5*V_fc when Training inclusion is enabled, otherwise V_fc. Select the nominal two smallest J_kernel values under REQ-122, subject to pending precision/cutoff-tie/survivor policies.
