## Out of Scope

Phase segmentation or per-phase model switching, new raw-data filters, automatic domain expansion, manual seed controls, auto-export or auto-save, arbitrary corrupted-project repair, higher-concurrency pools, and guarantees of a global optimum, no overfitting or cross-hardware bitwise equality are not authorized. The unrelated selectable-models working specification is outside this change set. Specification confirmation does not authorize any source/test/architecture/Git/external mutation.

## Open Decisions

None.

## Routing/Gates

The decision interview is complete; resume spec-governance materialization. Product execution remains unauthorized until the user sends exact 開始執行 for this confirmed change set. Required implementation gates remain pending; this is not implementation, architecture, numerical or runtime PASS.

- Before source edits, complete architecture ownership/boundary/type/state/dependency/parent mappings from the manifest, adoption, architecture description, baseline and ADRs. Current manifest learning invariants still describe old adaptive folds/worker organization; update them together with source/tests after authorization, never hand-edit generated views or self-accept an ADR.
- Implementation scope: learning-domain settings, constrained fold allocator, target-arithmetic evaluation/calibration, bounded coarse/fine optimizer, candidate-by-fold worker port/adapter/coordinator, Validation/kernel selection and result provenance, desktop settings/progress/error presentation, project serialization/default recovery, and associated architecture algorithm/flow descriptions.
- Implement a deterministic constrained sampler and fold-aware cache identity, bounded IPC/model retention, worker/library thread budgeting and serialization under the accepted contract. These are engineering obligations, not permission to change user-facing behavior or relax worker/resource/evidence rules.
- Unit/behavior evidence must cover folds and coverage failures, both fitting modes, numeric parity, Loss aggregation order, raw/four-decimal ties, endpoint projection/reuse, full-neighborhood convergence, independent branching, gate order, per-configuration kernel retention, no final refit and fallback/failure routing. Each applicable AC requires actual assertion results; superseded ACs require traceability/replacement review, not obsolete feature tests.
- Integration/runtime evidence must cover Windows worker startup/exit/cancellation and memory termination, queue/result barriers, truthful original settings/fold provenance, supported project round trips/default notices, retained Replay/Export eligibility and complete all-supplied Qualification. Verify representative 128 ms resource behavior before making capacity/performance claims. Runtime capability and native scheduler/resource evidence requirements must be established before product changes when those claims are tested.
- Run development and release architecture gates through python tools/architecture/architecture_cli.py with manifest/adoption/baseline and render through that same CLI. Compare generated views, use applicable analyzers, and retain build/full-regression and code-review Spec-axis evidence. A structural specification PASS is not an architecture or product gate PASS.
- If implementation reveals an actual discretionary contract gap, reopen the same specification before asking; do not silently substitute behavior or restart already resolved user decisions. No ADR is accepted by this specification alone.

## Discussion Context
### DISC-001: Improvement scope
- **Situation:** The user asks what can be improved after a table explanation; UI interpretation and model performance are distinct possible scopes.
- **Question:** May this change focus first on table readability and failure explanations while retaining the model, 150 ms window, and qualification acceptance rules?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Presentation work makes existing evidence understandable but does not reduce false triggers or missed collisions; detector investigation requires separate signal, annotation, and model evidence and could lead to different changes.
- **User answer:** User subsequently identified late-Cycle MAE/MSE as the concern, supplied four Replay CSV files, and asked how to reduce late-phase loss. The table-only scope was not accepted.
- **Explicit rationale:** The user observed repeated late-Cycle prediction error; the independent CSV audit supports that observation with a stated exception.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-001 records the current focus under REQ-001; REQ-002 and AC-002 capture the analytical goal. Method and implementation remain unapproved.

### DISC-002: Runtime model boundary
- **Situation:** Four Replay files confirm larger late-phase X/Y errors. Single-model weighting and phase-aware validation are candidates, not validated fixes. Weighted fitting may trade earlier-phase accuracy for later-phase accuracy. A stage-specific model would change deployment behavior and need stage selection evidence.
- **Question:** Should this improvement first be limited to one fixed FIR model and changes to Training only?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Retaining the FIR interface limits runtime/export changes but may limit achievable fit; permitting model/runtime changes broadens the search but adds deployment, selection, and compatibility requirements. No method is selected.
- **User answer:** User explicitly selected 分段評估、選模型 after the explanation that fitting stays unchanged and deployment uses one fixed FIR.
- **Explicit rationale:** User selected the explained method; no additional rationale or permission for accuracy regressions was stated.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-002 confirms the method under REQ-003 and AC-003. REQ-002 and AC-002 still require numerical and validation criteria; no source changes are authorized.

### DISC-003: Candidate ranking priority
- **Situation:** Phase metrics alone do not decide which candidate wins. The earlier A/B illustration favored reducing the worst phase error, but the exact ordering was not approved.
- **Question:** Should candidates be ranked first by the largest MSE among their front/middle/late phases (smaller is better), then by overall MSE when equal?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Worst-phase-first emphasizes balanced phase performance but can sacrifice overall mean error; overall-first largely preserves current ranking and may not improve the late phase. This question does not yet resolve speed/offset aggregation or empty-phase behavior.
- **User answer:** 接受。
- **Explicit rationale:** User accepted the stated worst-phase-first ordering and its explained overall-average tradeoff, without supplying further rationale.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-003 resolves ranking priority under REQ-004 and AC-004; DEC-002 remains the accepted method choice. Aggregation and phase-boundary decisions remain open.

### DISC-004: Phase partition and event masking
- **Situation:** The numerical audit split each original Cycle duration into thirds before excluding annotated events. Compressing eligible samples first would shift phase boundaries and stop comparing the same physical part of the motion.
- **Question:** Should each original Cycle be divided into equal-duration front/middle/late thirds, then retain existing Training-eligible samples inside each fixed phase without compressing time or moving boundaries after exclusions?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Fixed original-time thirds match the prior audit and preserve motion position, but event exclusions may leave unequal valid sample counts or an empty phase. Splitting retained samples into equal counts balances sample counts but moves the physical boundaries and changes the meaning of late-Cycle error. Empty-phase policy is a separate pending decision.
- **User answer:** First requested 可以learning settings時，讓使用者決定分割段數?; subsequently annotated the prior partition-method question with 接受 while answering the maximum-count question with 10段.
- **Explicit rationale:** User requested configurable count and explicitly accepted the previously described partition method. No further rationale was supplied.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-004 and REQ-005 retain configurable count. DEC-006, REQ-007 and AC-007 resolve original-time partition/mask ordering. REQ-004 and DEC-003 remain unchanged; empty-phase handling is still open.

### DISC-005: Configurable segment-count range
- **Situation:** A Learning Settings count is now requested. Higher counts allow more localized evaluation but leave fewer valid samples per segment and can expose empty segments after exclusions. The four-file audit used three; it does not establish a universally safe upper limit.
- **Question:** What maximum number of evaluation segments should users be allowed to select?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** A small bounded integer range keeps the UI simple and limits overly sparse partitions; a larger or uncapped range offers flexibility but requires explicit insufficient-evidence behavior. No upper bound is recommended as empirically validated. A default of three is a candidate consistent with the prior audit, not a confirmed setting.
- **User answer:** 10段。
- **Explicit rationale:** User selected a maximum of 10; no rationale or preference for minimum/default was given.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-005, REQ-006 and AC-006 fix the maximum at 10. REQ-005 and AC-005 still require lower-bound, default and lifecycle details.

### DISC-006: Lower bound and initial segment setting
- **Situation:** Maximum 10 is confirmed; a usable Learning Settings control still needs its lower bound and initial value. The existing four-file audit used three segments.
- **Question:** May the setting allow integers 1 through 10 with default 3, where 1 means evaluate the entire Cycle as one segment?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Allowing 1 offers a whole-Cycle comparison within the same evaluation machinery; starting at 2 enforces segmentation but removes that simple comparison. Default 3 matches the preceding analysis, while default 10 is finer but has fewer eligible samples per phase. No default is claimed to minimize actual error.
- **User answer:** 可以。
- **Explicit rationale:** User accepted the proposed 1-10 range and default 3. No additional rationale was provided; the default is not claimed to be a proven optimum.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-007, REQ-008 and AC-008 resolve the setting domain and default. DEC-005 remains the accepted maximum; REQ-005 and AC-005 still require lifecycle details.

### DISC-007: Empty per-Cycle phase
- **Situation:** An original-time phase may contain no eligible evaluation rows after exclusions and existing FIR support checks. Such a phase has no defined MSE; zero would falsely imply a perfect prediction. Existing code rejects wholly empty Training or held-out evidence, but has no phase-specific policy.
- **Question:** May an empty phase be explicitly marked No valid data and excluded from MSE calculations while other valid phases continue, without merging phases or rejecting the entire recording solely for that empty phase?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Skipping an explicitly reported empty phase preserves usable evidence and fixed phase boundaries but leaves that phase unevaluated. Rejecting the entire recording on any empty phase enforces complete phase coverage but may discard otherwise usable recordings at fine segment counts. Never invent a zero MSE or silently shift boundaries. This local policy does not decide the later cross-condition aggregate coverage requirement.
- **User answer:** 為什麼會有這種情境?我想的是cycle扣除ignore後，再進行分段動作。
- **Explicit rationale:** User expected Ignore to be removed before segmentation and challenged the empty-phase premise. No residual empty-phase policy was accepted.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-008, REQ-009 and AC-009 supersede DEC-006, REQ-007 and AC-007 for partition order. REQ-003 still needs residual empty/insufficient-phase handling after the prepartition exclusion set is settled. Existing wholly empty-evidence rejection remains unchanged.

### DISC-008: Collision treatment before partition
- **Situation:** The user specifies removing Ignore before splitting. Existing fitting/evaluation excludes Collision error samples; that does not establish whether Collision also occupies time/sample positions used to form evaluation partitions. If only Ignore is removed first, a fine phase can still lie entirely inside Collision and have no MSE rows afterward.
- **Question:** Should Collision also be removed before segmentation, along with Ignore?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Removing only Ignore follows the user's literal exclusion set and retains Collision positions during partition, but later MSE filtering may leave empty phases. Removing Collision as well makes partitioning track retained noncollision data, but changes boundaries relative to Ignore-only segmentation. Both preserve original timestamps for FIR and qualification and do not train to fit collisions.
- **User answer:** 對。
- **Explicit rationale:** User confirmed removing Collision as well; no further rationale was supplied.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-009, REQ-010 and AC-010 resolve the prepartition exclusion set. DEC-008 remains confirmed; equal division and remaining insufficient-evidence behavior still need definition.

### DISC-009: Equal division of retained samples
- **Situation:** Ignore and Collision are excluded before splitting. The remaining samples retain chronological order and original timestamps, but may be discontiguous in original time. Integer sample counts may not divide evenly by the selected segment count.
- **Question:** May the remaining samples be divided in chronological order into near-equal sample-count groups, with group sizes differing by at most one sample and any remainder assigned to earlier groups?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Near-equal counts are deterministic and easy to audit on sampled data; retained-duration boundaries can differ under timestamp jitter and require separate boundary conventions. Neither option splices FIR inputs. The location of FIR-support eligibility filtering and insufficient sample counts remain explicit follow-up considerations.
- **User answer:** 接受。
- **Explicit rationale:** User accepted the stated grouping rule and numeric example; no additional rationale was provided.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-010, REQ-011 and AC-011 resolve grouping and remainder allocation. REQ-009 and REQ-010 retain their accepted exclusion behavior; FIR-support eligibility and insufficient-sample policy remain open.

### DISC-010: Cross-Cycle and condition aggregation
- **Situation:** Existing selection aggregates held-out squared errors and XYZ observation counts by Speed x Offset and ranks the worst condition. New phase grouping needs an equivalent cross-Cycle aggregation policy before a worst-phase value is defined.
- **Question:** For each Speed x Offset, may all held-out Cycles' corresponding phases pool their squared errors and sample/axis counts into one phase MSE, then use the maximum over every condition and phase as the primary model-selection metric?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Pooling corresponding phases preserves sample-count weighting and separate conditions, but a single poor Cycle can be diluted by other Cycles in the same condition. Taking the worst individual Cycle-phase instead is more sensitive one-cycle extremes; averaging all speeds together can hide a weak condition. The proposed pooled policy retains XYZ equal weighting and the already accepted overall-MSE tie-breaker.
- **User answer:** Asked how separate Speed x Offset results are handled, requested alternatives, and subsequently selected 取最大值 for the cross-condition stage. Within-condition cross-Cycle pooling was not separately confirmed.
- **Explicit rationale:** The user accepted worst-phase ranking but has not specified how multiple Cycles and speed/offset conditions contribute. Existing implementation supports the provenance of the proposed pooling, not empirical superiority.
- **Resulting impact:** REQ-003, REQ-004, AC-003 and AC-004 require this aggregation contract. DEC-003 remains the accepted ranking order.

### DISC-011: Cross-condition aggregation alternatives
- **Situation:** User requests alternatives to taking the single maximum across Speed x Offset conditions. Keep each condition's worst-phase MSE as its input statistic while comparing cross-condition aggregators; do not silently change fitting, partitioning, qualification or the accepted single-FIR deployment.
- **Question:** Is the main concern that one poor Speed x Offset condition would dominate model selection?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Equal mean of condition worst-phase MSEs balances condition influence but can hide a very poor condition. Averaging the worst few conditions reduces dependence on one extreme but needs a tail count/fraction and loses the strict max objective. A convex combination of maximum and condition mean offers an explicit tradeoff but requires a weight. Each alternative keeps per-condition phase analysis; none has been benchmarked or approved. The pending maximum approach gives strict worst-condition priority, not proof of minimum false/missed events.
- **User answer:** 選擇取最大值。
- **Explicit rationale:** User selected maximum after reviewing alternatives. No additional motive or quantitative tolerance was supplied.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-011, REQ-012 and AC-012 resolve cross-condition maximum; DEC-003 remains intact. Within-condition cross-Cycle pooling in REQ-003 and AC-003 is still open.

### DISC-012: Corresponding phases across Cycles of the same condition
- **Situation:** Cross-condition maximum is now fixed. Multiple held-out Cycles in one Speed x Offset still require one MSE for each corresponding phase. Existing code pools squared error and observation counts; this preserves per-sample rather than per-Cycle weighting.
- **Question:** Within a single Speed x Offset, may corresponding Cycle phases pool their squared errors and valid sample counts into one MSE, retaining equal XYZ weighting?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Pooled sample-weighted MSE represents all eligible observations and matches the existing aggregation style; a single poor Cycle can be diluted by others. Maximum individual-Cycle phase MSE instead prioritizes a single worst occurrence. Equal weighting of Cycle averages differs when retained sample counts vary. No alternative has been benchmarked as better here.
- **User answer:** 接受。
- **Explicit rationale:** User accepted the pooling proposal and its stated dilution tradeoff. No additional rationale was supplied.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-012, REQ-013 and AC-013 resolve the statistical aggregation layer. DEC-011 and the partition/count decisions remain confirmed.

### DISC-013: Segment setting lifecycle
- **Situation:** Evaluation segment count affects candidate ranking. Existing Target Numeric Type is saved in projects, cannot change during Learning/Qualification/cancellation, and changing it marks results stale and the project dirty. Segment-count lifecycle is not yet specified.
- **Question:** May segment count use the same lifecycle: save and restore with the project, lock during a run, and require a new Learning run after a post-run change before results can be treated as current or exported as a qualified profile?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Persisted run-locked settings make results reproducible and prevent stale export, but require rerunning Learning after changes. Session-only or dynamically changing values need additional provenance/re-evaluation behavior and can obscure which setting produced a result. No automatic retraining is proposed.
- **User answer:** 接受。
- **Explicit rationale:** User accepted the lifecycle proposal; no additional rationale or automatic-retraining request was supplied.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-013, REQ-014 and AC-014 resolve setting persistence, locking and invalidation. Segment and aggregation decisions are unchanged. Missing-field compatibility remains separate.

### DISC-014: Existing FIR eligibility and insufficient samples
- **Situation:** Current held-out evaluation uses _fir_rows after annotation eligibility filtering; it requires all taps from the same contiguous source segment and finite inputs/targets. Preserving fitting/evaluation eligibility means not splicing across removed events or inventing predictions for unsupported rows. Phase labels should group the existing eligible prediction rows in chronological Cycle order, without resetting FIR at evaluation boundaries. A very short Cycle can have fewer eligible rows than the chosen count.
- **Question:** Using existing Training-valid prediction rows as the partition pool, if any required held-out Cycle has fewer rows than the configured segment count, may that history/model candidate be marked insufficient and skipped with provenance, without deleting the recording or automatically reducing the count?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Skipping the affected candidate preserves complete nonempty phases and the selected count, but may remove a long-history candidate or leave no viable model. Reducing count silently changes the requested experiment; dropping the short Cycle changes evaluation coverage; inventing zero-error phases is invalid. Existing fallback and previously qualified-kernel retention must remain intact. This is not a change to Replay startup zero-padding or Score inclusion.
- **User answer:** Asked why insufficient data could occur, then requested whether unsupported segmentation can be reported at segmentation time and asked about computational cost. No skip-candidate or global-block policy was accepted.
- **Explicit rationale:** User has not accepted residual insufficient-data handling; the earlier empty-phase proposal was challenged and remains unapproved.
- **Resulting impact:** REQ-003, REQ-009, REQ-011 and corresponding acceptance criteria need an exact eligible-row partition pool and insufficient-candidate policy before implementation.

### DISC-015: Segmentation preflight timing
- **Situation:** User requests early feedback. An interval-count prototype can compute eligibility from already validated contiguous runs without FIR matrix construction or training. Current startLearning has a synchronous preflight before launching its worker, but no segmentation setting check exists.
- **Question:** Should changing the segment count immediately check and show feasibility in Learning Settings, with a fresh confirmation check again before Start Learning begins expensive work?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Immediate feedback prevents waiting until training to discover invalid counts; it requires invalidation when data/annotations change and a responsive UI implementation. Checking only at Start Learning reduces repeated work but delays feedback. Whether a partially unsupported History merely warns, blocks globally, or skips candidates remains separate and must respect fallback semantics.
- **User answer:** 採用。
- **Explicit rationale:** User adopted the two proposed check points. No separate enforcement preference was supplied.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-015, REQ-016 and AC-016 resolve preflight timing. Blocking/warning semantics under REQ-015 and AC-015 remain pending; accepted lifecycle and statistical rules are unchanged.

### DISC-016: Partially unsupported histories and preflight enforcement
- **Situation:** Different histories require different contiguous support lengths. A chosen count may be feasible for short histories but not long ones, and a currently unavailable scope may become feasible under an existing later fallback scope. The earlier skip-candidate proposal was not accepted; immediate feedback alone does not determine whether Learning is blocked.
- **Question:** May preflight warn and skip unsupported history/scope candidates while allowing other partition-feasible candidates to run, and block Start Learning only if none is feasible across the existing allowable fallback scopes?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Partial continuation preserves usable candidates and existing fallback behavior but reduces the candidate set, which must be reported. Blocking whenever any history is unsupported enforces all-history availability but prevents otherwise valid training. Never silently lower the count, drop a short Cycle from an unchanged scope, delete recordings or manufacture zero MSE. A counting pass does not guarantee successful fitting or qualification.
- **User answer:** Corrected timing to Start Learning check followed by a confirmation prompt; stated that unsupported segmentation must still train and only the unavailable region cannot contribute Loss.
- **Explicit rationale:** User explicitly distinguishes phase-loss availability from training eligibility and rejects treating the count check as a reason to skip training.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-016/REQ-017/AC-017 supersede edit-time preflight. DEC-017/REQ-018/AC-018 reject the unapproved skip-history/global-block proposal. Existing fallback is not expanded or restricted by partition failure; missing-loss ranking remains open.

### DISC-017: Loss evaluation versus retraining intent
- **Situation:** User says unsupported partition data should still train and only the affected region cannot judge Loss, and refers to deciding whether to retrain. Prior accepted work defines candidate selection by held-out phase MSE, not an automatic repeated-training loop. Counts can detect missing evaluation data but cannot measure prediction loss.
- **Question:** When referring to retraining, does the user intend automatic additional training based on a Loss criterion, rather than the already discussed comparison of candidate models?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Existing candidate comparison chooses among fitted candidates without a new retry/threshold loop. Automatic retraining needs an explicit acceptance threshold, what changes on each attempt, stopping conditions and resource limits. No such loop or threshold is inferred from this message.
- **User answer:** 希望 Loss 不佳時自動再訓練，但有什麼可以參數可以修改?
- **Explicit rationale:** The term retraining introduces a possible scope ambiguity; counts and prediction loss must not be conflated. No new training algorithm is selected.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-018, REQ-019 and AC-019 record explicit automatic-retraining intent. DEC-017 still requires otherwise valid training despite missing phase Loss. Poor-Loss criterion, retry changes and stopping rules remain open.

### DISC-018: Parameters available for additional training
- **Situation:** Current service.py searches histories 4/8/16/32 ms, Ridge alpha 1e-6/1e-4/1e-2/1/100, scale STD/MAD, robust K 4/6/8 and score MAX_ABS/L2_NORM. Each history evaluates 60 rule combinations using grouped held-out Cycles. Fitting uses a direct Ridge solve and one robust refit with a five-percent rejection cap; it is not epoch-based gradient training. Identical inputs/settings do not create a new search candidate.
- **Question:** Should the first automatic-retraining extension be limited to a finer Ridge-alpha search, keeping the other training settings unchanged?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Finer alpha values retain the same deployed FIR size and fitting method but add CV work and cannot fix a missing model capability. Additional histories expand temporal support but increase feature/matrix and deployment compute/memory costs and may overfit. Changing robust K alters rejection of high-residual training rows but can confuse difficult normal motion with unwanted outliers; it is not guaranteed to lower phase Loss. Reweighting phases would be a distinct fitting-method change and is not approved. Keep phase definitions and validation evidence fixed for meaningful comparisons; exact retry ranges, trigger and stop policy remain separate decisions.

### DISC-019: Joint hyperparameter objective and gradient feasibility
- **Situation:** Best means the minimum held-out worst condition/phase MSE, with overall MSE as tie-breaker, under the already accepted aggregation; this phase objective is planned, not implemented. Current code uses condition-level held-out MSE without phase subdivision. The direct Ridge solve is differentiable in positive alpha while data and retained-row set are fixed. However, current K is integer-valued and controls scores > median(scores) + K * MAD(scores), source-support expansion and a five-percent rejection cap. Holding alpha and the other rules fixed, the retained set is piecewise constant in K, so phase Loss is flat between mask changes and can jump at them. Alpha can also change the mask; the maximum objective has kinks when the worst phase changes.
- **Question:** May the joint alpha/K search use a bounded derivative-free coarse-to-fine approach while preserving the current hard-rejection fitting method?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Derivative-free joint search can evaluate actual hard-rejection outcomes without false gradients and preserve fitting, but costs additional cross-validation evaluations and offers no guaranteed global optimum. Differentiable hyperparameter optimization would require a deliberate smooth-weight/surrogate design for K and careful treatment of the max objective; that changes the accepted fitting semantics and adds implementation/validation work, with no demonstrated gain. Alpha-only gradient plus discrete K search is technically another hybrid, but mask changes still require care and does not provide a joint smooth gradient. No method has been selected or benchmarked.
- **User answer:** User requests first optimizing Ridge alpha, History and Robust K via Loss gradient, then evaluating Score errors and using existing speed/offset fallback followed by reoptimization. The derivative-free proposal is not accepted.
- **Explicit rationale:** The recommended derivative-free direction follows the observed discrete hard-rejection structure, not a performance benchmark. A count check or low fitting residual alone cannot determine the best hyperparameters. CV-winning parameters remain subject to qualification and independent performance validation.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-020/REQ-021/AC-021 extend the parameter set and establish the requested optimization-before-qualification ordering. REQ-020 and AC-020 still require a search-method decision; no derivative-free or smooth-weight method was accepted. Trigger, ranges, K domain, comparison coverage, best retention, resource/stop limits and missing-phase behavior remain open. No product modifications are authorized.

Requiring gradients for all three would require explicit relaxation of tap selection and hard rejection, a changed training design, projection back to discrete deployed FIR and additional validation; it cannot be represented as a drop-in search change. Neither guarantees a global optimum or lower detector error. Lowest held-out Loss may not yield best detector coverage, so qualifying only one Loss winner could discard better-detecting runners-up.

Avoid repeating an Open Project failure solely because this new optional field is absent while preserving all other validation. No change to supported schema versions is inferred.

### DISC-038: Existing results after a tolerance-only setting change
- **Situation:** The tolerance configures future optimizer convergence, not a deployed detector threshold or existing FIR coefficients. Current numeric-type changes call _stale_results and canExportSelected requires Complete; copying that behavior to this new setting is a product policy choice. Run provenance must distinguish a current editable setting from the value that produced saved results.
- **Question:** If only the Loss tolerance changes after completion, may otherwise-current qualified results remain viewable/exportable with their original run tolerance, while the new setting applies to the next Learning run?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Preserving existing qualified results avoids forcing retraining merely to edit a future search setting, but requires clear original-run provenance and must not imply optimization under the new value. Marking results stale prevents confusing current settings with old results but blocks export of an unchanged qualified model until another run. Do not revive results already stale for data/annotation/type changes, upgrade unqualified diagnostics, or rewrite unknown legacy provenance.
- **User answer:** Accepted; user also reports that choice controls have disappeared. Address that interaction concern separately without reopening accepted product decisions.
- **Explicit rationale:** Changing the stopping tolerance alone does not mutate the existing model or its Qualification evidence. The proposal preserves that historical evidence rather than claiming the result satisfies a different search setting. Example: a run at 0.1 percent remains labeled 0.1 percent after the editable setting changes to 0.05 percent.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-039/REQ-040/AC-040 confirm post-run result preservation and refine REQ-037/REQ-038. This is not permission to bypass export or qualification guards. Other optimizer, validation and compatibility details remain open. No source, tests or architecture changes.

### DISC-039: Candidate admission before Qualification fallback
- **Situation:** The accepted pipeline optimizes whole-condition held-out Loss before Score/Qualification and reoptimizes after an existing fallback scope change. The open ledger still distinguishes admitting only the Loss winner from testing a Loss-ranked shortlist. Prediction MSE ranking is a different criterion from detector false/missed-event qualification; no actual candidate comparison or improved coverage has been measured.
- **Question:** When the Loss winner fails Qualification, should another Loss-ranked candidate be tried before removing Speed/Offset under the existing fallback?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Winner-only admission most directly follows the user's original best-Loss-then-qualification request, limits qualification work, and is the recommendation based on that requested workflow, but can discard a condition scope that another candidate might qualify. An ordered bounded shortlist can test that possibility without changing the Loss objective or detector gates, but adds final fitting/qualification cost and needs an explicit candidate count and stopping policy if selected. It does not guarantee improved coverage. No shortlist size is implied.
- **User answer:** 不需要. The user declines trying additional Loss-ranked models before existing fallback.
- **Explicit rationale:** Resolve which models reach Qualification before defining optimizer result retention and fallback orchestration. Recommendation is about fidelity to the requested workflow, not an empirical performance claim. The two policies will be compared in prose/table with one plain-language question because the current mode does not permit the structured choice tool.
- **Resulting impact:** Refines the unresolved admission behavior under REQ-021/REQ-023, without changing the accepted parameter ranges, Loss definition, shared held-out rows or detector gates. DEC-040/REQ-041/AC-041 confirm winner-only admission. No product edits are authorized.

### DISC-040: Empty common validation evidence within a round
- **Situation:** DEC-035 fixes common held-out target rows using maximum permitted History. Existing cross_validate_model_rules rejects a required fold/condition matrix with zero eligible FIR rows; _fir_rows requires contiguous finite support. At 1000 Hz, History 128 ms needs 129 samples per FIR row. A required fold/condition containing only shorter valid runs can have no common validation rows even if a shorter History can fit. This is a hypothetical eligibility case, not a finding in the user's recordings, and does not alter Replay startup zero padding.
- **Question:** How should the run handle a required fold/condition with no common validation rows at the approved History maximum?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Recommend preserving the approved domain and fixed comparison rule: mark the current round as insufficient evidence with affected fold/condition and reason, skip optimization/Qualification for that round, and use existing fallback and prior-qualified-result retention. This preserves established contracts but may yield no model. Alternatively automatically lower the History ceiling and rebuild one fixed shared mask for that whole round; this can retain a trainable scope but changes the agreed search domain and needs an explicit reduction policy and provenance. Never use Loss zero for missing evidence or quietly drop a required condition/fold. A single empty Cycle is not by itself a required fold/condition aggregate shortage.

- **Explicit rationale:** The recommendation preserves the accepted 0-128 ms search domain and the existing evidence-shortage fallback mechanism. It does not claim measured performance benefit or authorize marking numerical exceptions as evidence shortages.

### DISC-041: How many coarse candidates seed fine search
- **Situation:** Coarse-to-fine Loss search is accepted, but the number of coarse candidates retained as fine-search starting points is unresolved. This is internal Training search, not the already rejected Qualification shortlist: only the final Loss winner reaches Qualification under REQ-041.
- **Question:** How many distinct coarse parameter combinations should be retained as starting points for fine search?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** One starting point follows only the coarse Loss winner and uses less fine-search work but does not refine other coarse candidates. Three starting points take the three distinct best-ranked coarse combinations and refine each, then select one final Loss winner; this examines more neighborhoods at additional fitting/evaluation cost. The recommended proposal is three in light of the user's stated desire to explore better models, but three is a design proposal, not a measured optimum. The three may be nearby and do not guarantee distinct basins or a global optimum. Exact neighborhoods, refinement schedule, duplicate evaluation handling and convergence scope remain to be defined.

### DISC-042: Concrete coarse grid proposal
- **Situation:** Search bounds and three fine-search starting points are accepted, but the initial coarse parameter combinations are unspecified. Current source uses four Histories, five alpha values, three K values and two scale methods times two score modes; grouped cross-validation multiplies evaluation work. Larger History also changes fit cost, so tuple counts cannot be translated directly to run duration.
- **Question:** May each round start from the proposed 210 requested History/alpha/K combinations before sample-realization deduplication and fine search?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Proposed grid: requested History ms {0,4,8,16,32,64,128}, alpha {1e-6,1e-4,1e-2,1,100}, integer K {2,4,8,16,24,32}; evaluate their Cartesian product, 7 times 5 times 6 equals 210 requested triples. This includes approved bounds, preserves the existing coarse alpha values, and adds long Histories and broader K points, but costs more fits and still leaves unsampled gaps. A sparser alternative keeps the same History/alpha points and K {2,8,16,32}, yielding 140 requested triples; it reduces coarse evaluations but omits K=4 and K=24 as initial probes. Neither grid guarantees a global optimum; both still use three starts for subsequent fine search. The 210 count excludes cross-validation folds, robust refits and any retained scale/score-mode variants, and may decrease after History tap deduplication at a given sample rate.

- **Explicit rationale:** The 210-triple proposal makes the initial search reproducible and includes all approved parameter endpoints while reusing the current alpha grid. It is a proposed design, not a measured best density or a runtime estimate. Fine search can consider intermediate values; alpha final ratio 1.05 is not the coarse spacing. Treatment of scale and score variants remains a separate unresolved contract.
- **Resulting impact:** Specifies a possible initial-grid component of REQ-024 and REQ-043. DEC-043/REQ-044/AC-044 confirm the 210-triple grid. Worker scheduling remains unresolved; no product edits are authorized.

### DISC-043: Worker scheduling for coarse and fine search
- **Situation:** The user asks how workers are arranged after accepting 210 coarse triples. Static source inspection confirms SpawnHistoryExecutor requires exactly four jobs, launches independent Windows spawn children, merges results in declared order, retries startup/abnormal exit once, and stops on explicit memory/numerical/product errors. Aggregate parent-plus-worker RSS is checked against 85 percent of launch-time available RAM. Each current evaluate_history_job performs CV, final fit, Score and Qualification. The manifest records the same fixed-four policy; ADR-0002 remains proposed, not accepted. These are current facts, not the new optimizer implementation.
- **Question:** May the new optimizer retain at most four independent compute processes with coordinator-managed queued jobs, rather than one permanently assigned process for each History?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Proposed direction: one background coordinator owns scope, immutable folds/common masks, stable candidate identities and ranking; coarse work groups 30 alpha/K triples per History into seven requested-History jobs, dispatched to at most four independent processes as capacity becomes free. Workers evaluate all assigned candidates and return their Loss summaries, not only one winner per History. Wait for the complete coarse stage before selecting three starts. Fine search initially assigns one sequential refinement branch to each of three workers with no nested pools; the fourth need not be busy. After all branches finish, select and final-refit one winner, then run its Score/Qualification once as a stage (with existing detector threshold/confirmation comparisons). Fallback is sequential and regenerates work for the new scope. An alternative is launching all seven coarse History jobs concurrently; it exposes more simultaneous work but duplicates more datasets/matrices and changes the existing concurrency policy more substantially. No measured speed benefit is claimed for either direction.
- **User answer:** 粗搜最多 8 個、細搜 4 個. In an annotation the user asks whether 最後只驗證 1 個模型 means one worker validates it. This selects worker counts only; the accepted three fine-search starts remain unchanged.
- **Explicit rationale:** History grouping can reuse worker-local raw FIR/fold preparation across alpha/K combinations; three fine branches match the accepted starting-point count while preserving single-winner Qualification. Retain process isolation rather than implicitly choosing a shared ProcessPoolExecutor that changes worker-crash semantics. A task queue is conceptual pending interface/state ownership design. Enforce bounded per-worker caches and no nested CV process pools; BLAS thread budgets must be controlled and benchmarked because NumPy linear algebra can itself use threads (https://numpy.org/doc/stable/user/basics.performant_code.html). Exact BLAS budget, admission estimates, worker lifecycle/retries, IPC payloads, deterministic refinement barriers and progress accounting remain open. Four-process concurrency is a proposal, not evidence that four 128 ms workloads fit the user's machine. Memory failures must not be silently treated as Training evidence shortages or trigger scientific fallback.
- **Resulting impact:** Execution design will require explicit refinement of the prior fixed-four-History policy and changes to architecture manifest, execution port/job types, adapter, coordinator, tests and generated views only after authorization and ownership/gate validation. No ADR acceptance, architecture mutation, benchmark or runtime performance validation occurs in this turn. DEC-044/REQ-045/AC-045 confirm stage worker capacities and replace the unaccepted four/three proposal. Detailed job scheduling and Qualification worker count remain unresolved.

### DISC-044: Four fine workers sharing three search starts
- **Situation:** The user selected four fine-stage worker slots while three coarse winners remain the accepted fine-search starts. Assigning an entire sequential search branch permanently to one process can keep only three processes active. Model count and worker count are separate: single-model Qualification does not specify its worker count. The previously proposed seven History-grouped coarse jobs similarly do not keep eight coarse slots busy.
- **Question:** May the three fine-search starts generate candidate-evaluation jobs for a shared queue serviced by four workers, rather than dedicating one worker permanently to each start?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Recommended direction: a coordinator tracks three independent search states, prepares ready candidate evaluations from their current neighborhoods, and dispatches these to four workers. This can use the fourth slot when multiple evaluations are independent, without adding a fourth start. It requires bounded data/matrix caching, duplicate-job/result handling and stable barriers before each neighborhood update so completion timing cannot change search results. Alternatively keep one sequential branch per dedicated worker with four available slots; simpler ownership and cache locality, but the fourth has no branch and may stay idle. Neither guarantees four busy workers or a speedup, and no simultaneous dependent optimizer update is allowed.
- **User answer:** 可以.
- **Explicit rationale:** Worker capacity should not silently alter the approved number of search starts. The shared candidate queue is a proposed conceptual execution design, not acceptance of ProcessPoolExecutor or its failure semantics. A single final model may be qualified by one worker or partitioned independent recording evaluations, but that separate policy is not selected here.
- **Resulting impact:** Would clarify scheduling under REQ-043/REQ-045. DEC-045/REQ-046/AC-046 confirm shared fine-candidate scheduling with coordinator-owned updates and result barriers. Qualification process count, architecture artifact mutation and product implementation remain unapproved.

### DISC-045: Qualification worker count and independent recording jobs
- **Situation:** The final admitted model count is one, but Qualification process count remains open after the user's annotation. Static inspection of _qualification_score_cache and _qualify_model_from_scores shows recording-local continuous score/replay evaluation followed by condition aggregation. All workers must use the same frozen model and target numeric type. Threshold bounds derive from active-round evidence globally, while Qualification continues to cover all supplied recordings.
- **Question:** May Qualification use at most four compute workers, distributing complete recordings while validating only the one final model?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Proposed maximum-four arrangement: dispatch full-recording score preparation, aggregate active-round N/C centrally and form one shared threshold/confirmation candidate set, then dispatch complete-recording detector evaluations using that same candidate set and merge per-condition metrics centrally. Reuse each recording's score for the fixed model rather than recomputing it per threshold. This exposes independent recording work and retains recording-local detector continuity but adds bounded cache/IPC ownership and deterministic aggregation requirements. A single worker processing all recordings sequentially has simpler coordination and fewer concurrent buffers but no recording-level parallelism. Actual throughput and memory benefit require representative validation; four workers are not four models.
- **User answer:** 可以.
- **Explicit rationale:** Recording boundaries are existing independent replay units; arbitrary time slicing could lose FIR/confirmation/hysteresis continuity. A worker may process a complete recording at a time but cannot choose its own threshold, ignore shared bounds or omit excluded-from-training supplied conditions. Score preparation and detector-evaluation phases have a global bounds barrier. The proposal does not specify persistent-process/cache affinity, memory admission or retry mechanics yet and does not run Qualification concurrently with unfinished fine search.
- **Resulting impact:** Would refine the unresolved Qualification scheduling component of REQ-045 while preserving REQ-041 single-model admission and existing threshold/qualification semantics. DEC-046/REQ-047/AC-047 confirm the maximum-four Recording-level arrangement with central barriers/aggregation. No product changes are authorized.

### DISC-046: Memory-aware admission before starting worker jobs
- **Situation:** Accepted stage capacities are coarse maximum eight, fine four slots and Qualification maximum four. History up to 128 ms increases FIR feature width and per-job matrix costs; concurrent job capacity is not proof of available RAM. Existing code stops the run on explicit MemoryError or aggregate RSS-limit breach, rather than treating resource failure as insufficient scientific evidence. No representative 128 ms concurrency benchmark has been performed.
- **Question:** May the coordinator reduce actual simultaneous jobs below the stage capacity when pre-dispatch memory estimates indicate insufficient headroom, retaining queued work and all model-search rules?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Proposed adaptive admission: before starting additional work, estimate its incremental memory including resident process/data/cache costs; admit fewer concurrent jobs when necessary, leave remaining work queued, and show the actual count and memory reason. Preserve every candidate and all History/alpha/K bounds, three starts, common validation rows and qualification/fallback rules. If even one pending job cannot fit after active work finishes and releasable caches are cleared, stop with a resource diagnostic rather than waiting forever or altering scientific scope. Actual runtime MemoryError/RSS breach remains terminal under existing behavior, without an implicit OOM retry. Alternative: refuse the stage unless its configured concurrency can be supported and require the user to free memory/restart; this is more rigid but avoids adaptive admission complexity. The proposal can trade elapsed time for lower simultaneous allocations; it does not guarantee avoiding OOM.
- **User answer:** 記憶體不足，維持之前說硬體不足，取消工作。
- **Explicit rationale:** Stage capacities are upper bounds on active work, not a reason to alter parameter ranges or exclude Speed/Offset when hardware is constrained. Exact estimation, safety margin, cache reclamation and configured memory threshold still need design and validation; no new percentage is selected here.
- **Resulting impact:** Would refine resource admission under REQ-045/REQ-046/REQ-047 and explicitly distinguish pending-job admission from runtime retry/fallback semantics. DEC-047/REQ-048/AC-048 reject adaptive admission and confirm hardware-insufficiency reporting with whole-run termination. No product edits are authorized.

### DISC-047: Fine-search neighborhood at fixed parameter steps
- **Situation:** The user asks to start discussing remaining fine-search mechanics. Three starts, four shared candidate workers, fixed common validation rows, parameter domains and final alpha ratio 1.05 are accepted. Which neighbors are evaluated before each update remains undefined. Initial step sizes and step reduction are deliberately separate from this question.
- **Question:** May each fine-search start evaluate all valid neighboring combinations formed by lower/current/upper values on each of alpha, History taps and integer K, up to 26 neighbors excluding the already evaluated center?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Coordinate-only search tests up to six neighbors, changing one coordinate at a time; it has fewer candidate evaluations but does not test simultaneous coordinate changes at that step. The proposed full local neighborhood uses three choices per coordinate, giving 27 combinations including the center and at most 26 new neighbors. It includes single-coordinate and joint-coordinate changes, at greater fitting cost. The recommendation is the full neighborhood because the requested objective jointly tunes three parameters whose fitted predictions and robust row masks interact; this is a design rationale, not measured superiority. Invalid or duplicate boundary proposals are not extra evaluations; exact boundary transformation and caching policies remain open. Neither method guarantees a global optimum.
- **User answer:** 採用.
- **Explicit rationale:** Explain three choices as alpha divided by the current ratio/current alpha/multiplied by that ratio, History minus/current/plus the current tap step, and K minus/current/plus the current integer step. This does not set the initial ratio or step schedule, and final alpha ratio stays 1.05. Three starts can request at most 78 neighbor triples for such a sweep before overlapping/cached/invalid proposals; that is a job count, not a worker count or total number of CV fits. All work uses the four-worker queue and each required batch completes before its search-state update. Testing the neighborhood does not yet specify overall convergence or tie handling.
- **Resulting impact:** Would clarify the local candidate-set component of REQ-024/REQ-032/REQ-043/REQ-046. DEC-048/REQ-049/AC-049 confirm the at-most-26 joint neighborhood. No source changes or execution authorization are inferred.

### DISC-048: Initial fine-search steps and reduction schedule
- **Situation:** The joint neighborhood is accepted but its initial distances and reduction schedule are not. The accepted coarse grid has largest adjacent History gap 64 ms, K gap 8, and alpha ratio 100. Alpha final ratio is already 1.05. History is represented by integer taps, not a universal fixed millisecond resolution.
- **Question:** May fine search start at half the largest coarse coordinate gap and reduce the coordinate steps when a complete neighborhood has no strictly lower primary Loss, down to the proposed final discrete steps and accepted alpha ratio?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Proposed initial distances are History 32 ms converted to max(1, floor(0.032 times sample_rate)) taps, K step 4, and alpha multiplicative ratio 10 (half of the coarse log-space gap). Before final resolution, move to an improved candidate without reducing steps when strictly lower primary Loss is found; otherwise halve tap and K steps using integer floor with minimum one, and halve the alpha log step, equivalently replace ratio r with max(1.05, sqrt(r)). Coordinates already at minimum remain there. This starts with broader moves and progressively refines, but adds multi-resolution evaluations and is not a benchmarked optimum. An alternative is starting immediately at one tap, K step one and alpha ratio 1.05; simpler resolution handling, but only small local moves are tested from the coarse starts and reaching distant parameter values may require many updates. Both preserve domains and three starts.
- **User answer:** 採用.
- **Explicit rationale:** The proposed initial values derive from the largest adjacent gaps in the accepted coarse grid rather than new search bounds. At 1000 Hz the History example is 32,16,8,4,2,1 taps, corresponding to those millisecond values; other rates use their own sample period. K steps are 4,2,1, and alpha ratios follow repeated square roots ending exactly at 1.05. The reduction rule concerns pre-final resolution only; final-resolution stopping still requires the accepted complete-sweep improvement rule. Exact zero-Loss treatment, tie behavior and per-start versus whole-search convergence remain separate. No execution time cap or performance claim is added.
- **Resulting impact:** Would refine REQ-024/REQ-032/REQ-033/REQ-049 and explicitly set final integer steps to one tap and one K. DEC-049/REQ-050/AC-050 confirm the initial steps, one-tap/one-K minima and reduction rule. No product edits are authorized.

### DISC-049: Independent convergence of three fine-search starts
- **Situation:** The three starts share four candidate workers, but their centers, steps and improvement histories differ. Initial steps and reductions are now accepted. The scope of convergence remains open: a plateau in the best result across starts does not mean every start has reached its own final resolution and completed its required comparisons.
- **Question:** May each of the three starts satisfy its own final-step and complete-neighborhood Loss-improvement convergence criterion, with final selection and Qualification waiting until all three have finished?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Recommended independent completion: each start reaches all minimum steps, completes all its required valid neighbor comparisons and applies the configured relative Loss-improvement criterion to its own best Loss; retain improvements even when below tolerance. A finished start stops proposing work, while the shared workers continue ready work for unfinished starts. Once all three finish, choose one best observed valid candidate using the accepted ranking, final-refit and qualify it. This preserves exploration of all three starts but may take longer than a whole-search rule that stops other starts when the currently best global Loss plateaus; the latter can truncate a still-progressing start and weakens the existing complete-refinement intent. No runtime budget or global-optimum guarantee is proposed.
- **User answer:** 採用4路各自收斂，四路全部完成才進入最終選模. The user changes the three-start proposal to four starts and accepts independent completion of all four.
- **Explicit rationale:** A hypothetical case where A and C are converged while B still improves should not cancel B just because it has not yet beaten A. The four workers are shared compute slots, not one permanently assigned process per start. This decides convergence coordination; exact zero-Loss, equality/numeric tolerance, cache and tie handling remain separate. Resource/cancellation errors retain their terminal policies and do not count as successful convergence.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-050/REQ-051/AC-051 explicitly supersede the former three-start count and no-fourth-start restriction; REQ-052/AC-052 confirm independent convergence and an all-four completion barrier. Worker capacities, tolerance default, grid and single-model Qualification remain unchanged. No product edits are authorized.

### DISC-050: Retaining scale and score variants inside each parameter triple
- **Situation:** The user asks to continue after accepting four independently converging fine-search starts. The open ledger has not resolved how existing STD/MAD and MAX_ABS/L2_NORM variants participate. Static source cross_validate_model_rules compares these variants; _fit_mimo_fir_matrix uses their normalized residual scores to select robust-rejection rows before one refit. They therefore can change fitted coefficients and held-out Loss, not only later detector thresholds.
- **Question:** May every coarse and fine History/alpha/K triple continue comparing all four STD/MAD by MAX_ABS/L2_NORM variants, with the best accepted held-out Loss rank representing that triple?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Recommended preservation: evaluate all four categorical variants for each requested triple on the same folds/common held-out target rows, rank by the accepted whole-condition maximum MSE then pooled MSE and the eventual stable tie policy, and retain the winning variant with the triple's Loss. Coarse search then has 210 requested triples times four variants, or 840 rule configurations before alias/cache deduplication and CV/refit work. Choose the four best distinct triples, not four variants of the same triple, as fine-search starts; each fine neighbor is assessed in the same way. Alternative: fix one scale method and one score mode for the entire optimization, reducing variant work but requiring explicit choices and removing alternatives searched by the current Training. No runtime multiplier or improved detector performance is promised.
- **User answer:** After requesting clarification of residual versus MSE, the user selected 標準差 and XYZ 合成大小 in annotations and stated 選擇標準差跟X、Y、Z合成大小即可. This selects fixed STD/L2_NORM, not the proposed four-variant comparison; it does not change the MSE objective.
- **Explicit rationale:** Keeping current categorical candidates avoids silently changing robust fitting while adding alpha/History/K automation. STD uses residual mean and standard deviation; MAD uses median and scaled median absolute deviation. MAX_ABS combines normalized XYZ by maximum absolute component, while L2_NORM uses square root of summed squares. These Training-internal residual scores control robust refit; they are distinct from the unchanged held-out MSE objective and from later detector Score/threshold Qualification. Fine search may select a different winning categorical variant at a new triple; all variants are frozen with the final selected model before Qualification. This leaves the already accepted four-worker fine capacity and single-model Qualification unchanged.
- **Resulting impact:** Would clarify the categorical-variant component of REQ-023/REQ-024/REQ-044/REQ-051. DEC-051/REQ-053/AC-053 now select fixed STD/L2_NORM and reject the four-variant search proposal. No product changes are authorized.

### DISC-051: Deterministic ranking when prediction Loss ties
- **Situation:** In an annotation the user confirms that coarse/fine MSE ranking means smaller is better and asks to continue. This agrees with REQ-023: each model's primary metric is maximum condition MSE, and models minimize that metric; the existing secondary metric is overall pooled MSE. The remaining equal-metric order is not selected. Four parallel fine-search starts must not let completion timing decide model selection.
- **Question:** If primary and pooled MSE are both exactly equal, may ranking prefer shorter effective History, then use ascending alpha and K solely as a deterministic final order?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Proposed lexicographic order is smaller worst-condition MSE, smaller overall pooled MSE, fewer FIR taps, smaller alpha, then smaller K. This retains the accepted Loss objective and secondary metric and uses shorter FIR only when both error metrics tie. Alpha/K order is an arbitrary stable rule, not a claim that lower regularization or K is better. Alternatively use a predeclared stable candidate sequence after both MSEs tie, without explicitly preferring shorter History; this can retain an equally accurate but longer FIR and needs sequence generation independent of completion timing. Neither option compares rounded display values or treats the configured convergence tolerance as a ranking tie band.
- **User answer:** After clarifying that the proposal referred to all four ordering levels, the user stated 我的想法是相同loss的kernel都保留，都去做Qualification. The user chooses tied-kernel retention and rejects parameter-based elimination; this does not resolve whether pooled MSE also defines a tie.
- **Explicit rationale:** A shorter FIR has fewer coefficients/lag terms at the same input/output dimensions, so a shorter-History preference at exactly tied prediction metrics has a structural cost rationale, not measured runtime evidence. Preserve every strictly lower Loss as better even when its improvement is below the convergence tolerance. This question covers ranking of coarse candidates and best-model retention; exact equal-primary update/convergence semantics and zero-Loss handling remain separate. No worker completion order may act as a tie-breaker.
- **Resulting impact:** Clarifies REQ-023 minimization wording and proposes deterministic tie details under REQ-024/REQ-051/REQ-052. DEC-052/REQ-054/AC-054 accept retaining and qualifying all best-Loss ties instead of a single model. Tie metric/equality and candidate population remain pending. No product edits are authorized.

### DISC-052: Which Loss metrics define co-best kernels
- **Situation:** The user now requires all best-Loss ties to enter Qualification rather than selecting a single winner by History/alpha/K. Previously accepted ranking used maximum condition MSE first and pooled MSE second. It is unclear whether equal primary Loss alone now retains a kernel when pooled MSE differs.
- **Question:** If A and B have exactly equal best maximum-condition MSE but different pooled MSE, should both be retained and qualified?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Primary-only ties retain both A and B, leaving pooled MSE as diagnostic information rather than an admission filter; this most directly matches the user's reference to equal Loss and allows Qualification to assess both at extra cost. Requiring both primary and pooled MSE to tie preserves the old secondary ranking and reduces the tie set but excludes a model whose primary Loss is equally good. Example uses exact illustrative values: A primary 0.010, pooled 0.006; B primary 0.010, pooled 0.008; C primary 0.012, pooled 0.005. Primary-only retains A/B; the two-metric rule retains A only. C is not co-best under either rule.

- **Explicit rationale:** Resolve what equally best means before specifying candidate storage or multi-model Qualification scheduling. Do not silently treat display rounding or the convergence tolerance as a tie band. No change to worker counts is implied by retaining more kernels. Scope of all observed candidates versus final branch representatives and post-Qualification selection remains a separate open point.
- **Resulting impact:** Would refine REQ-023 and REQ-054 by selecting whether the existing pooled-MSE secondary metric filters the tied admission set. DEC-053/REQ-055/AC-055 confirm retaining both metrics in their established order with all ties preserved. No product implementation is authorized.

### DISC-053: Explicit correction to primary-only best-Loss retention
- **Situation:** The preceding response retained ties only after both maximum-condition and pooled MSE filtering. The user now explicitly changes that policy to retain every kernel at the minimum maximum-condition MSE.

- **Options and tradeoffs:** The selected primary-only policy retains A/B/C when their maximum-condition MSE is equal and minimal, even if C has larger pooled MSE. The superseded two-metric policy would exclude C. More tied kernels can require more Qualification work; this does not increase worker capacities or admit a kernel with worse primary Loss merely because its pooled MSE is smaller.
