Four fine-search starts remain unchanged; candidate storage/population, equality precision and handling ties at the coarse top-four cutoff still need precise rules.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-054/REQ-056/AC-056 explicitly supersede DEC-053/REQ-055/AC-055. The previous example now retains A, B and C and excludes D. No product changes or execution authorization are implied.

### DISC-054: Exact versus near-equal best-Loss ties
- **Situation:** The user asks to continue and why the interview stopped after acknowledging the last change. Continue to the next unresolved decision after reconciling selections rather than requiring repeated continue prompts. The current retention metric is minimum maximum-condition MSE alone, but equal-Loss numerical comparison is unspecified.
- **Question:** May tied-best retention require exact equality of the unrounded computed primary Loss values rather than display-rounded equality or a near-equality band?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Recommended exact comparison retains only candidates whose stored computed primary Loss equals the minimum; it follows the user's lowest-Loss rule and adds no new tolerance parameter, but tiny computed differences exclude otherwise similar models. A separate absolute/relative numerical equality band can include near-best models and reduce sensitivity to tiny differences, but admits a strictly larger computed Loss, increases possible Qualification work and requires its own explicit tolerance definition including zero handling. Neither policy may silently reuse the configurable 0.1 percent convergence-improvement threshold or compare formatted display strings.
- **User answer:** 應該是要看小數點後幾位進行四捨誤入?我想確認loss data是用training data來計算吧. The user raises decimal rounding and asks about the Loss data source; exact unrounded equality is not accepted, no decimal count is selected, and no switch to in-sample Loss is confirmed.
- **Explicit rationale:** Illustrative computed primary Loss values A=0.010000, B=0.010001, C=0.010000 may display identically at four decimal places. Exact equality retains A and C, not B. Whether B joins a near-equal set depends on a separately chosen band; no band value is proposed as approved. Exact comparison is about values produced within the run and is not a claim of cross-platform numerical reproducibility.
- **Resulting impact:** Would refine the equality component of REQ-034/REQ-054/REQ-056 while leaving pooled MSE diagnostic-only for admission, all minimum-tie retention, worker limits and convergence tolerance unchanged. No equality policy or product edits are approved in this turn.

### DISC-055: Decimal places for rounded Loss ties and Training-data clarification
- **Situation:** The user suggests rounding Loss to a chosen number of decimal places and asks whether Loss is computed from Training data. Existing cross_validate_model_rules forms training groups as the complement of each held-out fold and computes squared prediction errors only on held-out targets. This matches the previously accepted grouped-CV selection design. The source dataset is the same eligible Training recording data, but each fold's evaluation points are not used to fit that fold model.
- **Question:** If rounded Loss is used for tied-best retention, how many digits after the decimal point should be retained?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Illustrative five-decimal comparison makes 0.010000 and 0.010001 both display as 0.01000 and permits a larger tied set; six decimals distinguishes them and is more selective. These examples explain precision, not a recommended universal setting, and other digit counts are possible. Proposed scope is to retain the raw computed primary MSE for optimization and convergence and round only the final primary Loss used for tied-admission comparison; do not round individual residuals, sample errors or coefficients. Digit count, exact rounding convention and that limited scope are not yet approved.
- **User answer:** Annotation 1 selects 小數後4位. Annotation 2 says the three-Cycle example describes the last offset/speed case and requests computing Loss on all Training data after fitting to choose kernels, optionally retaining one third to one quarter for overfitting checks. The source confirms CV currently spans all active conditions, not only the final scope; the new Training-Loss selection direction is recorded separately.
- **Explicit rationale:** In an illustrative three-Cycle single-recording case, fit C1/C2 and evaluate C3, then rotate; pool held-out XYZ errors by active Speed/Offset and take maximum condition MSE. After selection, refit selected parameter sets on all otherwise eligible active Training data. This is neither Qualification collision-event scoring nor the final fitted model's in-sample residual MSE. The user's question is not treated as a request to replace previously accepted CV. Only eligible Cycle-contained Normal/Pick/Put rows participate; existing Collision/Ignore exclusions and common-History evaluation support remain unchanged.
- **Resulting impact:** Would refine the equality policy under REQ-056, with any optimization-versus-admission distinction made explicit before implementation. DEC-055/REQ-057/AC-057 confirm four-decimal tied retention. DEC-056/REQ-058/AC-058 record the requested Training-loss redesign; exact partition and overfit semantics remain open. No product edits are authorized.

### DISC-056: Meaning of all Training data when reserving overfit validation
- **Situation:** The user requests post-fit Training Loss for kernel selection, with approximately one quarter to one third reserved for checking overfitting. Existing CV actually includes all active Speed/Offset conditions; the previous three-Cycle table was illustrative, not a final-round-only rule. The requested replacement has an unresolved boundary: whether all Training data excludes the reserved subset.
- **Question:** Does all Training data mean all eligible fitting-subset data after setting aside the reserved validation portion, with that held-out portion excluded from fitting and the Training-Loss selection metric?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Recommended separation: split eligible active-scope evidence first; use about two thirds to three quarters for fitting and post-fit Training-Loss selection, and the reserved one quarter to one third for a separate validation/overfit assessment. This keeps the roles explicit but Training Loss alone does not establish generalization, so overfit criteria and their effect on selection must be defined later. An alternative all-data fitting/selection interpretation includes the reserved portion; its error can still be reported but cannot then serve as an independent held-out check of that same fitted model. Exact split proportion, group selection, fold rotation, after-check final refit and whether the overfit check is a filter or diagnostic are not selected here.
- **User answer:** 對. The eligible Training remainder excludes reserved Validation; candidate fitting and post-fit Training-Loss selection use that remainder.
- **Explicit rationale:** The user's latest request changes the selection objective, so prior CV-based selection is not simply repeated as the answer. Preserve whole-Cycle/Recording identity and prevent Training/validation leakage while deciding the new split; no random individual-row splitting is inferred. Public reference supporting the separation principle: https://scikit-learn.org/stable/common_pitfalls.html#data-leakage. This does not assert that the application uses scikit-learn or that a fixed validation subset proves absence of overfitting.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-057/REQ-059/AC-059 confirm disjoint data roles and post-fit Training-Loss selection. Exact partition membership/ratio, common-History comparison, adaptation of evidence-shortage rules, validation gate and final-refit policy remain open. No product edits are authorized.

### DISC-057: Reserved Validation as a diagnostic or Qualification admission gate
- **Situation:** Training Loss selects the minimum rounded maximum-condition MSE kernels. Reserved Validation is separate, but its effect on progression is undecided.
- **Question:** Should failure of a subsequently defined Validation check exclude a kernel from collision Qualification?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Recommended gate: assess the selected Training-Loss tied kernels on reserved Validation and admit only those passing the agreed check; this makes validation actionable but can reject every selected kernel and requires a defined criterion and all-rejected policy. Diagnostic-only validation reports metrics without excluding kernels, preserving progression but allowing suspicious validation results to proceed.
- **User answer:** 對，未通過 Validation 檢查的 kernel，不進入 Qualification.
- **Explicit rationale:** A Training/Validation gap can indicate generalization concerns but is not by itself proof of overfitting. No numeric ratio, threshold, metric aggregation, extra search, higher-Training-Loss admission or fallback action is implicitly chosen. A validation subset used for decisions is not an untouched final test.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-058/REQ-060/AC-060 confirm the gate and refine admission under REQ-056/REQ-059. Preserve Training-Loss ranking, tie precision, worker capacities, collision Qualification and hardware-failure handling. Criterion, threshold, evidence-shortage and all-rejected responses remain open. No implementation authorization.

### DISC-058: Condition-matched Training versus Validation MSE checks
- **Situation:** Validation now gates Qualification, but its comparison rule is undefined. The Training objective is maximum condition MSE. Comparing only independently aggregated maxima can hide a large increase in another condition.
- **Question:** Should Validation compare Training and Validation MSE for each same active Speed x Offset condition, requiring every condition to satisfy a subsequently chosen allowed-increase rule?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Recommend condition-matched checks: expose each condition's change and reject the kernel if any required condition fails; this matches the user's worst-condition concern but requires valid evidence in each partition and can be sensitive to small or noisy groups. Alternatively compare only maximum Training MSE and maximum Validation MSE: simpler summaries but maxima can come from different conditions and conceal deterioration below the overall maximum. Both consume the same predictions; neither requires additional fitting merely for aggregation.
- **User answer:** 可以. Accept condition-matched comparison and require all active conditions to pass.
- **Explicit rationale:** Illustrative A: Training=0.010, Validation=0.011; B: Training=0.001, Validation=0.004. Aggregate maxima increase by 10 percent while B increases by 300 percent. These are synthetic examples, not measured results or selected thresholds. Training/validation comparison is supported conceptually by https://scikit-learn.org/stable/modules/learning_curve.html; the condition-wise gate is a project design proposal, not a prescribed library rule or proof of overfitting. Percentage versus absolute/combined tolerance, zero/near-zero handling, numerical precision and missing evidence remain unresolved.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-059/REQ-061/AC-061 confirm condition-matched all-conditions checks under REQ-060. No numeric threshold, percentage/absolute criterion, split ratio, final-refit policy, higher-Loss admission or all-rejected fallback is inferred. No product edits.

### DISC-059: Configurable percentage allowance for Validation deterioration
- **Situation:** All active conditions must pass paired Training/Validation MSE checks, but the allowed deterioration has no formula or value. This setting must be distinct from the optimizer convergence-improvement tolerance and the detector Score threshold.
- **Question:** Adopt a separate Learning Settings percentage parameter for the permitted increase of Validation MSE over Training MSE?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Recommended percentage allowance compares error relative to each condition's Training baseline and is interpretable across different baseline magnitudes; it adds a user-facing parameter and needs a separate zero/near-zero policy. An absolute MSE increase allowance instead measures error growth in squared acceleration units and does not divide by a small baseline, but one fixed allowance has different relative strictness across conditions and requires a physical-unit scale decision. Neither tolerance is a statistical proof that overfitting is absent.
- **User answer:** 可以. Accept the separate configurable percentage allowance and the stated positive-baseline comparison rule.
- **Explicit rationale:** For positive, non-negligible Training MSE T and reserved Validation MSE V, the proposed pass condition is V <= T*(1+p/100). An illustrative p=20, T=0.010 permits V<=0.012 and rejects V=0.013. Twenty percent is an example, not an approved or empirically recommended default. Improvement on Validation is not rejected by this upper-bound rule. Zero/near-zero treatment, default, bounds, precision, persistence and run capture remain open; no arbitrary epsilon or equality rounding is assumed.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-060/REQ-062/AC-062 confirm the separate configurable Validation allowance, without changing four-decimal selection ties, raw-Loss optimization, collision thresholds, evidence partition or all-rejected behavior. Default and numerical/lifecycle edge cases remain unresolved. No product edits.

### DISC-060: Default Validation MSE increase allowance
- **Situation:** The user accepted a separate percentage parameter, but the earlier 20 percent example was not a selected default. No representative disjoint Training/Validation experiment establishes a suitable cutoff for these recordings.
- **Question:** What percentage should be the default permitted Validation MSE increase?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Illustrative 10 percent is stricter and rejects increases above 10 percent, but may exclude kernels because of normal between-Cycle variation. Illustrative 20 percent tolerates more variation and admits more kernels, but permits greater deterioration before Qualification. Neither value is empirically recommended; the user may supply another value. With T=0.010 the respective upper bounds are 0.011 and 0.012. Both have the same evaluation cost and retain independent collision Qualification.
- **User answer:** 預設20%.
- **Explicit rationale:** Remain neutral without measured split-specific variation. This is a default-setting decision, not permission to declare the model free of overfitting, automatically relax the allowance, or reuse the optimizer convergence threshold.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-061/REQ-063/AC-063 confirm the default as 20 percent. Bounds, zero/near-zero treatment, split membership, lifecycle and all-rejected behavior remain separate. No product edits.

### DISC-061: Zero and near-zero Training MSE under the percentage rule
- **Situation:** The selected percentage rule was limited to positive non-negligible Training MSE. Default p is now 20 percent. A division-based displayed percentage is undefined when Training MSE is zero, whereas the multiplicative pass inequality itself needs no division.
- **Question:** Extend the same multiplicative inequality to all finite nonnegative unrounded MSE values, including zero, with no additional absolute allowance?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Recommend retaining V <= T*(1+p/100) on unrounded values without an absolute floor: T=0 requires V=0, and tiny positive T retains the same relative rule. This preserves the accepted percentage meaning and needs no arbitrary physical-unit tolerance, but can reject numerically tiny absolute differences. Alternatively add an explicit absolute allowance for small baselines; this permits small absolute errors near zero but requires another calibrated squared-acceleration threshold and changes which kernels can pass. Neither behavior is inferred from the default selection.
- **User answer:** 可以. Accept the same percentage rule at zero and near-zero with no separate absolute allowance.
- **Explicit rationale:** For illustrative T=0, V=0 passes and V>0 fails under the recommended policy. For T=1e-8 and default p=20, the mathematical bound is 1.2e-8. Four-decimal rounding remains solely best-Training-Loss tied retention and must not turn positive gate inputs into zero. Do not compute V/T to decide acceptance at T=0, and do not label its displayed relative increase as a finite percentage. This question does not make invalid/degenerate FIR fits valid or change existing nonfinite/numerical-failure handling; exact representation and boundary tests remain implementation obligations.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-062/REQ-064/AC-064 extend REQ-062 to zero and near-zero finite unrounded baselines without a new tolerance. Default, data split, final refit, existing numerical-error behavior and all-rejected fallback remain unchanged or unresolved as applicable. No product edits.

### DISC-062: All best-Training-Loss kernels fail Validation
- **Situation:** All kernels tied at the minimum rounded Training Loss are evaluated by the mandatory per-condition Validation gate. The rule disallows failing kernels from Qualification but does not yet specify what happens when none passes.
- **Question:** If every retained best-Training-Loss kernel fails Validation, mark this round as Validation failed and continue the existing Speed/Offset fallback search, preserving earlier qualified results under existing rules?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Recommended existing fallback: record per-condition Validation failure reasons, do not run Qualification on rejected kernels, advance the existing scope schedule and optimize the new active Training scope. This preserves the minimum-Loss-only admission rule and existing control progression, but can reduce supported conditions and incur further training cost. Alternatively consider successively higher-Training-Loss candidates within the same scope; this may find a better-generalizing kernel without dropping conditions, but explicitly changes the previously selected minimum-Loss-only rule and requires a new candidate order, stopping policy and cost definition. No alternative is implemented or implicitly accepted.
- **User answer:** Initially asked 這是什麼? about 就繼續原本 fallback. After the source-backed speed/offset explanation, user answered 採用, accepting this fallback for all-Validation-rejected rounds.
- **Explicit rationale:** The recommended failure reason is Validation failed, not insufficient evidence and not collision false/missed detection, because collision Qualification did not execute. Do not automatically relax the configured Validation allowance, reuse excluded validation rows for fitting, retry an identical deterministic search, or infer that a particular Speed/Offset caused the failure. Follow the existing scope schedule rather than inventing a new removal order. Memory/numerical errors remain outside ordinary gate-failure fallback.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-063/REQ-065/AC-065 connect all-Validation-rejected rounds to the existing unsuccessful-scope fallback. Preserve prior qualified kernels under their original scope/provenance and existing exhaustion behavior. Scope changes require renewed optimization and valid partition evidence, with exact partition policy still unresolved. No product edits.

### DISC-063: Whole-group units for the new Training and Validation partition
- **Situation:** Separate Training and reserved Validation roles are confirmed, but the new post-fit selection workflow has no exact partition specification. - **Question:** For each active Speed x Offset, keep independent recordings whole when multiple are available; otherwise partition by complete Cycles without splitting a Cycle's eligible samples between Training and Validation?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Recommend retain existing whole-recording priority with whole-Cycle fallback: preserve existing evidence identities and prevent fitting on part of a held-out group, but exact reserved sample percentages may be unattainable with few or unequal groups. Alternatively always split by complete Cycles even when several recordings exist: allows finer allocation and both partitions can cover each recording, but forfeits whole-recording separation. Random individual-row splitting is not proposed or approved. Both require valid Training and Validation evidence for every active condition.

Preserve existing Collision/Ignore exclusions: whole group means assigning all otherwise eligible data from a group to one side, not including excluded samples. Exact ratio, selected group identities, deterministic ordering, insufficient groups, fixed assignment across candidates/fallback and final refit remain separate. Whole-recording priority assumes recordings represent independent acquisitions; do not infer that copied or overlapping files are independent.

Current whole-recording-priority implementation/manifest will need coordinated change after authorization. No exact split count, selected identities, fold rotation, evidence-shortage relaxation or product edits are authorized.

### DISC-064: Number of Validation Cycles per active condition
- **Situation:** All partitions use complete Cycles. The user requested approximately one quarter to one third reserved, but integer Cycle counts cannot always achieve that interval. Counts refer to Cycles, not sample counts; unequal Cycle lengths can produce different sample fractions.
- **Question:** For each active Speed x Offset with at least three valid Cycles pooled across its recordings, reserve floor(n/3) complete Cycles for Validation and use the remainder for Training?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Recommend downward rounding of one third: n=3,4,5,6,8 gives Validation counts 1,1,1,2,2 respectively. This preserves more fitting evidence and matches one quarter to one third in those examples except n=5, where one held-out Cycle is 20 percent. Upward rounding of one third allocates more Validation evidence but fewer Training Cycles; n=4 would reserve 2 (50 percent) and n=5 reserves 2 (40 percent). Neither policy can produce a 25-33 percent whole-Cycle split at n=5, and neither is selected yet.
- **User answer:** Annotation 1 accepts 採用 for the floor(n/3) proposal; the request adds 要保證每個recordining 至少要有一個cycle進行training.
- **Explicit rationale:** Recommendation favors preserving Training data while approximating the requested fraction. The arithmetic is an illustrative policy comparison, not measured validation accuracy. Cycles must retain existing eligibility and condition identity. Cases with fewer than three valid Cycles, the old per-recording minimum, precise Cycle choice, reproducibility and freezing across candidates/fallback remain unresolved; no automatic acceptance of insufficient evidence is inferred.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-065/REQ-067/AC-067 confirm per-condition counts; DEC-066/REQ-068/AC-068 require at least one Training Cycle per participating recording. Actual identities, selector, reproducibility, sparse-evidence policy and final refit remain unresolved. No product edits.

### DISC-065: Reproducible constrained selection of Validation Cycles
- **Situation:** Counts and per-recording Training minimum are confirmed, but which Cycle identities become Validation is undecided. Candidate comparisons need stable evidence rather than a new draw for every parameter triple.
- **Question:** Select complete Validation Cycles using deterministic fixed-seed constrained sampling, respecting the per-condition count and at least one Training Cycle per recording?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Recommend fixed-seed constrained sampling with canonical input ordering and recorded assignments: the same unchanged data and split configuration reproduce the same partition, candidates use the same evidence, and the design does not always reserve the beginning or end of recordings. One split still can be unrepresentative, and temporal independence is not guaranteed. Alternatively choose by an explicit chronological rule, such as later Cycles reserved for Validation: simple to explain and appropriate for testing later behavior, but weights the check toward recording end conditions and requires its own tie/order rule. Neither choice restores rotating CV selection.
- **User answer:** Initially requested an explanation and acknowledged understanding with 了解. After the explicit adoption question, user answered 可以, accepting fixed-seed constrained sampling, shared candidate partitions and reproducibility.
- **Explicit rationale:** Preserve exact floor(n/3) per condition and reject selections that leave a recording with no Training Cycle. No per-recording Validation quota or uniform-probability claim is implied. Candidate workers must not resample independently; propose freezing assignments before optimization and retaining assignments for unchanged conditions during scope fallback. Exact seed, canonical identity ordering, assignment persistence and missing-evidence behavior remain to be specified. Current source still requires at least three valid Cycles per recording (service.py around lines 695-707); the added one-Training-Cycle invariant does not relax that existing input gate. Pure assignment feasibility requires floor(n/3) <= sum(n_r-1), but no new failure response is selected here.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-067/REQ-069/AC-069 confirm the selector and candidate reproducibility direction. Exact seed/canonical identity implementation, fallback lifecycle and evidence-shortage behavior remain to be specified. No actual data partition, fit, benchmark or product edits.

### DISC-066: Keep per-condition partitions fixed across fallback scopes
- **Situation:** Fixed-seed sampling and common candidate partitions are accepted. Fallback changes active conditions and can restore excluded speeds after removing an offset. A single global random stream rerun on a changed set could otherwise alter assignments for conditions still present.
- **Question:** Create and freeze the condition-specific Training/Validation assignments before the run's search, retaining the same assignments for unchanged conditions across all fallback rounds, including reactivated speeds?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Recommend freeze per-condition assignments for the whole run: scope changes only activate/deactivate existing condition partitions; a returned speed uses its original Cycle roles. This isolates scope changes from split changes and preserves validation roles, but repeatedly evaluates the same reserved subset and does not explore alternative partitions. Alternatively redraw on each fallback round with deterministic round-specific inputs: evaluates other splits but introduces data-selection variation alongside scope changes, can move formerly held-out Cycles into Training, and requires a different evidence/provenance policy. No redraw is accepted here.
- **User answer:** 重新抽樣. Reject whole-run frozen assignments and select resampling for each fallback round.
- **Explicit rationale:** For a condition at 500 mm/s, excluding that speed then restoring it after removing the smallest offset must not change which of that unchanged condition's Cycles are Training versus Validation under the recommended policy. This concerns assignments only, not reuse of FIR coefficients, scores or Loss across newly optimized scopes. Annotation/data/settings mutation during a run and supported saved-project reproduction remain separate lifecycle matters. No empirical independence or generalization guarantee is asserted.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-068/REQ-070/AC-070 confirm round-level resampling. Preserve accepted fixed-seed reproducibility and within-round shared evidence, with round identity part of deterministic sampling provenance. Default seed, exact repeat policy, project persistence, final refit and shared History support remain open. No product edits.

### DISC-067: Coincidentally repeated partitions after resampling
- **Situation:** Each fallback round now redraws its constrained Cycle partition, but a new random draw is not a guarantee of different identities. With one recording and three eligible Cycles, reserving one Cycle has only three possible partitions.
- **Question:** Allow a valid new draw to coincide with a previous partition, without forcing a different assignment?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Recommend allow repeats: redraw using reproducible round-specific sampling inputs, accept the valid resulting partition and proceed without uniqueness retries or an exhaustion policy. This is simple and respects the fixed constraints, but a round may not get different held-out Cycles. Alternatively enforce a distinct partition from a selected previous set; this can increase partition diversity but changes the sampling rule, requires defining whether distinctness is adjacent or global and how to handle no remaining legal alternatives, and adds selection work. Neither policy is inferred solely from 重新抽樣.
- **User answer:** 可以. Accept coincidentally repeated legal Cycle partitions across round-specific draws.
- **Explicit rationale:** Illustrative repeated condition assignments could be Validation Cycle 2, then Cycle 1, then Cycle 2 again. These are hypothetical outcomes, not actual draws or a promise of exact seed outputs. Fixed base seed plus deterministic round identity preserves the earlier reproducibility decision; the user has not asked to abandon it for nondeterministic startup seeds. Do not force partition changes by violating floor(n/3), splitting Cycles or consuming every Training Cycle from a recording.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-069/REQ-071/AC-071 confirm repeated legal outcomes are allowed without uniqueness retries. Seed mechanics, final-refit policy and input-evidence details remain open. No actual sampling or product edits.

### DISC-068: Model identity after passing reserved Validation
- **Situation:** Candidate parameters are optimized using post-fit Training Loss, the lowest rounded-Loss tied kernels undergo reserved Validation, and failures are excluded. Whether the final kernel is refitted using reserved data after that check remains unresolved.
- **Question:** Use the same Training-only fitted kernel that passed Validation for subsequent Score, threshold search and collision Qualification, without an intervening refit on Training plus reserved Validation?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Recommend preserve the validated fitted kernel: retain its FIR coefficients and fitted residual normalization, so Validation evidence belongs to the model actually undergoing Qualification. This avoids another fitting stage but leaves reserved Cycles unused in fitting that kernel. Alternatively refit selected hyperparameters on both subsets before Qualification: more data participate in fitting, but coefficients and fitted statistics can change, the earlier Validation pass is not evidence for this new model, and a separate evaluation policy would be necessary. Neither alternative implies guaranteed better performance.
- **User answer:** Initially annotated the no-refit proposal with 不懂. After the three-Cycle clarification, user answered 了解，採用, accepting the same fitted kernel into Qualification without a combined-data refit.
- **Explicit rationale:** Existing one robust refit inside candidate Training is unchanged; the proposal only excludes an additional post-Validation combined-data fit. Threshold and detector-confirmation search still happen after fixed-model scoring under existing rules. Round-level resampling can change Cycle roles for newly fitted models in later rounds, but does not retroactively change earlier models or transfer their Validation pass to a different fitted model.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-070/REQ-072/AC-072 resolve the post-Validation final-refit policy, preserving Training-internal robust refit and later fallback training. Data partition, ties, worker/resource rules and existing Score/Qualification guards remain unchanged. No product edits.

### DISC-069: Training Loss includes robust-rejected evaluation points
- **Situation:** The selected objective is post-fit Training Loss over designated Training evidence. Existing _fit_mimo_fir_matrix first fits all eligible matrix rows, computes robust rejection using K, and fits again on retained rows. The objective must specify whether rows rejected only for robust refitting remain in Training-Loss evaluation.
- **Question:** Compute post-fit Training Loss on all otherwise eligible designated Training evaluation rows, including those excluded from the robust refit, rather than scoring only the candidate's retained rows?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Recommend include robust-rejected evaluation rows: candidates with different K are assessed on the same applicable evidence rather than candidate-dependent retained subsets, following the user's all-Training-data objective. Difficult or noisy eligible rows can still dominate MSE despite being excluded from the robust fit. Alternatively score only retained rows: reports residuals on the robust-fit subset, but different K can change both the model and the scored sample population, so lower reported Loss may reflect omission of difficult rows. Both preserve existing Collision/Ignore and Validation exclusions.
- **User answer:** 採用。我在想是否不需要排除了較難預測的點，因為IMU本來就是毛躁. Accept the evaluation rule; separately question fitting rejection.
- **Explicit rationale:** Static source evidence: service.py _fit_mimo_fir_matrix around lines 1561-1611 fits initial weights on all features, caps affected rows at floor(0.05*n), then uses features[retained] for robust refit. An illustrative eligible evaluation set of 1000 points with 50 robust exclusions uses 950 in the final robust fit but evaluates all 1000 under the recommended rule. This example is not a measured project outcome. It does not add excluded Collision/Ignore data or held-out Validation data back to Training Loss, and does not resolve History-specific support/common-mask details.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-071/REQ-073/AC-073 confirm evaluation of all otherwise eligible Training points. Fitting rejection removal remains pending DISC-070. No product edits.

### DISC-070: Removing residual-based fitting rejection
- **Situation:** User questions whether noisy IMU data should retain difficult points during fitting. Current service.py _fit_mimo_fir_matrix first fits Ridge, computes residual scores and a K-controlled cutoff, rejects affected rows capped at five percent, then refits. Residual size does not prove a sample is faulty.
- **Question:** Replace robust rejection/refit with Ridge on all eligible Training rows and remove the inactive Robust K search dimension, leaving alpha and History optimization?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Full-data Ridge preserves legitimate difficult normal-motion evidence and simplifies fitting, but large residuals have more influence through squared error and actual faulty points may distort the fit. Retaining current robust refit preserves alpha/History/K optimization and some protection against atypical points, but may exclude legitimate vibration from the final fit. Conditional recommendation favors full-data Ridge if the intended policy is to fit all valid normal-operation evidence; no empirical performance winner is established.
- **User answer:** 我認為取消Robust refit，因為已經有ignore了。Robust refit 有點像是對資料進行濾波了. Accept removal following the prior explicit alpha/History-only impact explanation.
- **Explicit rationale:** Source inspection shows K controls the rejection cutoff and otherwise is stored/search/export metadata. Removing rejection/refit leaves no model effect for the current K. Ridge uses squared residuals plus coefficient regularization; see https://scikit-learn.org/stable/modules/generated/sklearn.linear_model.Ridge.html. Robust regression addresses outliers/model errors; see https://scikit-learn.org/stable/modules/linear_model.html#robustness-regression-outliers-and-modeling-errors. Noise alone does not establish bad samples or prove robust fitting unnecessary. No new performance experiment was run.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-072/REQ-074/REQ-075/AC-074/AC-075 confirm removal and its mechanical two-coordinate search consequences: 35 requested coarse pairs and at most eight neighbors, preserving four starts and worker capacities. Raw waveforms are not filtered by the old robust refit; it selects fit rows. Ignore remains an explicit exclusion, not proof that all remaining samples are fault-free. New-model metadata/export compatibility and precise fitted-statistics evidence must be specified before implementation. No product edits.

### DISC-071: Common evaluation timestamps for Training Loss across Histories
- **Situation:** Earlier REQ-036 selected common held-out timestamps. The objective has since changed to post-fit Training Loss, so the comparison mask must be adapted explicitly. ### DISC-072: Capture and lock the Robust refit option for a Learning run

Neither option silently changes active candidate semantics.

### DISC-084: Duplicate parameter candidates within one round
- **Situation:** Coarse search and multiple fine-search starts can reach the same effective FIR tap count and alpha, plus active K when Robust refit is on. Different parameter sets with equal Loss remain distinct under accepted all-ties retention.

- **Options and tradeoffs:** Recommend deduplication by effective parameter identity plus complete fitting/evaluation context, sharing in-flight/completed valid evaluations while preserving each start's own convergence state and provenance. This avoids duplicate jobs and entries but requires explicit coordinator ownership, bounded cache and deterministic identity. Alternatively evaluate once per start: simpler isolated state but redundant work and duplicated kernel bookkeeping. Neither option merges different parameters merely because their Loss or coefficients match.

- **Explicit rationale:** Example two starts both request History 16 ms and alpha 0.01 with Robust off at the same sample rate, same round, partition and numeric type. One evaluation can serve both without dropping either search path. With Robust on, K is part of identity; nominal History aliases use actual taps. Cross-round reuse is not implied.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-087/REQ-091/AC-091 confirm identical-parameter/context deduplication under REQ-085 and REQ-089, preserving REQ-087 worker ceiling, independent paths, all-start completion and hardware-insufficiency cancellation. No unbounded model retention, source change or measured speedup is approved.

### DISC-085: Exact-zero Loss convergence without division by zero
- **Situation:** Relative improvement uses (J_old-J_new)/J_old for positive unrounded Loss. An otherwise valid finite MSE candidate may reach exactly zero, where this ratio is undefined. A displayed rounded 0.0000 is not sufficient evidence of exact zero. Existing finite-fit and valid normalization checks remain independent.
- **Question:** At exact raw best Loss zero, retain the complete minimum-step neighborhood/convergence process, treat zero-to-zero as no improvement without division, and wait for all other admitted fine paths?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Recommend keeping the established full-sweep rule: reduce steps after completed non-improving neighborhoods, finish at minimum steps, and classify zero-to-zero as no improvement. This preserves planned comparisons and opportunities to retain other evaluated tied kernels, but performs more evaluations even though nonnegative MSE cannot improve below zero. Alternatively stop that path immediately on valid exact zero: saves evaluations but reduces exploration of equally scoring candidates and would require an explicit exception to the full-sweep rule. Neither stops other paths or bypasses Validation/Qualification.
- **User answer:** 可以.
- **Explicit rationale:** Zero is the lower bound of nonnegative MSE, not evidence of good reserved Validation or collision detection. This question covers exact raw zero only; positive Loss rounded to zero still follows the positive-baseline calculation.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-088/REQ-092/AC-092 confirm exact-zero behavior under REQ-034, refining REQ-032/REQ-052/REQ-089. Preserve REQ-064 Validation handling, numerical-invalidity checks and same-round tied retention. No implementation.

B may keep diagnostic evidence but is not a qualified export candidate. Earlier qualified kernels remain under their existing provenance rules; display/recommendation order is separate.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-089/REQ-093/AC-093 confirm all qualified tied-kernel retention under REQ-054/REQ-085. Preserve REQ-091 identical-parameter deduplication, scientific fallback scope, Validation admission, resource-failure policy and actual verified coverage. No product changes.

### DISC-090: Convergence percentage input range and precision
- **Situation:** The configurable relative Loss-improvement tolerance has an accepted default of 0.1 percent, but its editable domain/precision is still unspecified. It applies only after the final minimum-step complete sweep, independently of the Validation allowance (default 20 percent) and detector threshold.
- **Question:** Allow convergence improvement tolerance from 0.001 to 100 percent inclusive with at most three decimal places, retaining the 0.1 percent default?

It permits finer or looser stopping than the default, excludes zero which cannot satisfy the existing strict improvement-below-tolerance test at a positive plateau, and excludes values above the 100 percent maximum possible decrease of nonnegative Loss. Its cost is not permitting requested tolerances below 0.001 percent without a future precision change. Alternatively permit any finite positive tolerance up to 100 percent with higher precision/scientific notation: more flexibility, but more complex input/display and a greater chance of very long searches for tiny improvements. Neither option guarantees an optimum or a specific runtime.
- **User answer:** 可以.
- **Explicit rationale:** The 0.001 percent floor is a proposed UI precision choice, not a measured optimal threshold or scientific necessity. Smaller values demand smaller improvements before stopping and may increase work; larger values are more permissive but still require minimum steps and a complete sweep. Four-decimal final Loss tie rounding remains separate.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-093/REQ-098/AC-098 complete the input domain of REQ-035 and constrain future persisted values under REQ-038/REQ-039. Preserve REQ-034, REQ-092, locking/provenance and final-step/full-sweep rules. No product changes.

The 100 percent cap is a proposed product policy, not a mathematical upper bound or empirically optimal threshold.
- **User answer:** 可以.
- **Explicit rationale:** At p=0 require V<=T; at p=20 require V<=1.2*T; at p=100 require V<=2*T. All required conditions must pass, and T=0 still requires V=0 at every permitted p. This percentage is not an accuracy rate, proof of no overfitting, or a Qualification substitute.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-094/REQ-099/AC-099 complete the input domain of REQ-062 and constrain persistence validation under REQ-083. Preserve REQ-063 default, REQ-064 gate, capture and historical-result provenance. No product changes.

### DISC-092: Defaults for absent new settings in supported projects

Alternatively require the user to choose each absent setting before Learning: makes the new configuration explicit but adds a setup step for every such project. Neither expands supported schemas, repairs present invalid values, infers historical settings from new defaults or grants old models new validation/qualification status.

- **Explicit rationale:** Defaults apply to next-run configuration only. If an old model used Robust refit, setting a missing next-run checkbox to off must not rewrite its model record as plain Ridge. No automatic source-project overwrite is implied; use the existing explicit Save/Save As flow.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-095/REQ-100/AC-100 confirm REQ-079/REQ-083 absent-field defaults consistently with REQ-039. Preserve saved valid values, REQ-081/REQ-084 provenance/eligibility guards and unsupported-schema rejection. Present-invalid-field policy remains separate. No product edits.

### DISC-093: Invalid present Learning Settings during Open Project
- **Situation:** Missing new fields now receive defaults, but a saved field may exist with an invalid type, nonfinite value, out-of-range value or unsupported precision. Treating such a field as absent would silently replace an explicit configuration.

- **Options and tradeoffs:** Recommend explicit atomic rejection: prevents silent changes to the user's stored configuration and keeps the current session intact, but requires correcting the file or using a separate explicitly authorized recovery path before it can open. Alternatively open the project's otherwise valid data in a repair-required state and block Learning until the user fixes the setting: makes recovery easier in the UI but needs additional staged invalid-state ownership and must not silently change model provenance or eligibility. Neither option treats a present invalid value as an absent field or broadens supported schemas.

A missing Validation field still defaults to 20 percent under REQ-100. This proposal only addresses these newly governed settings, not blanket recovery of arbitrary corrupt project content.

### DISC-094: Multiple equally best improving fine neighbors
- **Situation:** Coarse admission keeps all second-place raw-Loss ties, and final retention keeps all distinct rounded-best evaluated kernels. Within a fine path, however, a completed neighborhood may contain multiple distinct parameter candidates tied at the lowest raw Loss, all strictly improving on the current center. Choosing which centers continue exploration is distinct from retaining their already evaluated models.
- **Question:** If several neighbors tie at the best raw Loss and strictly improve the current center, continue fine search from all those centers as independent branches?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Recommend continuing all equally best improving centers to follow the user's preference not to discard equal-Loss alternatives; this can discover different subsequent improvements but increases branch count, work and resource demand without guaranteeing a better model. Alternatively choose one deterministic center and keep the others only as evaluated candidates for final selection: bounds path growth but needs an explicit tie selector and explores fewer directions. Both preserve final tied retention; neither chooses by worker completion order or exceeds eight concurrent fine workers.
- **User answer:** 可以.
- **Explicit rationale:** Example current raw Loss 0.010 and distinct neighbors B/C each 0.008: both improve and tie for the best, so both continue under the proposal. Neighbors merely equal to the current center do not create plateau branches under the existing no-improvement step-reduction rule. Exact duplicate parameter/context evaluations still share results, all surviving branches must finish, and hardware insufficiency still cancels the whole run.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-097/REQ-102/AC-102 confirm tied improving fine-neighbor continuation under REQ-050/REQ-089, preserving REQ-091 evaluation reuse, REQ-087 worker cap, REQ-092 exact-zero handling, material-improvement stopping and final retention. No implementation.

### DISC-095: Training-only residual STD calibration for each fitted model
- **Situation:** STD residual normalization and XYZ L2 Score are fixed, while Robust refit is now optional. Current domain/learning/service.py lines 1596-1605 computes final residual centers/scales on robust-retained fitting rows and stores them in FrozenFirModel. The new plain mode needs an explicit calibration row set, separate from common-mask MSE evaluation and reserved Validation.
- **Question:** Compute each final fitted model's per-axis residual mean/STD solely from the rows used in its final Training fit: all eligible fitting rows with Robust off, retained refit rows with Robust on, then freeze those statistics for that model's Replay/Qualification?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Recommend final-fit Training rows: matches the existing robust mode, gives plain mode a direct corresponding rule, and excludes reserved Validation from fitted normalization. With Robust on, rejected rows do not set normalization scale even though they still participate in accepted common-mask Training Loss evaluation. Alternatively use all otherwise eligible Training fitting rows for calibration in both modes: includes rejected-row residual spread in robust normalization and may change its Score/threshold behavior, requiring a change from existing robust semantics. Neither includes Validation or recalibrates separately for each replay recording.
- **User answer:** 可以.
- **Explicit rationale:** Calibration uses final-model residuals with one stored mean and STD per External XYZ axis, pooled over that model's eligible active Training fitting data. Common 128 ms masks remain the separate comparable Training/Validation MSE row sets; fitting eligibility is unchanged. Fixed-model statistics are reused across supplied recordings so replay does not adapt normalization to the recording being checked. Preserve existing invalid-scale safeguards; no epsilon repair or zero-STD acceptance is proposed.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-098/REQ-103/AC-103 confirm final-fit Training-only calibration, refining REQ-073/REQ-074/REQ-076 while preserving REQ-080 common evaluation masks, fixed STD/L2 Score and same-validated-model Qualification. No product changes.

### DISC-096: Target-numeric model evaluation for Loss and Validation
- **Situation:** The user selects float32 or float64 before Learning. Current service.py _predict_rows uses a matrix multiply, while _target_replay_arrays lines 1432-1465 casts coefficients/inputs and explicitly accumulates products in the selected target dtype. The new Training-Loss optimizer and held-out Validation must specify which deployed-model representation and prediction arithmetic they evaluate.
- **Question:** Keep Ridge/optional robust fitting in float64, but quantize the final fitted model to the selected target numeric representation before Training-Loss/Validation evaluation, using the same FIR arithmetic semantics as target Replay and float64 MSE aggregation?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Recommend evaluating the selected-target model throughout ranking and Validation: avoids knowingly selecting solely on unquantized coefficients when the selected export/replay model differs. It requires shared/verified target-numeric prediction logic and may cost more than vectorized high-precision evaluation. Alternatively optimize and validate using float64 model predictions, converting only before Score/Qualification: closer to current matrix-scoring cost but fitting-stage rankings/Validation can differ from the target-model values. Neither approach by itself proves bitwise agreement with arbitrary physical hardware/compiler settings.
- **User answer:** 可以.
- **Explicit rationale:** Proposed sequence: fit in float64 on the accepted Training rows (including existing float64 internal robust-selection/refit if enabled); convert final coefficients/biases to target dtype; predict and compute raw residuals with target Replay semantics on the fixed evaluation masks; aggregate squared residuals/MSE in float64. Calibrate mean/STD on REQ-103 rows using the final target-model residuals, then store target-representable calibration values. Use the same final model/statistics for subsequent Score/Qualification/export without a post-Validation conversion changing the validated model. This is numeric representation, not a new alpha/History search dimension or time shift.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-099/REQ-104/AC-104 confirm target-numeric evaluation and same-validated-model identity, refining REQ-080/REQ-091/REQ-103. Preserve finite/scale guards after conversion, split, raw Loss, startup padding and threshold/Qualification rules; no silent numerical repair or measured performance claim. No product edits.

### DISC-097: Expected candidate-specific numerical invalidity
- **Situation:** The new bounded optimizer explores many candidates and evaluates target-numeric models. A particular otherwise valid-data candidate may fail explicit model-validity checks, such as nonfinite target coefficients/predictions or an unusable residual STD. Current history_workers.py classifies ValueError/ArithmeticError as batch failures; indiscriminate exception swallowing would also hide bugs or invalid source data.
- **Question:** For explicitly recognized candidate-specific model invalidity, record the parameters/reason and skip that candidate while continuing other candidates, with all-invalid rounds advancing existing fallback?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Recommend skip only explicitly classified expected candidate invalidity: permits valid alternative models to be found and reports why a candidate cannot be used, but requires narrow error categories and complete diagnostics. If no valid candidate remains in a round, record no valid model and follow existing scientific fallback. Alternatively stop the whole run on any such candidate invalidity as current batch-level error handling does: simpler fail-fast handling but one invalid parameter choice prevents evaluating other potentially usable choices. Neither converts an invalid model to zero Loss, silently changes dtype/scale/History, or catches all runtime exceptions as candidate failures.
- **User answer:** 可以.
- **Explicit rationale:** Scope is candidate-specific finite/normalization checks on otherwise valid input, not invalid source data, program errors, worker crashes or resource failures. Memory shortage and insufficient Cycle allocation remain whole-run stop; worker crash/retry behavior and unclassified exceptions remain separate. Invalid neighbors do not rank or satisfy a successful comparison with fabricated metrics; their explicit evaluated-invalid outcome may be recorded as unavailable under the future search contract.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-100/REQ-105/AC-105 confirm narrow candidate-invalidity skip/fallback handling around REQ-104 while preserving numerical validity, REQ-096 terminal Cycle shortage, hardware cancellation and REQ-094 common-evidence handling. No implementation.

### DISC-098: One retry for worker startup or unexpected exit
- **Situation:** Current history_workers.py retries a History job once after startup failure (lines 139-145) or an unexpected process exit without a terminal result (lines 236-242). Reported memory failure stops all workers immediately; reported data/numerical/product failures are terminal. New candidate-specific invalidity now has its own skip policy, and the new scheduler operates on candidate/recording jobs instead of fixed History jobs.
- **Question:** Preserve one infrastructure retry per affected job for worker startup failure or unexpected exit without a terminal outcome, rerunning the identical job once and stopping the entire Learning run if it fails again?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Recommend preserving one retry: may recover a transient worker launch/exit problem without changing the scientific search, but repeats that job's work and does not guarantee recovery. Alternatively stop on the first such failure: simpler, faster failure reporting but loses recovery from transient infrastructure problems. Neither retries explicit memory failure or user cancellation, nor treats known invalid candidates as infrastructure failures.
- **User answer:** 不需要，直接說硬體規格不足.
- **Explicit rationale:** A retry uses the same captured data, Cycle split, parameters, numeric type and job identity, not new sampling or a changed kernel. Completed unrelated results are not blindly recomputed; completion accounting accepts one authoritative terminal result. Startup and exit failures share the affected job's single retry budget (at most two attempts), not an unlimited budget per failure subtype. Program exceptions already reported as such remain terminal.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-101/REQ-106/AC-106 reject retries and confirm immediate whole-run stop. The requested hardware-insufficiency message is carried to DISC-099 to distinguish reporting intent from an unproven cause. Preserve REQ-048, REQ-105 and REQ-096 distinctions. No source or architecture edits.

### DISC-099: Evidence-accurate message for unclassified worker failure
- **Situation:** The user requests no retry and the message hardware specifications insufficient. No-retry termination is now settled. Current startup catches general process-start exceptions and unexpected-exit handling only has an exit status; neither alone proves insufficient hardware. Explicit memory errors or monitored memory-limit breaches do provide a distinct hardware-memory insufficiency reason.
- **Question:** Keep immediate no-retry termination, but report confirmed memory shortage as hardware/memory insufficient and report unknown worker failure as Learning stopped: worker execution failed; check hardware specifications and execution environment, with actual available error details?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Recommend evidence-specific messages: honors immediate stopping and directs the user to hardware/environment checks without falsely diagnosing the cause, while retaining actionable worker/error/exit details. Alternatively use a shared neutral heading such as execution unavailable and show the actual known reason underneath; simpler consistent presentation but less direct hardware guidance. Declaring hardware insufficiency as a confirmed cause for every unknown worker failure is not supported by the current evidence.
- **User answer:** 可以.
- **Explicit rationale:** No failure occurred in this design turn; these are future message semantics. Do not convert a program exception, cancellation or unknown exit into a proven memory/hardware diagnosis. This wording discussion does not reopen the user's zero-retry instruction.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-102/REQ-107/AC-107 confirm evidence-specific presentation under REQ-106, preserving REQ-048 confirmed-memory handling and distinct failure policies. No product edits.

### DISC-100: Persist actual per-round Cycle assignment provenance

It adds metadata and consistency/compatibility checks. Alternatively save only the seed/configuration and reconstruct assignments on demand: smaller metadata but depends on the exact sampler/data version and gives weaker direct evidence of actual earlier assignments. Neither guarantees identical numerical results across changed runtimes or hardware.

Open restores historical records without applying next-run settings to them. Subsequent Start Learning still draws as accepted rather than implicitly resuming an interrupted run. Scope is provenance for saved results and originating rounds, not an unlimited archive of every past run, automatic saving or duplicate raw data.

### DISC-101: Automatic fresh-run seed and recorded actual N-fold assignments

A manually chosen/reused seed allows intentional repetition but adds a user setting and requires changing it for a fresh draw; that alternative is not already authorized. Saving only a seed gives less direct inspection and depends on preserving the exact sampler version, identities and ordering; actual mappings retain what happened even if later software changes. Training membership follows from the remaining Cycles within that round; no raw data duplication is required. The chosen assignments are fixed for all same-round coarse/fine candidates and their kernels. Explicit project Save/Save As persists the captured metadata; Open restores it as historical evidence, not the next run's active partition. Different draws may legally coincide, so no retry forces a different partition. No manual seed control, same-round resampling to improve Loss/evidence or automatic disk write is implied. ### DISC-102: Considering grouped N-fold and its role in model selection
- **Situation:** The user is considering N-fold instead of the previously discussed single complete-Cycle holdout with Training-Loss optimization. No CV redesign is adopted. Standard grouped folds train a new model for each held-out group of Cycles; rotating evaluation cannot be performed by fitting one model to all Cycles and merely scoring subsets.
- **Question:** Should the proposed N-fold design also change coarse/fine parameter selection to held-out cross-validation MSE instead of the currently accepted post-fit Training MSE?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Recommend held-out CV MSE selection if the aim is selecting alpha/History/(enabled K) for unseen-Cycle prediction and reducing reliance on a single split; it requires fold fits for each evaluated parameter set, a precise worst-condition aggregation, and a final-model policy. Alternatively keep Training MSE selection and use N-fold only to check retained parameter sets: retains the chosen optimization objective and may need fewer CV evaluations, but does not use held-out performance to guide the search and still produces different fold models. Continuing the existing single holdout remains available if CV cost or evidence constraints are unsuitable. No recommendation here guarantees improvement or eliminates overfitting.
- **User answer:** 對。
- **Explicit rationale:** Official sources checked: https://scikit-learn.org/stable/modules/cross_validation.html describes per-fold refitting, held-out evaluation, correlated time-series/group issues, fit-only preprocessing and tuning/evaluation limitations; https://scikit-learn.org/stable/modules/generated/sklearn.model_selection.GroupKFold.html describes nonoverlapping groups; https://scikit-learn.org/stable/auto_examples/model_selection/plot_nested_cross_validation_iris.html distinguishes tuning CV from independent performance estimation. Illustrative one-recording three-Cycle 3-fold fits use Cycles 1/2 vs 3, 1/3 vs 2 and 2/3 vs 1. Each fold uses identical hyperparameters but generally different coefficients; these folds are not Speed/Offset fallback rounds or workers. More folds increase fitting work, not necessarily wall time by exactly N. Complete Cycle grouping avoids splitting overlapping FIR samples across fit/evaluation, but correlated Cycles in a recording do not establish new-recording generalization.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-104/REQ-109/AC-109 adopt N-fold Validation MSE search selection, superseding the Training-MSE objective. Fold count/aggregation, old ratio gate, singleton constraints and final-model policy remain open; DISC-103 addresses aggregation first. Complete-Cycle, numeric and per-fit no-leakage safeguards remain. No product edits.

### DISC-103: Pool held-out errors within each condition before taking the worst condition
- **Situation:** CV Loss now selects parameters, while the user previously chose maximum condition MSE. Different folds can contain different numbers of eligible target samples after Cycle boundaries, Ignore/Collision and shared History support, so averaging fold MSEs equally differs from pooling their squared errors.
- **Question:** For each Speed x Offset condition, sum squared held-out XYZ residuals across all folds and divide by that condition's total held-out scalar residual count, then take the maximum of these condition MSEs as the candidate CV Loss?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Recommend pooled per-condition MSE then maximum condition: gives every eligible XYZ residual equal weight within its condition and preserves the previous point-weighted condition-MSE intent. Larger held-out folds have proportionate influence; small folds are not equally weighted solely for being folds. Alternatively average per-fold condition MSEs equally before the condition maximum: gives each fold equal influence but makes short folds disproportionately influential per sample. Taking a maximum over fold-condition pairs would be yet another, stricter objective and is not implied by maximum-condition MSE.
- **User answer:** User supplies a 3-fold illustration and explains: Validation輪流做。Kernel就會有三種; each kernel is evaluated on training and validation data, three MSEs are averaged, and the kernel with the smallest average is selected. This is a conceptual explanation requiring clarification, not approval of the proposed sample-weighted pooling.
- **Explicit rationale:** Proposed MSE(c) = sum over held-out fold/sample/XYZ squared residuals for condition c divided by the corresponding scalar residual count; Loss = max_c MSE(c). Only predictions from models which did not fit that held-out Cycle contribute. Compute with the accepted target arithmetic and float64 accumulation; do not normalize by residual STD or round intermediate fold/condition values. This is not claiming pooled out-of-fold MSE equals an equal-fold mean or an unbiased independent test estimate.
- **Resulting impact:** REQ-109 CV selection remains the latest accepted direction, but DISC-104 must distinguish candidate configurations, separately fitted fold kernels and held-out-only averaging. Do not silently accept Train-plus-Validation mixed scoring, equal-fold weighting, N=3 or selection of a single best fold; the proposed pooling in DISC-103 remains unapproved. No implementation.

### DISC-104: Clarify the supplied CV diagram and which MSEs are averaged
- **Situation:** The supplied image codex-clipboard-d9bff8a1-7361-4cdb-87d4-7e1ea66468bb.png shows three rotating folds and columns Model 1/2/3 with MSEs (0.2,0.4,0.3), (0.4,0.5,0.6), (0.4,0.5,0.3) and means 0.3/0.5/0.4. Its author does not define model identity in the image. - **Options and tradeoffs:** Recommend held-out-only CV averaging: compares predictions on data not used for the corresponding fold fit and matches the CV selection direction in REQ-109. It entails N fitted kernels per parameter configuration, not one fixed kernel scored on all data. Alternatively the user's described per-kernel average mixing Training and Validation MSEs is a custom mixed objective: mathematically calculable and describes combined fit, but includes in-sample errors and is not a held-out CV score; adopting it would supersede REQ-109 and need explicit weights and final-kernel selection rules.

The held-out-only mean is 0.3; Training errors may be separately recorded but are not extra values in that mean. Three configurations times three folds means nine fold fits (internal robust work separate), generally different coefficients per fold. The winning column selects a parameter configuration, not automatically the fold kernel with MSE 0.2. Official references: https://scikit-learn.org/stable/modules/cross_validation.html and https://scikit-learn.org/stable/modules/generated/sklearn.model_selection.GridSearchCV.html. Diagram values are user-provided examples, not measured project data.

### DISC-105: Shared pool of candidate-by-fold evaluation jobs
- **Situation:** The user requested multiple workers for CV, but interrupted DISC-105 to settle the metric. DEC-107 through DEC-113 now define selectable mixed Loss, condition aggregation, N range/default and Cycle coverage. The pending job-granularity decision resumes; the eight-worker global search ceiling is already accepted.
- **Question:** Make one candidate-parameter combination evaluated on one fold a job, and dispatch all ready candidate/fold jobs through one shared queue to at most eight search workers?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Recommend candidate-by-fold jobs: exposes parallel work even when only a few parameter candidates are ready and lets any free worker take another ready fold. It requires shared bounded data/cache/result management and a central all-required-fold barrier before a candidate is ranked; no nested worker pools. Alternatively assign a whole candidate's N folds sequentially to one worker: simpler scheduling with lower job overhead, but fewer opportunities for parallel work when few candidates remain. Both preserve target numeric semantics and forbid more than eight concurrent search workers.

Each fold fits a joint model across that fold's eligible active-condition Training data, not a separate model per condition. Every candidate uses the same centrally constructed fold plan and evaluation masks. Coordinator aggregates complete fold results, never treats a first result or partial average as complete CV evidence; individual workers do not choose folds, thresholds or optimizer moves.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-114/REQ-119/AC-119 accept candidate-by-fold job granularity, shared queue with no more than eight search workers, and all-fold completion before ranking. Preserve same centrally fixed fold plan, central aggregation/search ownership, separate Qualification ceiling and terminal hardware/worker error policies. No product edits.

### DISC-106: Training/Validation mixed selection Loss versus hard Training rejection
- **Situation:** The user explicitly wants Training MSE mixed into assessment so candidates fitting Training data poorly can be discarded. Mixing errors into a scalar ranking changes preferences, but does not guarantee a fixed maximum Training MSE. A hard rejection rule requires a separately defined Training-quality threshold.

- **Options and tradeoffs:** Equal-weight mixed ranking directly incorporates the requested Training contribution without an extra absolute cutoff; it allows tradeoffs, so a candidate with higher Training error may still win if Validation error compensates, and it is not pure held-out CV performance. The 50/50 split is a simple proposal, not a measured optimum. Alternatively use Training MSE as a separate pass/fail gate, then rank passing candidates by the accepted Validation average: enforces explicit Training exclusion but requires a meaningful threshold and per-fold/condition gate policy. No absolute threshold or alternate weighting is implied.

Training and Validation sets and statistics remain separated for fitting; only the selection metric mixes their post-fit errors. Source https://scikit-learn.org/stable/modules/cross_validation.html documents separate train/test scores; https://scikit-learn.org/stable/modules/learning_curve.html discusses interpreting Training and Validation performance. These sources do not prescribe 50/50 weighting. An illustrative mean T=0.40,V=0.10 gives mixed 0.25, versus T=0.20,V=0.40 giving mixed 0.30: the lower mixed score can still have worse Training MSE.

### DISC-108: Average folds within each condition before taking the worst condition
- **Situation:** The user previously selected maximum Speed x Offset condition MSE, later accepted equal fold averaging, and now accepts optional equal Training/Validation weighting. The order of fold averaging and condition maximum can change parameter ranking and needs confirmation.
- **Question:** For each active Speed x Offset condition, compute its per-fold Loss using the selected Training-inclusion mode, average those fold losses equally, and then take the largest condition average as the candidate's selection Loss?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Taking the maximum of condition fold averages preserves a worst-condition objective over each condition's complete CV estimate and equal fold weights; a single unusually bad fold may be moderated by the remaining folds for that condition. Taking each fold's worst condition first and averaging those maxima instead emphasizes whichever condition is worst in each fold, but can combine different conditions across folds and produce a different, more conservative objective. Recommend maximum after per-condition fold means to preserve the user's earlier maximum-condition intent. Neither option sample-weights folds or pools all conditions.

### DISC-111: Per-recording Training Cycle guarantee in every rotating fold
- **Situation:** REQ-068/REQ-095 preserve at least one Training Cycle from each participating recording, while input evidence minimum remains one valid complete Cycle. Exhaustive rotating Validation holds each assigned Cycle out once. A recording with one Cycle necessarily has zero Training Cycles in that Cycle's held-out fold, even if other recordings of the same condition provide ample fitting evidence. These policies cannot all be satisfied for a singleton recording.
- **Question:** Preserve at least one Training Cycle per recording in every fold, and stop before fitting when Start Learning preflight cannot allocate such folds?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Preserve the per-recording invariant in every fold: matches the earlier explicit Training guarantee and terminal allocation-shortage policy, but implies at least two valid Cycles per participating recording for exhaustive CV, with further condition/fold coverage constraints still required. Alternatively allow a recording to be wholly held out in a fold while other recordings provide that condition's Training evidence: can support singleton recordings in sufficiently populated conditions, but changes the earlier per-recording guarantee and evaluates across recording boundaries. Recommend preserving the guarantee unless the user explicitly relaxes it. Keeping a singleton always in Training would fail exhaustive held-out rotation and is not silently substituted.
- **User answer:** 改成ecording 至少有兩個有效 Cycle
- **Explicit rationale:** One-Cycle recording A: when its sole Cycle is Validation, A contributes no Training Cycle. Two or more Cycles is a necessary, not by itself sufficient, requirement for the proposed guarantee; Cycle-to-fold allocation must prevent all Cycles of a recording occupying the same fold and still satisfy later condition/common-evidence requirements. Accepting/annotating a recording with one valid Cycle can remain allowed; the stricter feasibility is checked before learning. No claim that every recording needs at least N Cycles, no silent reduction of N and no row-level splitting.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-112/REQ-117/AC-117 raise the per-recording minimum to two valid Cycles and retain every-fold per-recording Training coverage. This supersedes REQ-095 minimum one while preserving REQ-096 terminal allocation-shortage handling. No raw-data import/annotation editing restriction is added. Condition-level N coverage remains pending DISC-112; no product edits.

### DISC-112: Require nonempty Validation Cycles per condition in every fold
- **Situation:** Each recording now requires two valid Cycles, but final Loss averages all N folds within every active Speed x Offset condition. A condition with only two Cycles cannot provide a held-out Cycle in every fold when N=3, even though its recording passes the two-Cycle minimum.
- **Question:** Require at least N valid Cycles in total per active Speed x Offset condition, assign at least one of its Cycles to Validation in every fold, and stop Learning at preflight if the required allocation is infeasible?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Require every condition in every fold: keeps the declared equal-N-fold condition comparison complete and comparable, but requires enough annotated Cycles and may reject larger N values for small conditions. Alternatively allow conditions absent in some folds: uses sparse evidence but requires changing the approved averaging/comparability policy and specifying missing-fold treatment. Recommend complete per-condition coverage to preserve REQ-114; do not silently skip empty folds, omit conditions or auto-reduce N.
- **User answer:** 可以
- **Explicit rationale:** Proposed count constraints are n_r>=2 per recording and sum of valid Cycles over recordings in each condition >=N, plus actual assignment checks preserving every-fold per-recording Training coverage. At N=3, a condition with one two-Cycle recording fails total count; two two-Cycle recordings total four and pass this count check, but still need valid allocation and target-support checks. Cycles remain indivisible and each is held out once. This does not require every recording to have N Cycles. It does not settle balancing, randomized allocation order or effective-sample support.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-113/REQ-118/AC-118 accept condition-total-at-least-N and nonempty per-condition Validation Cycle coverage in every fold, with terminal preflight failure if counts or assignment are infeasible. The former single-holdout count formula is superseded for this workflow. Balancing, random assignment, common-target evidence and final kernel identity remain open. Resume the pending worker discussion DISC-105; no product edits.

### DISC-113: Final fitted kernel after N-fold parameter selection
- **Situation:** Each retained parameter configuration now has N different fold-trained kernels. The accepted scalar selects parameter configurations and does not by itself identify a deployable kernel. REQ-072 previously forbade a combined-data refit after a single reserved Validation check; applying a different final-fit policy requires explicit approval.
- **Question:** After CV selects the retained best parameter configurations, use each configuration to fit one final kernel on all eligible active-round Cycles, including Cycles that rotated through Validation, then calculate Score/search thresholds and run Qualification on that final kernel?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** One final full-evidence fit per retained parameter configuration uses all eligible active-round fitting evidence without choosing an arbitrary fold and produces one deployable kernel per tied configuration. It adds a fit and produces a different model whose coefficients/statistics were not directly held out in the earlier CV; fold CV Loss must remain parameter-selection evidence, not be relabeled as direct Validation of this final model. Alternatively preserve the no-additional-fit policy by carrying the fold-trained kernels into Qualification: preserves their precise fitting/evaluation provenance but leaves multiple deployable candidates per parameter configuration and increases Qualification work, with retention/gate rules to specify. Recommend full-evidence final fitting if the user accepts explicitly replacing REQ-072; no claim of guaranteed improvement or absence of overfitting.
- **User answer:** After the explanatory turns, the user selects 從fold kernels選擇最低2個 loss and asks N-fold計算validation mse的時候檢查是否超過training data mse 容許百分比?
- **Explicit rationale:** All eligible data means only existing active-round fitting scope and eligible Cycle Normal/Pick/Put evidence; Collision, Ignore and excluded fallback conditions remain excluded from fitting. Use the selected Robust mode (off by default); a final fitting operation may internally include its existing robust refit only when enabled. Preserve each distinct best rounded-Loss tied parameter configuration; no best-fold cherry-picking, coefficient averaging or renewed hyperparameter search. Compute the final model's own target-arithmetic residual calibration under the agreed row rules. Qualification tests that exact final model across supplied conditions under existing rules, without attaching fold-model qualification/validation passes to it. The old 20-percent overfit gate remains explicitly unresolved, not silently bypassed by this proposal.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-115/REQ-120/AC-120 choose existing fold kernels with nominal count two, not a new full-data fit. Gate timing/order is clarified in DISC-115; comparator/pool/cutoff ties and survivor-count details remain open. No product changes.

### DISC-114: Distinguish minimum aggregated parameter Loss from minimum fold-kernel Loss
- **Situation:** The user asks 不是選擇最小的MSE? about choosing among A/B/C fold kernels.
