Keeping all fold kernels for subsequent qualification avoids choosing by the smallest own-fold result but increases downstream work and requires retention rules. The full-data final-fit alternative in DISC-113 remains unaccepted. This question clarifies intent, not a claim that minimum own-fold Loss is statistically preferable.

- **Explicit rationale:** Illustrative own-fold losses A=0.2, B=0.4, C=0.3 have arithmetic mean 0.3 for a single condition; another parameter configuration could have mean 0.5, so the first configuration wins the accepted parameter comparison. Choosing A because 0.2 is smallest is a separate second step. Fold Loss uses the selected mode, default 50 percent Training plus 50 percent Validation, not an unannounced switch to pure Validation MSE. Multiple-condition fold-kernel aggregation, tie handling, exact evaluation evidence and the pending Validation admission gate require explicit reconciliation if this direction is selected.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-115/REQ-120/AC-120 choose existing fold kernels with nominal count two, not a new full-data fit. Gate timing/order is clarified in DISC-115; comparator/pool/cutoff ties and survivor-count details remain open. No product changes.

### DISC-115: Check each fold's Validation allowance before choosing two fold kernels
- **Situation:** The user now wants the two lowest-Loss fold kernels and asks whether Validation MSE is checked against the Training MSE allowed-increase percentage when N-fold computes it. Existing REQ-061 through REQ-064 define same-condition V<=T*(1+p/100), default p=20 percent, with every required condition passing. Placement relative to the new two-kernel selection was not previously defined.
- **Question:** Compute and flag the per-condition Validation allowance after each fold produces Training/Validation MSE, then exclude failing kernels and choose the two lowest-Loss fold kernels among the passing ones from the retained best parameter configurations?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Gate first, then choose two passing kernels: prevents failed folds occupying the two downstream slots, but can select a passing fold whose Loss ranks below two rejected folds. Select two first, then gate: preserves the two raw-loss leaders irrespective of gate outcome, but one or both may be rejected and no replacements are implied. Recommend gate before fold-kernel selection to implement the user's stated quality check without admitting failed kernels; this does not authorize higher-loss parameter configurations when best parameter groups fail.
- **User answer:** 可以
- **Explicit rationale:** For each fold kernel and each same Speed x Offset condition, compare raw finite nonnegative V_fc with T_fc*(1+p/100); p defaults 20, equality passes, T=0 only permits V=0, and no epsilon/rounding is added. At T=0.10, V=0.11 passes and V=0.13 fails the 0.12 limit. All required conditions must pass, not a comparison of separately maximized Training/Validation errors. Preserve all N valid folds and their original numerical errors in parameter CV Loss; an allowance failure is not missing/invalid MSE and must not be dropped to improve a parameter average. Fold kernels still have differing Validation Cycle identities; admission does not prove absence of overfitting. The exact fold-kernel Loss comparator and tied-parameter pool scope remain to confirm, as do cutoff ties and fewer-than-two survivors.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-116/REQ-121/AC-121 confirm per-fold same-condition Validation allowance checks, removal of failures before nominal-two fold-kernel selection, and unchanged complete-fold parameter Loss. Preserve model identity and existing allowance semantics. Per-parameter versus global pool, scalar, cutoff ties and fewer-than-two survivors remain pending; no product changes.

These examples assume no cutoff ties and sufficient survivors only to isolate pool scope; exact fold-kernel scalar, cutoff ties and shortage policy remain separate decisions.

### DISC-117: Worst-condition own-fold Loss for individual fold-kernel ranking

For three Validation-passing kernels A,B,C with two-condition losses A=(0.20,0.50), B=(0.40,0.30), C=(0.30,0.35), their scores are 0.50,0.40,0.35, selecting C and B within that configuration. ### DISC-118: Alternatives to maximum condition Loss for fold-kernel selection
- **Situation:** The user requests other methods instead of the pending worst-condition scalar for selecting two passing fold kernels per retained parameter configuration. This comparison is scoped to individual fold-kernel ranking, not the accepted max-of-condition-fold-means parameter objective.
- **Question:** Use the equal arithmetic mean of a kernel's own condition Losses to rank the two fold kernels, if the user's preference is average performance with equal importance per condition?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Maximum condition Loss emphasizes the worst evaluated condition but lets one difficult condition determine the entire ranking. Equal-condition arithmetic mean gives every Speed x Offset condition equal importance regardless of data volume, but strong conditions can compensate for a weak one. Sample-pooled MSE reflects per-sample error across the available evaluation data, but conditions with more eligible observations dominate and changes in data collection proportions can change ranking. Equal-condition mean is a conditional recommendation only if the user wants overall condition-average performance; maximum remains consistent with worst-condition priority. None is an evidence-backed universally better or Qualification-improving method.

- **Explicit rationale:** For equal-condition mean, K_f=mean_c(L_fc), where L_fc follows the default-on 50/50 Training/Validation mixture or Validation-only toggle. For sample pooling, compute pooled Training MSE using that kernel's Training subset SSE/counts across conditions, separately compute pooled Validation MSE using its Validation SSE/counts, then apply 0.5*T_pool+0.5*V_pool when enabled or V_pool alone when disabled. Do not merge Training and Validation rows in a way that changes the accepted 50/50 weights. Preserve the established XYZ MSE convention and masks. At illustrative condition losses A=(0.10,0.70), B=(0.45,0.45), C=(0.38,0.48), maxima are 0.70/0.45/0.48 and equal means are 0.40/0.45/0.43: maximum selects B,C; mean selects A,C. Arithmetic checked directly, not fitted project performance. Official MSE documentation supports sample weighting: https://scikit-learn.org/stable/modules/generated/sklearn.metrics.mean_squared_error.html ; it does not prescribe these project aggregation priorities. Reuse available fold error evidence and counts; no additional fit or coefficient change is proposed. Different kernels still have different held-out Cycle identities, and changing the aggregate does not remove that selection limitation.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-118/REQ-123/AC-123 adopt maximum own-fold condition Loss as the kernel ranking scalar, then choose the lowest values. Clarify the name as worst-condition Loss. Equal-condition/sample-pooled alternatives are not selected. Preserve parameter-level aggregation and all admission/pool/model-identity rules. Precision/cutoff ties and survivor shortages remain pending; no implementation.

### DISC-120: Proceed with one passing fold kernel; handle zero without filling slots
- **Situation:** The nominal two-plus-cutoff-ties selection occurs after Validation admission. A best parameter configuration can have only one passing fold kernel or none, despite complete valid Cycle counts and complete finite CV measurements. This is not the earlier Cycle-allocation shortage that stops Learning.
- **Question:** If only one fold kernel passes, proceed with that one; if none pass, skip Qualification for that parameter configuration, and use the existing Validation-failed round fallback only when every retained best parameter configuration has no passing kernels?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Use the actual passing count: retains an eligible kernel without relaxing the allowance or inventing data and avoids rejecting an otherwise usable single survivor; zero-survivor configurations contribute no model while other tied-best configurations can continue. Requiring at least two passing kernels would reject a configuration even when one kernel passes and may trigger fallback sooner. Recommend actual passing count because two is a selection target, not a separately approved minimum evidence requirement, and because the existing policy excludes failed kernels instead of forcing them into slots.

- **Explicit rationale:** Proposed outcomes per retained parameter configuration: at least two pass -> apply REQ-124 two-plus-second-place-ties; one passes -> that unchanged kernel proceeds to Score/threshold/Qualification; zero pass -> no kernel from that configuration proceeds. If all retained best configurations have zero passes, report Validation failed and follow the already selected Speed/Offset fallback, preserving eligible prior qualified results. Do not fill from higher-loss parameter configurations, redo folds, change N, retrain a combined-data kernel or loosen p. Candidate-specific numerical invalidity, Cycle-count/allocation shortages, worker failure and memory errors keep their distinct existing policies.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-120/REQ-125/AC-125 accept actual-survivor-count handling, preserving a sole passing kernel and all-best-groups-empty Validation fallback. No failed-kernel or worse-parameter backfill, threshold relaxation, resampling or extra fit. The final kernel selection/admission chain is settled; remaining CV assignment/provenance/support details are separate. No implementation.

### DISC-121: Balance the number of complete Cycles per condition across folds
- **Situation:** N, complete-Cycle grouping, per-condition coverage and per-recording Training retention are fixed, but the criterion for distributing Cycles among folds is not. Equal Cycle counts and similar sample counts can disagree when Cycle lengths or eligible-data proportions differ.

Balance eligible sample counts instead: aims for comparable data volume but can produce substantially different Cycle counts and needs a length/mask-aware constrained allocation rule. Recommend Cycle-count balance for consistency with the user's chosen whole-Cycle design; not a claim that one balance criterion always produces better models.

- **Explicit rationale:** Proposed per-condition fold sizes are floor(n/N) or ceil(n/N), so the largest and smallest Cycle counts differ by at most one when the required assignment is feasible. At N=3, n=7 gives counts 3,2,2 (fold order not prescribed); n=4 gives 2,1,1. Preserve at least two valid Cycles per recording, at least one Training Cycle per recording per fold, at least one Validation Cycle per condition per fold, and exactly one held-out assignment per Cycle. Each candidate uses the same plan within a round. Randomization mechanics/seed persistence and effective 128 ms evaluation support are separate; no silent relaxing of allocation constraints or raw-row splitting.

### DISC-122: Lifecycle of N and Include Training MSE Learning Settings
- **Situation:** N=2 through 10 (default 3) and Include Training MSE (default on) are accepted controls. The other Learning Settings already capture/lock one run's values, save/restore project settings, and preserve original qualified-result provenance when idle next-run settings change. Applying that lifecycle to these two new controls is not yet explicit.

Allowing editable next-run pending settings during a run could offer flexibility but requires separate visible active/pending values and additional state; it is not already selected. For idle edits, preserving otherwise-current qualified results matches prior settings behavior; invalidating them on setting-only edits would require a deliberate change to the existing agreed provenance policy. Recommend the established lifecycle for consistency.
- **User answer:** 可以
- **Explicit rationale:** Capture the values once, disable edits through coarse/fine, gate evaluation, Qualification, fallback and cancellation cleanup, then unlock. Save/Save As preserves configured next-run values and each result's original captured values; Open restores both without relabeling earlier results. An idle edit alone neither reruns checks nor revives evidence-stale/diagnostic/failed results, and preserves existing active-run export and new-run replacement guards. Missing/invalid persisted fields remain a separate unresolved policy; defaults and bounds are already selected and are not reopened.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-123/REQ-128/AC-128 adopt the proposed run capture/lock, explicit-save restoration and next-run-only idle edit lifecycle. Preserve original-result evidence and eligibility guards. Defaults/bounds remain selected; missing/invalid serialized values are clarified in DISC-123. No implementation.

### DISC-123: Missing or invalid serialized N and Training-inclusion settings
- **Situation:** New-setting defaults and normal save/restore behavior are fixed, while supported saved projects can lack these fields or contain invalid values. - **Explicit rationale:** N must be an integer in 2 through 10; missing or invalid values (such as 12 or a fractional value) would reset to 3, not clamp to an endpoint. Training inclusion must be a valid boolean; absent or invalid values would reset to on. Preserve valid nondefaults such as N=5 and disabled inclusion. Corrections apply only to next-run Learning Settings, not stored kernels' original N, metric mode, fold maps or validation provenance. Missing historical evidence stays unavailable, not fabricated from defaults. Do not overwrite the project file automatically; persist corrections only when the user saves. Unsupported schemas and unrelated corrupt source/model data keep existing handling.

The lifecycle/default policy is closed; the closure audit identifies fine-search out-of-bound neighbor handling in DISC-124 as a remaining outcome-changing policy. No implementation.

### DISC-124: Project fine-search neighbor proposals to the nearest allowed bound
- **Situation:** History, alpha and enabled K bounds, initial/final steps and duplicate-candidate reuse are settled, but REQ-033 explicitly leaves boundary handling open. A fine step can propose a coordinate outside its allowed domain, and skipping it versus evaluating the nearest bound can change which parameter combinations are visited.
- **Question:** Clip each out-of-bound fine-neighbor coordinate to its nearest allowed endpoint, then deduplicate and evaluate the resulting valid candidate under the existing same-round cache rules?
- **Options and tradeoffs:** Project to the endpoint: retains a chance to evaluate a boundary combined with the neighbor's other coordinates without expanding the domain, but can add a boundary evaluation and produces duplicates that must be removed. Discard the out-of-domain neighbor: avoids that evaluation but may miss that boundary combination in the current neighborhood even if coarse search evaluated other endpoint combinations. Recommend endpoint projection to honor inclusive bounds and preserve edge exploration; this does not promise a global optimum.
- **User answer:** The user annotates 採用「超出範圍就改測最近邊界值」 with 可以; accepted.
- **Explicit rationale:** At an illustrative 1 kHz sampling rate, a requested History move from 120 ms by +32 ms proposes 152 ms and would evaluate the realizable 128 ms endpoint instead; runtime representation uses integer tap counts and the sample-rate-realized maximum. Alpha 99 multiplied by 1.05 proposes 103.95 and would evaluate 100. When Robust refit is enabled, K=31 plus a step of 4 proposes 35 and would evaluate 32. Lower bound overshoots analogously project to History zero/current-sample tap, alpha 1e-6 or enabled K=2. Apply projection per coordinate in the joint neighborhood before exact effective-parameter deduplication; proposals equal to the center or an already evaluated same-context candidate do not trigger a duplicate fit. Other still-valid joint coordinate changes remain, so reaching one boundary does not mean the entire search has converged. Preserve the existing full-neighborhood/step reduction/convergence process and all bounds.
- **Resulting impact:** DEC-125/REQ-130/AC-130 settle the boundary portion of REQ-033/REQ-049/REQ-050/REQ-075/REQ-077 while retaining REQ-091 reuse and REQ-119 complete-fold results. This is specification confirmation only, not product implementation authorization.

### Decision closure audit: accepted rules versus implementation obligations, 2026-08-29
- The N-fold shared-evidence application follows REQ-080/REQ-094/REQ-109/REQ-119: construct separate Training and Validation common target masks per fold using the realized 128 ms support, hold them fixed across parameter candidates, and keep original candidate-specific fitting evidence. Any required condition/subset with no common evaluation targets retains the established round-skip/fallback classification, not same-round resampling or lowered History. This restates the accepted rules for the fold layout, not a new threshold.
- REQ-119 requires all N valid fold results before parameter-level ranking. A narrowly classified expected numerical invalidity in any required fold therefore prevents that configuration from obtaining a valid complete CV Loss; REQ-105 skips that invalid configuration while other configurations continue. A finite MSE merely failing the Validation allowance is different and remains in the CV aggregate under REQ-121. Invalid source data, unexpected program/worker failures and memory shortage keep their whole-run stop policies. No partial-fold averages are authorized.
- Seed/provenance, balanced Cycle allocation, settings lifecycle, metadata truthfulness, kernel-selection count/ties, no extra final fit and all-best-rejected fallback are now user-decided. Concrete deterministic sampler implementation, source/type/state ownership, fold-aware cache identity, bounded memory/IPC, worker-library thread budget, serializer field layout and regression/runtime verification are implementation/design obligations under those accepted constraints; they are not new user-facing options by themselves.
- Search-boundary behavior is closed by DEC-125/REQ-130/AC-130. The Current resolution index explicitly disambiguates phase removal, fold versus parameter Loss, optional robust fitting, starts/worker counts, final fold kernels, evidence/failure classes and settings lifecycle. Earlier pending clauses are retained only as chronological history. No additional outcome-changing decision was identified in this audit. Implementation obligations are moved to Routing/Gates rather than mislabeled as user decisions. Do not equate this audit with source implementation, architecture-gate PASS or numerical/runtime validation.

### Factual clarification: Candidate configurations versus fold-trained kernels
The user asks why the image has Model 1/2/3 and requests examples for all columns. The image does not identify the actual algorithms or parameter values; under its usual CV interpretation these columns compare three candidate configurations, while rows represent three folds. Three-fold validation does not force three candidate configurations: one configuration can be evaluated with three fold fits, or ten configurations with thirty fold fits.

For an illustrative mapping to the current single-family FIR search, hold History at 16 ms and Robust off, and label alpha 0.0001 as Model 1, alpha 0.01 as Model 2, and alpha 1 as Model 3. These alpha assignments are invented teaching examples, not metadata from the image or project measurements. With input groups A/B/C, all configurations use AB fit/C Validation, AC fit/B Validation, and BC fit/A Validation. Each configuration is refitted in every fold. The image supplies Model 1 Validation values 0.2/0.4/0.3 (mean 0.3), Model 2 values 0.4/0.5/0.6 (mean 0.5), and Model 3 values 0.4/0.5/0.3 (mean 0.4). The means select a configuration under the illustrated equal-fold rule, not automatically one of its fitted fold kernels. This mapping implies nine fold fits for three configurations times three folds, with optional internal robust fits a separate count. It does not assert the shown MSEs would result from the illustrative alpha values. Source context remains the official CV/GridSearchCV documentation cited in DISC-104.

No decision is inferred about actual N, search-grid size, fold-weighting, cross-condition aggregation, final exported kernel or the 20-percent gate. The current turn answers understanding only and does not require another design-choice question.

### Factual clarification: History depth and common evaluation are not fixed delay
History is the input lookback duration. Source build_candidate_plan around service.py line 1703 uses tap_count=floor(History_ms*sample_rate_hz/1000)+1, counting the current sample. At 1000 Hz, 4 ms corresponds to five taps and 128 ms to 129 taps per input path. This is not a neural-network layer depth or a count of training iterations.
Source _fir_rows around lines 1622-1649 requires complete finite same-segment History for training/evaluation rows. A short History can use earlier target timestamps than a long History; common evaluation holds those target timestamps constant to compare errors on the same observations. No prediction/measurement time shift or fixed detection-delay compensation is introduced.
At target time 200 ms, a 4 ms History uses current/past inputs from 196 through 200 ms and a 128 ms History uses 72 through 200 ms, both predicting External acceleration at 200 ms. Source _target_replay_arrays around lines 1432-1465 uses input[index-lag] and writes the prediction at index; it does not wait for future samples and zero-pads unavailable startup history. Runtime/replay startup Score inclusion stays unchanged. History can affect model response and computation, but 128 ms of lookback does not by itself imply a fixed extra 128 ms detection delay; actual detector latency remains a Qualification result.
These are source-grounded explanations and illustrative times, not measured performance evidence or another policy decision.

### Factual clarification: no additional fit after Validation, 2026-08-28
Illustrative one-recording three-Cycle partition: Training Cycles 1 and 3 fit kernel A using their eligible evidence, including the accepted existing robust refit; Cycle 2 is reserved Validation. Validation runs A on reserved inputs and compares predicted External IMU acceleration with measurements to calculate MSE, without changing A's fitted coefficients. Passing that check would send the same A into the existing all-supplied-recording Score/threshold/Qualification stage under the pending proposal.
The phrase no retraining after Validation excludes an additional fit using Cycles 1, 2 and 3 combined before Qualification. That extra fit could yield kernel B with different coefficients, and A's earlier reserved Validation pass cannot simply be attached to B. It does not mean that A was never trained, remove the Training-internal robust refit, forbid approved optimizer fitting, or disable fitting new kernels after later Qualification/fallback failures. Original Collision/Ignore fitting exclusions remain; complete-recording Qualification still checks the supplied event annotations.
This is explanatory design context, not source behavior newly implemented or a new policy selection. DISC-068 remains pending; no product edits.

### Factual clarification: fixed-seed Cycle selection, 2026-08-28
A seed is the starting value for a pseudorandom sequence. With unchanged data identities/order, seed and sampling algorithm, the proposed constrained selector produces the same Cycle assignment again. It provides a reproducible selection rather than drawing a new split independently for every candidate or rerun. It does not mean always selecting the first or last Cycle and does not guarantee representative or statistically independent evidence.
Illustrative only: one Speed x Offset has Recording A with Cycles A1/A2/A3 and Recording B with B1/B2/B3. Six total Cycles require two Validation Cycles. One valid result is Validation A2/B1 and Training A1/A3/B2/B3. Both recordings retain Training Cycles. Under the same canonical inputs, seed and algorithm, rerunning reproduces that assignment. These Cycle identities are a hypothetical demonstration, not a real generated draw, selected seed or executed project split.
The proposed partition is created once before candidate search and shared by candidate kernels, so comparisons do not depend on candidates receiving different splits. Reproducibility is not proof of absence of overfitting, and changing data or selector settings can change the selection. DISC-065 remains pending; no product edits.

### Factual clarification: meaning of existing fallback, 2026-08-28
Read-only inspection of LearningService.initial_fallback_state, advance_fallback and _next_fallback_state in src/dual_imu_collision_studio/domain/learning/service.py: sort supplied speeds and offsets ascending. On unsuccessful range matching with multiple Training speeds, remove the lowest remaining speed and retain offsets. When that speed search is exhausted (or the current range matches), remove the smallest active offset, restore all supplied speeds and start the next outer round. Stop when no offsets remain. This is Training-scope progression, not deletion of source recordings or automatic identification of a bad recording.
For supplied speeds 500/1000 and offsets 4/5/7/8, an all-unsuccessful path is: 500+1000 with 4/5/7/8; 1000 with 4/5/7/8; 500+1000 with 5/7/8; 1000 with 5/7/8; 500+1000 with 7/8; 1000 with 7/8; 500+1000 with 8; 1000 with 8. This is an illustrative path, not an assertion about current unsaved project state or measured outcomes.
Under the proposed optimizer redesign, each changed scope would search alpha/History/K anew, then apply Training-Loss selection, Validation and, only for passing kernels, collision Qualification. Existing Qualification iterates all supplied recordings even when excluded from fitting. Earlier qualified kernels retain their existing scope/provenance. Current source does not implement the newly discussed Validation gate; using all-Validation-failed as a trigger for this fallback remains pending DISC-062. Do not label an unexecuted Qualification as a collision failure. No product edits or new decisions.

### Factual clarification: residual, MSE and robust-fitting variants
The user asks why scale/score variants are discussed when coarse and fine search compare prediction Loss. Both searches retain the same accepted held-out raw acceleration MSE objective. Predicted External IMU acceleration minus measured External IMU acceleration is the signed residual; MSE is the mean squared residual over eligible XYZ observations. Current source writes actual minus predicted, which yields the same squared errors. Condition MSEs pool held-out observations across folds and are ranked by maximum active Training condition MSE, then pooled MSE. These are not normalized detector scores or phase losses.
STD/MAD and MAX_ABS/L2_NORM are existing internal robust-fitting choices: residual centers/scales and normalized XYZ combination determine rejection scores used with K during training, then the one robust refit produces FIR coefficients. Those choices can change predictions without changing the held-out MSE formula or removing held-out evaluation points. Comparing all four variants for each new optimizer triple remains unapproved. Training-internal residual scoring is distinct from later frozen-model detector Score/threshold Qualification. This explanation does not select a new objective, remove the current robust refit, or authorize product changes.

### Factual clarification: why History affects Loss comparisons, 2026-08-28
History determines how many lagged Internal IMU inputs the fitted FIR uses to predict External XYZ at a target timestamp. It therefore affects both predictions and which timestamps have complete valid support under the current evaluation construction. The MSE formula itself only needs predicted and actual values; if predictions already exist, no additional History operation is needed to calculate MSE. Compare candidates by first fitting/predicting with each candidate's History and then computing errors on the same held-out target timestamps. Common evaluation points do not imply identical model inputs or fitted coefficients. This is distinct from detector Score/threshold qualification and does not change the existing Replay zero-padding policy.

### Factual clarification: validation data points, 2026-08-28
A held-out validation point is an eligible sample timestamp whose actual External IMU target was not used to fit that fold model. Its Internal IMU inputs plus required FIR history produce a predicted External XYZ value, compared with measured External XYZ to compute residual error. In an illustrative single-recording three-Cycle case, two Cycles fit one fold and the third supplies validation samples; roles rotate across folds. This uses existing recordings, not newly requested data, and one point may train a different fold. After parameter selection the final model fits all eligible current-scope training evidence. Cross-validation prediction Loss is distinct from detector Score/threshold/false-event Qualification. This clarification does not approve the proposed common-row policy or redefine grouped folds.

### Factual clarification: minimum candidate step, 2026-08-28
Minimum step means the finest candidate spacing used by refinement, not a gradient-training learning rate. One FIR tap changes effective History by one sample period (1 ms at 1000 Hz); adjacent integer K values differ by one. Alpha uses a logarithmic coordinate, so a finest spacing corresponds to a multiplicative ratio; multiplying/dividing by 1.1 is only an explanatory example, not a selected setting. Reaching the final resolution does not by itself mean stop: improving candidates can still be followed at that resolution until the subsequently approved complete-sweep convergence rule holds. The actual alpha resolution, refinement schedule, Loss tolerance and combined termination policy remain unapproved. No product changes or performance claims.

### Factual clarification: bounds versus completion, 2026-08-28
History and integer K have finite admissible values at a fixed sample rate. Alpha has a bounded interval but no approved candidate discretization or final log-space resolution. Repeatedly refining that interval has no defined completion point solely from the endpoints. A fixed finite candidate set can run to exhaustion without a clock limit; adaptive search can likewise stop at an approved finite schedule or resolution. Clock limits are separate waiting-time preferences and are not required. No alpha resolution, finite-step count or local plateau rule is selected by this explanation.

### Factual clarification: integer search and bounds, 2026-08-28
The user accepts integer K and asks why it needs a range rather than an integer gradient. Bounds specify allowed values; candidate strategy specifies how to choose the next value. Comparing J(K-1), J(K), J(K+1) at fixed alpha/History and fixed other rules is an integer-neighborhood or finite-difference local search, not an ordinary derivative on a smooth continuous domain. The current hard-rejection/refit rule gives identical models for adjacent K values when retained rows do not change and can jump when they do. Thus zero adjacent differences do not establish that more distant K values cannot improve Loss. An integer-neighborhood stage is compatible with the already accepted broad-to-fine direction, but exact neighborhood schedule, plateau handling and joint-parameter refinement are not yet selected. Do not infer a switch to pure local descent or removal of the approved 2-32 bounds. Static source supports this explanation; no optimizer benchmark or code modification was performed. SciPy documentation separately specifies bounds and integrality for derivative-free search: https://docs.scipy.org/doc/scipy/reference/generated/scipy.optimize.differential_evolution.html.

### Eligible-row evidence checked 2026-08-28
Read-only count check against unchanged saved project and unchanged four Replay CSV hashes: each Cycle is an eligible mask minus annotated Ignore/Collision, separated into contiguous source runs. Existing _fir_rows produces sum(max(0, run_length-taps+1)) valid rows for finite uniformly sampled data. At 33 taps (32 ms at 1000 Hz), minimum valid rows per recording are 7mm_50:7633, 7mm_100:5453, 8mm_50:7633, 8mm_100:5787. Across all 12 Cycles, the minimum is 5453 (7mm_100 Cycle 3), yielding 545 or 546 samples per group for 10 groups. None of these Cycles has the proposed insufficient-sample edge case for taps 5/9/17/33. The separate hypothetical 40-sample contiguous run at 33 taps yields 8 valid rows and cannot fill 10 nonempty groups. This is an eligibility-count check, not retraining or evidence of better model performance. No new missing-data policy is accepted.
